# PHPArm Expansion Plan

Transition from auto-repair shop management to a multi-division field service / service-order platform per `expansion.md`.

## Viability Assessment

**Overall: viable, estimated 12-18 months.** The existing foundation is strong — 372 PHP services, 98 migrations, React 19 frontend with 182 views, established auth/roles/branches/audit, inventory with transfers + transactions, work orders with status history, estimates with e-sign, inspections with templates, invoicing with reconciliation, payroll, dispatch, messaging, and CMS.

The expansion is **not a rewrite** — it is a **pivot** from single-domain (auto repair) to multi-division field service.

### What already exists and maps well
Work orders, estimates, invoices, payments, inventory (multi-location via transfers), inspections (templates + media), audit log, RBAC, branches, dispatch, messaging, CMS, notifications, reminders, reconciliation, payroll.

### What's partially there and needs extension
- **Customer model** — flat `customers` table; needs company/sites/contacts hierarchy. `branch_ids` migration is a starting point.
- **Inspections** — already template-driven; needs service-line scoping, deficiency→WO generation, QR launch.
- **Reminders** — extend into proper PM plans with entitlements.
- **Customer portal** — `customer-portal/` views exist but need request wizard, approval center, asset view, pay flows hardening.
- **Role system** — add `accounting`, `warehouse`, `customer_portal_user`; rework permission matrix per division.

### What's entirely new
1. **Divisions / Service Lines** as first-class tenancy dimension
2. **Sites & Site Assets** register with parent/child asset trees (panel→controllers→readers)
3. **Support Desk / Tickets** distinct from work orders, with SLA clocks, escalation, auto-routing
4. **Contracts & Service Agreements** with e-sign, entitlements, SLA tiers, auto-renew, margin analysis
5. **Preventative Maintenance engine** (freq-based + meter-based + condition-based)
6. **Fleet Maintenance** (own + customer fleets; telematics hooks)
7. **FIT Inspections compliance layer**
8. **Capital Planner** (lifecycle scoring, multi-year plans)
9. **Subcontractor management**
10. **Quotes→Change Orders** for project/install work

### Risk factors
- **Permissions explosion** — string-based `permission.name` check will strain under divisions × per-site restrictions. Need policy objects.
- **Routes file** — `routes/api.php` is already 10,202 lines. Must split before anything else.
- **Data model churn** — `customers` table touched by dozens of services; company/site hierarchy migration is risky.
- **Customer portal isolation** — expansion.md explicitly requires the new portal be separate from existing customer signup/request flows. Strict boundary enforcement required.
- **Schema scale** — will add ~60-80 tables on top of existing ~120+. Indexing/query strategy critical.

---

## Phased Plan

### Phase 0 — Foundation (pre-req for everything) ← COMPLETE
- [x] Split `routes/api.php` into per-module route files loaded by registrar
- [x] Introduce `divisions` table + `division_id` FK plumbing (nullable → backfill → NOT NULL)
- [x] Refactor permission check into policy objects (string-compatible)
- [x] Add `accounting`, `warehouse`, `portal_user` roles; rework permission matrix
- [x] Custom fields engine (schema + API + UI) scoped by `(division_id, entity_type)`
- [x] Branch/region dashboards bootstrap

Phase 0 deliverables landed:
- Route modules in `routes/modules/` (customer_retention, modules_and_user_groups, divisions, custom_fields, branch_dashboards) loaded via `RouteContext` DI
- Migration `099_divisions.sql` with backfill; Migration `100_custom_fields.sql` for the field engine
- `AccessGate` now consults a `PolicyRegistry` before falling back to string checks
- New roles in `config/auth.php`: accounting, warehouse, portal_user (kept alongside legacy `customer`)
- New APIs: `/api/divisions`, `/api/custom-fields/{definitions,values}`, `/api/branches/dashboards/overview`, `/api/branches/{id}/dashboard`
- New permissions: `settings.divisions.manage`, `custom_fields.view`, `custom_fields.manage`, `branches.dashboard.view`

### Phase 1 — CRM expansion  ✅ COMPLETE
- [x] `companies` table + `sites` + `site_contacts` + `billing_contacts`  (migration 101)
- [x] Migrate existing `customers` to legacy shim or contact under a company  (`CustomerLinkageService` + `POST /api/customers/{id}/promote-to-company`)
- [x] Site-level fields: access instructions, hours, alarm/gate codes, blackout windows  (migration 102 + `FieldCipher` + `site_blackout_windows`)
- [x] Contact permission controls (site/billing scope)  (`ContactPermissionScope` + `ContactScopeResolver` + `PortalScopePolicy`)
- [x] Generalized audit trail surface on every record  (CRM mutations audit-logged; `/api/audit` accepts entity_id/event/limit/offset; `AuditEntry::toArray()` + `created_at` exposed)

**Phase 1 deliverables:**
- Schema: `companies`, `sites` (FK → companies ON DELETE CASCADE), `site_contacts`, `billing_contacts`, `site_blackout_windows`; `customers.company_id` nullable FK; `sites.legacy_customer_id` for reverse lookup
- Sensitive field encryption: `SITE_CODES_ENCRYPTION_KEY` env → libsodium `crypto_secretbox` for alarm/gate codes; per-access decryption + redacted list responses
- Policy layer: `portal.*` entitlements resolved per-contact via `ContactScopeResolver` (site + billing contacts aggregated with site/amount constraints); legacy `customer` role untouched
- New APIs: `/api/companies`, `/api/companies/{id}/sites`, `/api/sites/{id}`, `/api/sites/{id}/codes`, `/api/site-contacts`, `/api/billing-contacts`, `/api/site-blackout-windows`, `/api/customers/{id}/company-linkage`, `/api/customers/{id}/promote-to-company`
- New permissions: `crm.companies.{view,manage}`, `crm.sites.{view,manage}`, `crm.sites.codes.{view,manage}`, `crm.contacts.{view,manage}`
- Audit: every CRM mutation logs event + entity_type + entity_id + actor; generic reader now filters by entity_id so UIs can render per-record timelines

**Phase 2 deliverables:** 7 new tables/column batches (migrations 103-107), 18 asset routes (1 public), AssetController + AssetQrService + AssetLifecycleService + 4 repositories. All mutations audit-logged.

### Phase 3 — Support Desk / Tickets
- [x] `tickets` table (separate from `workorders`) with category/subcategory/priority/status workflow _(3.1 — migration 108 tickets + ticket_categories + ticket_events; TicketController with 10 routes, 8 statuses, 4 priorities, 7 sources, auto-minted T-YYYYMMDD-NNNNNN numbering, timeline events per mutation)_
- [x] SLA clocks (response/arrival/resolution), pausable _(3.2 — migration 109 ticket_sla_policies + ticket_sla_clocks; SlaClockService with pause/resume/stop/breach-detection using `accumulated_seconds + (NOW() - last_started_at)` elapsed model; TicketController wired: attach on create, pauseAll on → pending_customer, resumeAll on exit, stopKind('response') on first_response_at or first customer-visible comment, stopKind('resolution') on → resolved, stopAll on → closed/cancelled; 5 new routes — GET /tickets/{id}/sla + 4 CRUD on /ticket-sla-policies; listDueForBreach SQL drives the minute-by-minute cron)_
- [x] Auto-routing engine (skill + location + contract tier + category) _(3.3 — migration 110 ticket_routing_rules; TicketRoutingService applies first matching rule at create time, skipping fields the caller set explicitly; 8 matchers (division/company/site/category/subcategory/priority/source/asset_type) + 3 actions (queue/assignee/priority); auto_routed timeline event records which rule fired; 4 CRUD routes `/api/ticket-routing-rules`; behavior test proves first-match, asset-type lookup, evaluation-order)_
- [x] Escalation rules + queues per department/division _(3.4 — migrations 111 ticket_queues + FK on tickets.queue_id, 112 ticket_escalation_rules + ticket_escalation_events; TicketEscalationService evaluates open tickets against active rules, supports `stale` / `sla_breach_imminent` / `sla_breached` triggers, cooldown-gated, fires reassign + raise_priority actions plus timeline event; 8 new routes (4 queues + 4 escalation rules); two new cron jobs — `ticket-sla-breach` (every min) + `ticket-escalation` (every 5 min); behavior test covers 8 trigger/cooldown cases)_
- [x] Ticket↔WO link + parent/child tickets _(3.5 — migration 113 ticket_workorder_links M:N join with link_kind enum `spawned|references`; parent/child strengthened — updateTicket rejects self-parenting, cycles, and two-level nesting; getTicket now returns workorder_links alongside children + timeline; 3 new routes — GET/POST/DELETE `/api/tickets/{id}/workorders`)_
- [x] Close reasons (nuisance, duplicate) + resolution/failure codes _(3.6 — migration 114 ticket_close_reasons + ticket_resolution_codes + ticket_failure_codes, seeded with 5 close reasons (nuisance, duplicate, resolved, unresolved, out_of_scope) via INSERT IGNORE; TicketCloseReasonRepository standalone (has requires_detail column), TicketResolutionCode/FailureCodeRepository share abstract TicketCodeCatalogRepository; TicketController enforces catalog at transition — resolved requires a known resolution_code, closed requires a known close_reason, close_reason.requires_detail=1 additionally requires a close_note (persisted as 'close_note' timeline event, not stored as a column); steady-state edits on already-terminal tickets skip enforcement; 12 new routes (4 each for close-reasons / resolution-codes / failure-codes); 15-case behavior test covers transition-vs-steady-state, unknown-code rejections, requires_detail gating, failure_code optional membership)_

### Phase 4 — Contracts & Service Agreements

- [x] Auto-renew scheduler + utilization reporting _(4.3 — ContractRenewalService.autoRenewDue runs through listExpiringThrough(today+90d), filters via isAutoRenewEligible (status=active AND auto_renew=1 AND renewal_term_months>0 AND end_date<=today+renewal_notice_days), creates successor contract with renewed_from_contract_id+start_date=old.end_date+1day+end_date=+term_months-1day, mirrors contract_sites + contract_entitlements (reset_on_renewal=1 zeros quantity_used, reset_on_renewal=0 carries it over via consume), transitions old→renewed, appends 'renew' amendment with delta_json.new_contract_id, emits contract.renewed audit; expireDue transitions past-end non-renewing contracts to expired; renewManually bypasses auto_renew for admin-triggered renewal (rejects cancelled/renewed); ContractUtilizationService computes per-entitlement status ladder (ok/warning>=80%/exceeded>100%) + overall rollup + companyRollup aggregates across active contracts; 4 new routes GET `/api/contracts/{id}/utilization`, GET `/api/companies/{id}/contracts-utilization`, GET `/api/contracts/renewals/due`, POST `/api/contracts/{id}/renew`; new daily cron `contracts-renewal` at 01:00 wired into bin/cron/run.php; 16-case behavior test covers eligibility, successor linkage, site/entitlement mirroring, reset_on_renewal carry-over, expireDue guard against renewed contracts, manual renewal, utilization ladder + permission gate)_
- [x] Contract-driven billing lines (entitlement consumption) _(4.4 — migration 117 contract_consumption_ledger append-only table (contract_id + entitlement_id NULL + source_type in [ticket/workorder/invoice/manual] + source_id + entitlement_kind + amount_requested/covered/overage DECIMAL(12,2) + unit_rate_cents snapshot + actor + occurred_at); ContractBillingService.resolveCoverage is a pure preview function returning {contract, entitlement, amount_requested, amount_covered, amount_overage, unit_rate_cents} — ranks usable entitlements by smallest-remaining-first so finite buckets drain before NULL-unlimited ones (NULL quantity_allowed treated as unlimited, used for SLA 'coverage' kind); applyConsumption writes ledger row AND increments contract_entitlements.quantity_used atomically via repository.consume(id, amount) AND emits `contract.consumption_recorded` audit (entitlement_id NULL when no coverage — ledger still records full overage for traceability); buildInvoiceLineSuggestions translates a coverage result into [{covered line at entitlement.unit_rate_cents}, {overage line at standard rate}] skipping either zero-quantity side; listLedger gated on contracts.view; recordManualAdjustment gated on contracts.manage, requires non-empty notes, rejects cross-contract entitlement_id, emits `contract.manual_adjustment` audit; 3 new routes GET `/api/contracts/{id}/consumption-ledger`, POST `/api/contracts/{id}/consumption/preview` (supports optional standard_rate_cents for invoice-line preview), POST `/api/contracts/{id}/consumption/manual-adjustment`; 22-case behavior test covers coverage (no contract, unlimited, kind mismatch, inactive/exhausted buckets, bucket-draining order, finite-before-unlimited, site scoping, wrong company, non-active contract, invalid kind/amount), applyConsumption (ledger + entitlement increment + audit, no-coverage fallback), invoice-line splits, and manual-adjustment guardrails)_
- [x] SLA tier enforcement at ticket create _(4.5 — new ContractSlaResolver service given a Ticket returns the tightest-response_minutes TicketSlaPolicy linked by any active covering contract's active entitlement.sla_policy_id — NULL company_id short-circuits to null, site-scoped contracts filtered via existing ContractRepository.listActiveForSite NOT EXISTS OR EXISTS pattern, inactive entitlements + inactive policies + non-active contracts skipped; SlaClockService now takes an optional ContractSlaResolver (backwards-compat — null falls through to policies.findForTicket(division_id, priority)) and exposes resolvePolicy(Ticket) returning {policy, source: 'contract'|'default', contract_id, entitlement_id}; attachForTicket mints clocks against the resolved policy so gold-tier customers get their contracted response/arrival/resolution targets instead of the division default; TicketController.createTicket calls resolvePolicy and emits an `sla_tier_contract` timeline event (with policy_id, contract_id, entitlement_id, per-kind minutes) when source='contract', plus adds sla_policy_id/sla_source/sla_contract_id to the ticket.created audit context; wiring in routes/modules/tickets.php passes a concrete resolver so the HTTP path gets contract SLA, while bin/cron/ticket-sla-breach + bin/cron/ticket-escalation keep the null default (cron doesn't create tickets); 13-case behavior test covers null company fallback, default fall-through, entitlement without sla_policy_id, contract override wins, tightest-wins across competing contracts, site-scope inclusion/exclusion, inactive entitlement + inactive policy filters, non-active contract filter, attachForTicket uses contract policy targets, null-resolver backwards-compat path, no-policy fallthrough)_

### Phase 5 — Preventative Maintenance
- [x] `pm_plans` + `pm_schedules` linked to customers/sites/assets _(5.1 — migration 118 two tables: pm_plans (company_id+division_id both nullable so plans can be shared globally or scoped, title, description, default_priority enum matching existing p1_critical..p4_low, estimated_duration_minutes, checklist_json JSON, default_category_id/queue_id/assigned_user_id forward-looking for 5.3 auto-generation, is_active, created_by_user_id) + pm_schedules (plan_id FK CASCADE, company_id, site_id+asset_id both nullable so a schedule can bind to a site-wide task or a specific installed asset, frequency_kind enum fixed_interval/calendar/meter/condition, frequency_config JSON carrying per-kind params for 5.2 engine, starts_at DATE, next_due_at DATE, last_generated_at DATETIME, lead_time_days, status enum active/paused/archived, contract_id+contract_entitlement_id nullable forward-looking for 5.5 billing linkage, notes); PmPlanRepository + PmScheduleRepository with JSON hydration + search filters; PmScheduleRepository.listDueThrough(cutoff) for 5.3 cron uses `DATE_SUB(next_due_at, INTERVAL lead_time_days DAY) <= :cutoff` so lead_time behaves as "generate N days early"; PmController plan CRUD + schedule CRUD with validation (title required, priority/status/frequency_kind enums, duration + lead_time_days non-negative, plan/site/asset FK existence checks); deletePlan blocks when active schedules still reference the plan (archive-first workflow) to avoid orphaned schedules; updateSchedule strips last_generated_at from body (owned exclusively by 5.3 generator cron); 10 routes GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /api/pm/plans + /api/pm/schedules in routes/modules/pm.php wired into routes/api.php module loader; new perms pm.view + pm.manage granted to manager (both), technician (both — execute PM work), dispatcher (view only — routing awareness); 17-case behavior test covers plan validation (title, priority enum, duration non-negative), plan update empty-title guard, deletePlan active-schedule guardrail + audit on delete, schedule validation (plan_id/company_id/starts_at required, status + frequency_kind enums, lead_time_days non-negative, plan/site/asset existence checks), updateSchedule last_generated_at strip, and pm.view + pm.manage gate enforcement)_
- [x] Frequency engine (daily→annual + meter/hours + condition-triggered) _(5.2 — PmFrequencyService is a pure-compute engine with three entry points: initialNextDue(schedule) returns starts_at for every kind so creation is deterministic, advanceAfterGeneration(schedule) is called by the 5.3 cron right after spawning a PM ticket to roll next_due_at forward one tick, and advanceForReading(schedule, reading, today) handles meter-driven schedules when a fresh reading crosses the threshold; fixed_interval accepts interval_days/weeks/months/years in frequency_config and uses PHP DateInterval (PnD/PnW/PnM/PnY) so month/year edge cases (Feb→Mar rollover, leap day → non-leap year) are handled by standard library; calendar walks up to 400 days forward (loop-guard against mis-configured rules) matching day_of_month (with last-day clamp so dom=31 still lands on Feb 28), day_of_week (mon..sun abbreviated or full), and months_of_year filter which combine AND-wise for quarterly patterns like {months:[1,4,7,10], day_of_month:1}; meter projection supports multi-interval catch-up — if a reading jumps past multiple thresholds baseline_reading advances by floor(overrun/interval) multiples so a single cron pass can collapse missed ticks, and the mutated baseline is written back into schedule.frequency_config for the caller to persist; condition kind is a no-op on auto-advance so manual triggers stay owner of next_due_at; invalid frequency_kind, missing fixed_interval spec, invalid day_of_week, and empty calendar config all throw InvalidArgumentException so misconfigured schedules fail loudly; 18-case behavior test covers every branch above including Feb clamp, quarterly jump Apr→Jul, weekly day_of_week, meter skip-advance, condition no-op, and unknown-kind guard)_
- [x] PM→WO auto-generation job (extend `bin/cron/run.php`) _(5.3 — generates tickets not workorders because Workorder requires customer_id/vehicle_id/estimate_id (auto-repair-shop specific) while support-desk PM fits the Ticket model which already has company_id/site_id/asset_id/division_id; migration 119 adds pm_generations append-only audit table (schedule_id+plan_id+ticket_id nullable+due_at+generated_at+status enum generated/failed+failure_reason TEXT) indexed on schedule+plan+ticket+due_at so 5.4 compliance report can aggregate by schedule; PmGeneratorService.runDueThrough(today) walks PmScheduleRepository.listDueThrough, spawns one ticket per schedule with source='pm_generator' + source_ref='pm_schedule:{id}' carrying plan defaults (category_id, priority, queue_id, assigned_to_user_id, division_id) and composing title as '{plan.title} — due {dueAt}' + description from plan.description with checklist_json rendered as indented bullet list (handles both string entries and {label:...} objects); generateOne() records pm_generations row with ticket_id, advances schedule via PmFrequencyService.advanceAfterGeneration (returns null for meter/condition so cron leaves next_due_at untouched for event-driven kinds), always stamps last_generated_at and re-persists frequency_config so meter baseline mutations survive, emits pm.generated audit event; per-schedule try/catch isolates failures — a broken plan or ticket-insert error records a status='failed' generation row with truncated failure_reason (≤500 char) and run continues so one bad schedule doesn't block the whole batch; bin/cron/pm-generator.php wired into bin/cron/run.php at '0 2 * * *' (daily 02:00, after contracts-renewal at 01:00 but before inventory-low-stock at 08:00); 7-case behavior test covers empty due list, fixed_interval happy path (ticket payload, source/source_ref, cadence advance 2026-04-20 → 2026-05-20, audit), checklist append, meter kind leaves next_due_at untouched, missing-plan failure isolation across multiple schedules, ticket-create failure captured as failed row with failure_reason, and plan priority flow-through)_
- [x] Missed/overdue tracking + compliance reporting _(5.4 — PmScheduleRepository.listOverdue(asOf, filters) returns active schedules where next_due_at < asOf (optional company_id/site_id filters applied via WHERE clause); PmComplianceService has three public methods: overdueReport(asOf) returns {as_of, count, overdue:[{schedule_id, plan_id, company_id, site_id, asset_id, frequency_kind, next_due_at, last_generated_at, days_overdue}]} — days_overdue computed via DateTimeImmutable::diff for UI colour-coding; scheduleCompliance(schedule, since, until) computes expected cadence ticks over window (fixed_interval uses intdiv(span_days, interval_days_equiv) where weeks×7/months×30/years×365; calendar walks PmFrequencyService.advanceAfterGeneration forward from starts_at up to 600 iterations counting ticks that land in [since, until] so expected uses the SAME math as generation to prevent drift), compares actual via PmGenerationRepository.countsForScheduleSince, returns {expected, generated, failed, compliance_rate=min(1, gen/exp)}; meter/condition return expected=null + compliance_rate=null + reason='event_driven' so UI can render "event-driven (N/A)" instead of a bogus %; companyCompliance(companyId, since, until) iterates PmScheduleRepository.search({company_id, status:active}), aggregates expected/generated/failed into a weighted total, per_schedule array surfaces individual rows so managers can drill down; zero-interval config returns expected=0 → rate=1.0 by convention (no ticks expected = fully compliant); no-schedules case returns rate=null rather than 0 to distinguish "no data" from "zero%"; 3 new routes in routes/modules/pm.php — GET /api/pm/compliance/overdue (filters: as_of, company_id, site_id), GET /api/pm/schedules/{id}/compliance (since defaults to -90 days, since/until as query), GET /api/pm/compliance/companies/{companyId} — all gated on pm.view; PmController accepts optional PmComplianceService (nullable for backwards-compat with existing tests that construct without it); 10-case behavior test covers days_overdue math, company filter propagation, fixed_interval 90-day window expected=3 with gen=2/fail=1 → rate=2/3, rate cap at 1.0, meter + condition null rate with event_driven reason, calendar monthly walk, zero-interval convention, weighted companyCompliance rollup with mixed kinds (meter contributes to generated but not expected), empty-company null rate)_
- [x] Contract entitlement linkage _(5.5 — migration 120 extends pm_generations with consumption_applied_at DATETIME + consumption_entitlement_id + consumption_amount DECIMAL(12,2) + consumption_ledger_id, indexed on consumption_applied_at so Phase 5.4 "pending completion" queries are cheap; PmGenerationRepository.markConsumptionApplied uses a WHERE consumption_applied_at IS NULL clause — if the UPDATE affects 0 rows it throws so racing callers can't double-apply; new PmLinkageService.applyCompletion(actor, generationId, entitlementKind, amount, notes?) is the single entrypoint — gates on pm.manage, validates amount > 0, loads generation + schedule, returns {status: skipped, reason: already_applied} with the prior ledger_id/entitlement_id/amount if consumption was already recorded (idempotency); generations with status=failed or ticket_id=NULL are rejected because there's nothing to bill against; if the schedule has no contract_id the completion still stamps consumption_applied_at (so the "pending" list drains) and emits the audit with contract_bound=false but skips ContractBillingService entirely; contract-bound completions call ContractBillingService.applyConsumption with sourceType='ticket' + sourceId=the generated ticket_id (NOT the generation_id) so the 4.4 consumption ledger threads back to the user-visible ticket, passing notes='PM completion: pm_schedule=X pm_generation=Y' when caller didn't supply their own; the returned ContractConsumptionLedger.id, entitlement_id, amount_covered, amount_overage all flow into pm_generations and the audit context so overage is visible without joining the ledger; new route POST /api/pm/generations/{id}/complete accepts {amount, entitlement_kind?='hours', notes?}; wiring in routes/modules/pm.php constructs a ContractBillingService using existing ContractRepository/ContractEntitlementRepository/ContractConsumptionRepository + ctx gate/audit (reusing the Phase 4 infrastructure); PmController accepts optional PmLinkageService (nullable for backwards compat) — 14 total pm routes now; 10-case behavior test covers contract-bound happy path (verifies sourceType=ticket + sourceId=ticket_id threading, entitlement_id + ledger_id in response, consumption_applied_at stamp, pm.completion_recorded audit), non-contract schedule skips billing but still marks applied with reason=no_contract_binding, idempotency (second call returns skipped with prior ledger metadata, billing not re-invoked), zero + negative amount rejection, missing-generation guard, failed-generation + orphan-schedule rejection, pm.manage gate, and overage split (amount_covered=2.0, amount_overage=2.0 on 4.0 request) surfaced in both return value and audit context)_

### Phase 6 — Customer Portal (isolated)
- [x] Separate portal auth bounded to admin-provisioned accounts only _(6.1 — migration 121 portal_accounts table is the durable enforcement point: user_id + company_id with UNIQUE(user_id, company_id) prevents duplicate bindings, allowed_site_ids JSON NULL (null ≡ all sites in company), is_active + revoked_at + revoked_reason enables soft-revoke preserving audit trail, provisioned_by_user_id records the admin actor, last_login_at stamped by PortalAuthService.login; PortalAccount model with isUsable() (active AND not revoked) + allowsSite(int) helpers; PortalAccountRepository CRUD with JSON hydration, provision() throws on duplicate (user_id, company_id), listByCompany(activeOnly) for admin UI; PortalAuthService core flow: (a) login(email,password,ip,ua) verifies creds → rejects non-portal_user role → loads+validates active portal_account → issues scope-gated JWT with extra claims {scope:'portal', portal_account_id, company_id, site_ids} → emits portal.auth.login audit → stamps last_login_at (returns null on any failure with no info leak); (b) provisionAccount(actor, input) is admin-gated on users.create, supports both "new user" {name,email,password,company_id,site_ids,notes} and "bind existing user" {user_id,company_id,site_ids} shapes, validates every site_id belongs to the target company_id before inserting (multi-tenant safety), rejects binding non-portal_user roles; (c) assertValidSession(user, payload) is the per-request re-validation — rejects scope!='portal', account not found, user-ownership mismatch, is_active=0, revoked_at set, company_id claim mismatch — so revocation takes effect within one request regardless of JWT TTL; (d) assertSiteAccess, assertListAccess, revokeAccount, updateAccount round out the surface; scope boundary enforced in three places (belt+suspenders): AuthService.staffLogin now also rejects role='portal_user' alongside 'customer' so portal users can't use the staff login path, Middleware::auth() decodes bearer payload and throws if scope='portal' so portal tokens can't hit staff routes, PortalAuthService.assertValidSession rejects staff tokens on the portal side; JwtService.generateToken extended with optional extraClaims array (merged into payload, reserved keys iss/sub/iat/exp/type protected) + new validateTokenWithPayload returning {user, payload} for scope inspection — backwards compatible; new Middleware::portalAuth(PortalAuthService) is bearer-only (no cookie/session fallback) and attaches user + portal_account + portal_scope request attributes; routes/modules/portal.php has three groups: public throttled login (10req/60s strict) at POST /api/portal/auth/login, portalAuth-gated GET /api/portal/auth/me, and staff-auth + users.create-gated admin CRUD at POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /api/portal-accounts (DELETE takes reason in request body); routes/api.php module loader registers 'portal'; PortalAuthService made findUserByEmail/findUserById/createPortalUser protected specifically for testability (FakePortalAuthService subclass overrides in-memory without needing PDO); 19-case behavior test covers all three boundaries — login gating (wrong password no-leak, staff role rejected, portal_user without account rejected, revoked account rejected at login, scope+company+portal_account_id+site_ids claims in issued token, last_login_at stamped), session re-validation (matching claims accepted, staff-scope rejected, ownership mismatch rejected, live revocation rejected without TTL wait, company_id mismatch rejected), admin provisioning (creates user+account with scoped sites + audit, cross-company site scoping rejected, users.create gate enforced, unknown company rejected, cannot bind non-portal user, revocation flips flags + audits and is idempotent), plus assertSiteAccess allow/deny and null-sites-grants-all semantics)_
- [x] Request wizard (type selector → category routing) _(6.2 — migration 122 adds portal_visible TINYINT+index to ticket_categories (default 0 so every existing row stays staff-only until admin explicitly opts-in; indexed composite (portal_visible, is_active) so the wizard's hot-path read stays cheap); TicketCategoryRepository gains listPortalVisibleRoots() + listPortalVisibleChildren(parentId) which combine is_active=1+portal_visible=1 and reuse the same ORDER BY name pattern; create()/update() now accept portal_visible so admins can toggle per-row via the existing ticket_categories.manage endpoint; new PortalRequestWizardService orchestrates three wizard steps — listRequestTypes(account) returns portal-visible top-level rows, listCategoriesForType(account, rootCategoryId) rejects unknown/non-portal-visible/child-passed-as-root and returns portal-visible children, submitRequest(user, account, input) mints a ticket with invariants enforced server-side regardless of body contents (source='portal' forced, company_id forced from account, reported_by_user_id=user.id, reporter_name/email from user — body's company_id/assigned_to_user_id/queue_id/parent_ticket_id/status/source ignored so portal input cannot privilege-escalate); site scoping is a three-layer guard (site exists, site.company_id matches account.company_id, PortalAuthService.assertSiteAccess honors allowed_site_ids) and asset_id is validated against the asset's site in the same three layers then defaults site_id from the asset when caller didn't pin one; priority is clamped to ALLOWED_PRIORITIES=[p3_normal, p4_low] so portal users cannot self-escalate to p1/p2 — disallowed values fall back to the root's default_priority (if allowed) else p3_normal; after create the ticket is passed through the existing TicketRoutingService from Phase 3.3 so portal submissions inherit routing rules (rules can match on source='portal' to send them to a triage queue); a non-internal 'created' ticket_event is recorded so the portal user can see their own submission on the timeline plus an 'auto_routed' internal event when a rule fires; audit event portal.request.submitted carries portal_account_id, company_id, site_id, asset_id, category_id, subcategory_id, priority, routing_rule_id; new PortalRequestController is a thin HTTP facade; routes/modules/portal.php adds three portalAuth-gated routes — GET /api/portal/request-types, GET /api/portal/request-types/{id}/subcategories, POST /api/portal/requests — and wires fresh TicketCategoryRepository + TicketRepository + TicketEventRepository + TicketRoutingRuleRepository + TicketRoutingService + SiteAssetRepository off $ctx->connection; PortalAccount.isUsable() is re-asserted in submitRequest as a belt+suspenders check so even a stale $account from a prior middleware pass can't submit after revocation; 24-case behavior test covers listing filters (active+portal_visible only, name-order sort, non-portal-visible root rejection, child-passed-as-root rejection), submission invariants (untrusted company_id/assigned_to_user_id/queue_id/parent_ticket_id/status/source all ignored, forced portal scope, source_ref stamped as portal_account:{id}, non-internal created event), validation (empty title, >255 title, >20000 description, missing/unknown/hidden/child-as-root category, subcategory wrong-parent/hidden, unknown/cross-company/out-of-scope site, null allowed_site_ids grants all in-company sites, asset default site fallback, cross-company/unknown asset, priority clamp + p4_low passthrough), Phase 3.3 routing rule fire with audit routing_rule_id, and revoked portal_account submission rejection)_
- [x] Approval center (estimates / change orders / renewals) _(6.3 — PortalApprovalService is a single entrypoint for everything a portal user has to act on; no new migrations because Phase 2.2's estimates + 4.1's contracts + 4.3's renewal flow already carry every field we need; scoping is explicit and server-side: estimates resolve via customers.company_id because the legacy estimates table has no company_id column (the Phase 1.1 nullable FK on customers bridges that gap) and contracts scope via contracts.company_id directly; loadScopedEstimate/loadScopedContract both call assertUsable(account) first so revoked portal_accounts are blocked from every action regardless of middleware (belt+suspenders — Middleware::portalAuth already checks, but defense-in-depth here keeps the service honest if someone calls it outside the HTTP path); ESTIMATE_PENDING_STATUSES=[pending,sent] and CONTRACT_PENDING_STATUSES=[draft,pending_signature] are the only statuses that accept decisions — approving an already-approved estimate or cancelling a cancelled contract throws InvalidArgumentException 'not awaiting approval' so idempotent replays from the portal UI fail loud rather than silently double-audit; approveEstimate/rejectEstimate delegate the transition to EstimateRepository::updateStatus (same method staff use) so the existing estimate.status_changed audit + rejection_reason column wiring flows for free, then layer a portal.approval.estimate_{approved,rejected} audit on top carrying portal_account_id + company_id + customer_id + note/reason so the portal timeline is distinguishable from staff actions; contract approval transitions to 'active' + stamps signed_at + signer_ip + signer_user_agent (route passes Request.getClientIp() and User-Agent header through so signature metadata reflects the actual portal session, not the server) and rejection transitions to 'cancelled' + stamps cancelled_at + cancellation_reason='Portal rejection: {reason}'; every contract decision writes a ContractAmendment row — approving a successor contract (renewed_from_contract_id NOT NULL) uses kind='renew' while approving a fresh contract uses kind='change_scope' so the Phase 4.1 append-only amendment log carries the distinction the UI needs to render "Renewal accepted" vs "Contract accepted"; rejection always uses kind='terminate' regardless of renewal-or-new since either way the contract is dead; delta_json carries portal_account_id + prior_status + note/reason for auditors; listPending returns {estimates:[], contracts:[]} with separate serialized payloads — estimates expose id/number/status/customer_id/vehicle_id/expiration_date/grand_total/subtotal/tax/customer_notes/created_at (everything the portal approval card needs without a second fetch), contracts expose id/contract_number/status/kind (new|renewal)/renewed_from_contract_id/title/description/start_date/end_date/billing_frequency/billing_amount_cents/auto_renew/terms_markdown; pendingEstimatesFor walks ESTIMATE_PENDING_STATUSES and filters client-side via customer FK (N+1 on customer lookup, documented acceptable for pending queues which are by definition short human-reviewed lists — can add listByCompanyId helper later if queues grow huge) while pendingContractsFor uses ContractRepository::search with company_id filter (efficient SQL path because contracts.company_id exists as a real column); PortalApprovalController is a thin HTTP facade — route handlers stay one line each, controller trims body strings and enforces "reason required" for both reject endpoints so reject-without-reason fails at the HTTP boundary; 5 portalAuth-gated routes in routes/modules/portal.php: GET /api/portal/approvals, POST /api/portal/approvals/estimates/{id}/approve (body:{note?}), POST /api/portal/approvals/estimates/{id}/reject (body:{reason}), POST /api/portal/approvals/contracts/{id}/approve (body:{note?} + ip/UA auto-injected from Request), POST /api/portal/approvals/contracts/{id}/reject (body:{reason}); deliberately NOT delegating to EstimateController::approve/reject because those gate on estimates.manage staff permission — portal path authenticates via portal_account ownership instead, so bridging the two paths would require permission-splitting inside EstimateController which is exactly the foot-gun Phase 6.1 isolated by design; 20-case behavior test covers listPending filter matrix (mix of matching-company/different-company/null-company customers across pending+sent+approved estimate statuses + draft+pending_signature+active contract statuses — expects only [pending,sent] × matching-company estimates and [draft,pending_signature] × matching-company contracts), renewal serialization (kind=renewal when renewed_from_contract_id set), listPending revoked-account rejection, approveEstimate happy path (status transition + note flowed into reason + audit with portal_account_id/company_id/customer_id), approveEstimate cross-company rejection via customer FK, approveEstimate missing-customer rejection (fail-closed — if we can't resolve scope we reject), approveEstimate double-approve rejection, approveEstimate unknown-id rejection, rejectEstimate happy path + audit, rejectEstimate blank-reason rejection, approveContract (new) transition + signer metadata + change_scope amendment + audit kind=new, approveContract (renewal) writes renew amendment + audit kind=renewal, approveContract cross-company rejection, approveContract non-pending-status rejection, approveContract unknown-id rejection, rejectContract cancellation + cancellation_reason + terminate amendment + audit with reason, rejectContract blank-reason rejection, rejectContract non-pending-status rejection, revoked portal_account blocked from all four actions, non-positive id rejection before any repo lookup)_
- [x] Site asset view, invoices, payments, saved methods _(6.4 — three new service surfaces (PortalAssetViewService, PortalBillingService) behind two thin controllers (PortalAssetController, PortalBillingController) and 11 new portalAuth-gated routes in routes/modules/portal.php; only one new migration (123_portal_payment_methods.sql) because sites + site_assets + invoices + payments already exist from earlier phases — we're adding a portal-owned tokenized payment method vault and read-only views over everything else; PortalPaymentMethod model + PortalPaymentMethodRepository store ONLY gateway tokens (external_customer_id + external_method_id) plus non-sensitive display metadata (brand/last4/exp_month/exp_year/label/is_default) — no PAN/CVV/full card data ever touches our database because client-side tokenization (Stripe Elements / Square Web SDK / PayPal Smart Buttons) produces the opaque reference before it ever reaches us; UNIQUE (portal_account_id, gateway, external_method_id) prevents double-save of the same gateway token and FK ON DELETE CASCADE to portal_accounts garbage-collects vault references when an account is purged; setDefault is a two-statement transaction (clear all is_default=0 for this account, set target is_default=1) so two near-simultaneous default-flip requests can't leave the account with two defaults; site view layer (PortalAssetViewService) wraps SiteRepository + SiteAssetRepository with the same three-layer scope guard from Phase 6.2 — assertUsable(account) then company_id match then assertSiteAccess — and serializeSite deliberately strips alarm_code_encrypted, gate_code_encrypted, and notes from the staff Site shape so portal users never see gate codes or internal notes even via direct hit; asset serialization is similarly curated — identity/location/warranty/lifecycle fields pass through, sensitive columns stay hidden; listAssetsAtSite resolves the site before enumerating assets so cross-company site IDs reject at the site layer rather than leaking "no assets here" vs "this site isn't yours"; getAsset re-resolves scope through the asset's site.company_id so an asset whose site belongs to a sibling company reports "does not belong" rather than 404 (consistency-first over enumeration-first — same pattern as Phase 6.2 asset_id validation); billing layer resolves invoice scope via customers.company_id because the legacy invoices table has no company_id column (same bridge Phase 6.3 uses for estimates — Phase 1.1's nullable FK on customers.company_id is the pivot); CustomerRepository gains listIdsForCompany(companyId) returning only IDs so PortalBillingService can walk (customer_id, status) × InvoiceService.list without hydrating full Customer rows it doesn't need — N+1 accepted here because portal invoice lists are by definition short (a single tenant's worth), can add a company-scoped batch helper if queues ever grow huge; PORTAL_VISIBLE_INVOICE_STATUSES=[pending, sent, partial, paid] and UNPAID_INVOICE_STATUSES=[pending, sent, partial] — deliberately drops void/uncollectible/credit-memo from the portal view since those are administrative states portal users can't act on; unpaid_only=true narrows to UNPAID_INVOICE_STATUSES so the portal's "pay now" surface only shows actionable invoices; results sorted by issue_date DESC so newest-first rendering is the server's responsibility not the client's; startCheckout mirrors the public-link pay flow (InvoicePublicPaymentTokenService pins amount via short-lived token before gateway handoff) because we reuse the same PaymentProcessingService.createCheckoutSession path staff use — pinning the amount server-side means a portal user can't tamper with checkout amount via browser devtools, and consumeToken after the gateway returns closes the window where a leaked token could be replayed; paid-invoice and zero-balance guards fail loud (InvalidArgumentException) so the UI surfaces a meaningful error rather than silently creating a $0 Stripe session; provider validated against [stripe, square, paypal] enum; success_url/cancel_url are only passed through if controller supplied them — service never trusts arbitrary URLs and controller strips anything that isn't a non-empty string (defense against redirect-to-attacker-site); portal.payment.checkout_started audit carries portal_account_id + company_id + customer_id + provider + amount so auditors can trace every portal-initiated payment; savePaymentMethod enforces gateway∈enum, external_method_id 1-128 chars (gateway tokens are ~30 chars but 128 handles future formats), external_customer_id ≤128 chars, last4 exactly 4 digits after non-digit stripping (so "4242" and "•••• 4242" both normalize), exp_month 1-12, exp_year 2000-2100 (so the UX fails-loud on obvious typos), label ≤80 chars and trimmed-to-null when blank; if this is the user's first saved method it auto-promotes to is_default=true via setDefault call after create — single non-default saved method is a UX dead-end (every checkout still has to ask "which method?") so we just promote it; portal.payment_method.saved audit carries gateway + brand + last4 + is_default (never the token itself, auditors never need the token); setDefaultMethod loads scoped method via loadScopedMethod (same assertUsable + portal_account_id match) then calls the atomic swap + audits portal.payment_method.set_default; deletePaymentMethod same pattern + portal.payment_method.deleted audit carrying gateway so auditors know which vault had the token revoked (actual gateway-side revocation happens in a later webhook-triggered cleanup job — this just drops our reference); loadScopedMethod returns "payment method {id} not found" for both unknown-id AND cross-account-id because using "not yours" as the error would let a malicious portal user enumerate other accounts' method IDs via timing/message differences (defense in depth against account enumeration); serializeMethod deliberately hides external_customer_id + external_method_id from the API response because those are gateway-internal references the client never needs to see — only ID + display metadata; routes (11 total) — GET /api/portal/sites, GET /api/portal/sites/{id}, GET /api/portal/sites/{id}/assets (accepts status+query+limit+offset), GET /api/portal/assets/{id}, GET /api/portal/invoices (accepts unpaid_only+limit), GET /api/portal/invoices/{id}, POST /api/portal/invoices/{id}/checkout (body: provider, success_url?, cancel_url?), GET /api/portal/payment-methods, POST /api/portal/payment-methods (body: gateway, external_customer_id?, external_method_id, brand?, last4?, exp_month?, exp_year?, label?), POST /api/portal/payment-methods/{id}/default, DELETE /api/portal/payment-methods/{id}; payments config loaded inline in portal.php via require __DIR__ . '/../../config/payments.php' to match the api.php pattern (payment config is not part of $ctx->config); 2 behavior test suites: PortalAssetViewServiceTest (14 cases — listSites company-scope + inactive-rejection + whitelist-honor + serialization-strips-secrets + inactive/revoked rejection, getSite cross-company + whitelist + unknown-id, listAssetsAtSite scope-before-enumerate, getAsset scope via asset.site + whitelist + unknown) and PortalBillingServiceTest (20 cases — listInvoices customers.company_id scope + status whitelist + unpaid_only narrowing + sort-newest-first + empty-company + inactive rejection, getInvoice scope + cross-company reject + unknown/non-positive reject, startCheckout happy-path with token issue+consume + gateway call + audit + amount pinned to balance_due, already-paid reject, zero-balance reject, bad-provider reject, savePaymentMethod first-save-auto-defaults + subsequent-save-does-not + gateway/external_method_id/last4/exp_month/exp_year validation + external-ids-hidden-from-response + audit, listPaymentMethods account-scope + no-id-leak, setDefaultMethod swap + audit + cross-account-is-not-found + unknown-is-not-found, deletePaymentMethod + audit + cross-account-is-not-found, revoked account blocked from ALL seven entrypoints))_
- [x] Messaging tied to tickets/WOs (reuse `MessageThread`) _(6.5 — one new migration (124_portal_message_thread_links.sql) adds ticket_id + workorder_id (nullable, indexed, FK ON DELETE SET NULL) to message_threads and is_internal TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 + portal_account_id nullable FK to message_messages with composite index (thread_id, is_internal) so the portal's hot-path "show me non-internal messages for this thread" read stays one index scan; idempotent ALTERs via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running the migration on an already-patched schema is a no-op rather than an error; MessageThread and MessageMessage models gain the new fields; new PortalMessagingService sits alongside the existing staff MessagingService (they share the message_* tables but never cross methods) — dual-service split keeps portal scope/filtering isolated and prevents a future staff-side refactor from accidentally opening a portal surface; three-layer scope at every entrypoint (belt+suspenders — Middleware::portalAuth already gates): assertUsable(account) rejects revoked/inactive, tickets scope via tickets.company_id (direct FK — unlike estimates/invoices which bridge through customers.company_id, tickets carry company_id directly so the simpler scope path is available), if ticket.site_id is set PortalAuthService.assertSiteAccess enforces allowed_site_ids, workorders scope via customers.company_id bridge (legacy workorders table has no company_id column — same Phase 6.3/6.4 pattern); loadScopedThread re-validates end-to-end: the thread MUST be linked to a ticket or workorder that passes the above scope AND the portal user must already be a participant in message_participants — unlinked threads (ticket_id IS NULL AND workorder_id IS NULL, staff-only by definition) reject with "not linked to a portal-accessible ticket or workorder", and threads the portal user hasn't been added to reject with "not a participant" (portal never adds itself to arbitrary staff threads — can ONLY start a new one on its own ticket/WO); listMessages and the unread_count subquery in listThreads both WHERE is_internal = 0 so staff-flagged internal notes stay off the portal no matter how the thread was started; all portal INSERTs set is_internal=0 and stamp portal_account_id so the staff UI can distinguish portal-session authorship from the underlying user.id (portal_account.user_id IS a real users.id so sender_id alone would confuse the staff timeline); start-thread and post-message are each a single transaction (thread+participant+initial message insert committed together; post-message insert + UPDATE updated_at committed together) so a mid-insert crash can't leave the portal with a thread but no rendering body or vice versa; CURRENT_TIMESTAMP used instead of NOW() throughout for portability across MySQL-prod and SQLite-tests; body validation trims then rejects empty + caps at 20000 chars (same limit staff messaging uses); sanitizeSubject prefers caller-supplied subject, else falls back to ticket.title or 'WO-' + workorder.number so every thread has a human-readable subject; audit events portal.message.thread_started + portal.message.posted carry portal_account_id + company_id + message_id + ticket_id/workorder_id + body_length (body text itself never in audit — audit logs aren't PII-cleared); PortalMessagingController is a thin HTTP facade — 7 one-line methods + requireBody() that trims and rejects empty at the HTTP boundary so controller-level validation fails fast before service transaction opens; 7 portalAuth-gated routes in routes/modules/portal.php: GET /api/portal/tickets/{id}/threads, POST /api/portal/tickets/{id}/threads (body:{body, subject?}), GET /api/portal/workorders/{id}/threads, POST /api/portal/workorders/{id}/threads (body:{body, subject?}), GET /api/portal/threads/{id}/messages, POST /api/portal/threads/{id}/messages (body:{body}), POST /api/portal/threads/{id}/read; markRead upserts into message_reads (SELECT latest → UPDATE if row exists else INSERT) so repeated read-pings don't duplicate rows — and only counts non-internal messages as "latest" so staff-posted internals can't poison the portal's last_read_message_id pointer; 14-case behavior test (PortalMessagingServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern (DashboardServiceTechnicianScopeTest-style Connection subclass + minimal message_* schema) with PmFakeTickets/PmFakeWorkorders/PmFakeCustomers repo fakes and an anonymous PortalAuthService subclass overriding assertSiteAccess — test gates on extension_loaded('sqlite') equivalent (PDO::getAvailableDrivers includes 'sqlite') and prints a clean skip line when run in environments without pdo_sqlite; coverage: startThreadForTicket happy path (thread + participant + message created, portal_account_id stamped, subject defaults to ticket.title, audit emitted), startThreadForTicket cross-company rejection, startThreadForTicket allowed_site_ids whitelist (outside-whitelist rejected + inside-whitelist allowed), startThreadForTicket body validation (blank + >20000), startThreadForWorkorder happy path via customers.company_id bridge with WO-number subject default, startThreadForWorkorder cross-company via customer.company_id mismatch, postMessage tagging + audit, postMessage blank-body rejection, postMessage + listMessages reject thread where portal user isn't a participant, loadScopedThread rejects unlinked (ticket_id+workorder_id both NULL) threads, listMessages filters is_internal=1 and stamps is_from_portal flag correctly (initial portal=true, staff reply portal=false), listThreadsForTicket returns only participating threads for the given ticket (hidden thread on same ticket where portal user isn't a participant correctly excluded), markRead records + upserts (second call updates rather than inserting duplicate row), revoked portal_account is blocked from ALL 7 entrypoints, non-positive + unknown ids are rejected before DB work)_
- [x] Document/photo upload _(6.6 — one new migration (125_portal_uploads.sql) adds a polymorphic portal_uploads table that spans all three portal upload surfaces (tickets / workorders / message_threads) behind a single (entity_type, entity_id) pair — one junction table instead of three keeps the repository surface small and the audit events (portal.upload.{created,deleted}) one code path; row columns: portal_account_id (FK CASCADE to portal_accounts so account purge GCs references), company_id (denormalized from portal_accounts so the hot-path per-entity read doesn't need a join), entity_type VARCHAR(32) + entity_id (polymorphic link), original_name VARCHAR(255) (sanitized before persist), stored_path VARCHAR(512) (webroot-relative, e.g. /uploads/portal/10/202604/abc123.png), mime_type VARCHAR(120) (server-detected from magic bytes, never client-supplied), size_bytes UNSIGNED INT, sha256 CHAR(64) (for dedup + download-time integrity verify), uploaded_by_user_id, created_at + deleted_at (soft-delete); three indexes: (entity_type, entity_id, deleted_at) for the canonical "uploads on ticket 42" hot path, (portal_account_id, deleted_at) for per-account audit, (company_id, deleted_at) for tenant-wide cleanup jobs; FK install is idempotent via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running the migration on a patched schema is a no-op rather than an error; PortalUpload model + PortalUploadRepository (create/findById(includeDeleted=false)/listForEntity/softDelete — all reads filter deleted_at IS NULL by default, the flag exists for cleanup jobs that walk the tail to unlink physical files); PortalUploadValidator is strict — detects MIME via finfo on the magic bytes (NEVER trusts client-supplied $file['type'] or the extension in $file['name'] because both are trivially forged), extension is derived from the detected MIME so "malware.exe renamed to cute.jpg" fails because finfo reads the real bytes, allowlist is [image/jpeg, image/png, image/webp, image/gif, image/heic, application/pdf] (deliberately excludes docx/xlsx because they have macro execution surface and need ClamAV integration before we can safely accept them — easy to extend later), 10MB default cap (UPLOAD_MAX_SIZE env override), captures sha256 at validate time so the hash is pinned to the exact bytes that passed validation, sanitizeOriginalName strips path separators + control bytes + caps at 255 chars so "../../etc/passwd" can't escape the upload dir when echoed in Content-Disposition; PortalUploadStorage abstracts the filesystem — writes to public/uploads/portal/{company_id}/{yyyymm}/ with random 16-byte hex filenames so attackers can't enumerate tenants' files by guessing IDs AND so leaked direct URLs aren't predictable even if webroot served the dir statically (double defense — even if apache/nginx misconfigs and serves the dir, you'd need the random filename to hit it), month partition keeps any single dir under a reasonable inode count for archival jobs, persist() tries move_uploaded_file first (the SAPI safety check) then falls back to rename/copy so CLI imports and tests can still drive the service, resolveAbsPath rejects any path containing ".." or that doesn't start with the configured publicBaseUrl so the download endpoint can't be tricked into serving arbitrary filesystem reads; PortalUploadService orchestrates: assertUsable(account) → loadScopedTicket/Workorder/Thread (belt+suspenders even though middleware already gates) → PortalUploadValidator.validate(file) → storage.allocatePath(company_id, ext) → storage.persist(tmp, dest) → repo.create(row) → audit portal.upload.created — with filesystem rollback if the DB insert throws (storage.unlink() before rethrow so we don't leave orphan files on disk when a transaction fails); loadScopedThread requires the thread to link a ticket/WO that passes scope AND the portal user to be a participant (same pattern as Phase 6.5 messaging — unlinked staff-only threads reject with "not linked to a portal-accessible ticket or workorder"); getUploadForDownload RE-RUNS the full entity scope on every download (a URL that worked last month for a now-revoked account / reassigned ticket / narrowed allowed_site_ids list will 401 at download time, not just at list time — this is the critical bit, uploads stay "alive" on disk behind a revalidating URL rather than a signed-URL-with-expiration); deleteUpload soft-deletes (deleted_at stamped) + best-effort physical unlink (fire-and-forget — if unlink fails the audit still goes, a cleanup job can handle NFS hiccups later) + requires portal_account_id match (account A cannot delete account B's upload even if they share a scope); PortalUploadController is a thin HTTP facade — one line per endpoint, requireFile() rejects missing $_FILES['file'] with a clear "upload as multipart/form-data with field name file" message at the HTTP boundary; 8 new portalAuth-gated routes in routes/modules/portal.php: POST /api/portal/tickets/{id}/uploads, POST /api/portal/workorders/{id}/uploads, POST /api/portal/threads/{id}/uploads, GET /api/portal/tickets/{id}/uploads, GET /api/portal/workorders/{id}/uploads, GET /api/portal/threads/{id}/uploads, GET /api/portal/uploads/{id} (download — streams bytes with Content-Disposition: attachment + sanitized filename + nosniff + private no-store cache), DELETE /api/portal/uploads/{id}; 15-case behavior test (PortalUploadServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with PuTestStorage subclass that bypasses move_uploaded_file for CLI/test paths + real PNG magic bytes (base64-decoded 1x1 pixel) so finfo actually runs and validates the allowlist path end-to-end, validator covered separately for: PNG happy-path with sha256 capture, text file with .png extension rejected (proves MIME-from-magic-bytes not from filename), empty file rejection, sanitizeOriginalName strips ../ and caps 255; service covered for: uploadForTicket (persists + records + audits with ticket_id in context), cross-company ticket rejection, allowed_site_ids whitelist honored, uploadForWorkorder bridges via customers.company_id + cross-company rejection, uploadForThread with participant gate + audits linked ticket_id, non-participant rejection, unlinked-thread rejection, listForTicket scoped to that ticket, getUploadForDownload happy path, download re-runs entity scope (ticket company reassigned → download denied even though denorm row still matches — proves we walk the entity every time not just the cached company_id), deleteUpload soft-deletes + unlinks + audits + hides from list, cross-account deleteUpload rejection, revoked portal_account blocked from all 4 action-group entrypoints, non-positive + unknown id rejection)_
- [x] ETA tracking _(6.7 — one new migration (126_portal_eta_promises.sql) adds a polymorphic portal_eta_promises table spanning both portal-visible entities (ticket + workorder) with an append-only revision chain; columns: company_id (denormalized from the owning entity so the hot-path per-entity read doesn't need a join), entity_type VARCHAR(32) + entity_id, window_start_at + window_end_at DATETIME (the ETA is a window not a point so customers can see uncertainty without us making up false precision), source enum (manual/system/traffic_api — manual is staff-typed, system is forward-looking auto-computed from a schedule, traffic_api is forward-looking integration with Phase roadside's TrafficAwareEtaService), confidence TINYINT 0-100 NULL (rendered as a confidence band on the portal), note VARCHAR(1000) NULL (short customer-visible reason inline in the timeline — "parts delayed 2 days", "tech arrived early"), created_by_user_id, created_at, superseded_at + superseded_by_id self-ref FK ON DELETE SET NULL (nullable because the latest row has no successor and cancelled rows have no successor either); three indexes: (entity_type, entity_id, superseded_at) for the canonical "current ETA on ticket 42" (superseded_at IS NULL) read, (entity_type, entity_id, created_at) for history walks, (company_id, created_at) for tenant-wide jobs; FK install idempotent via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running is a no-op rather than an error; PortalEtaPromise model with SOURCE_* constants + ALLOWED_SOURCES array; PortalEtaPromiseRepository with four methods — create (append-only insert), findById, findCurrentForEntity (ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1 WHERE superseded_at IS NULL so there's at most one "current" row and the service layer enforces the invariant), listHistoryForEntity (full chain newest-first), markSuperseded($id, $supersededById) which UPDATEs with a WHERE superseded_at IS NULL clause and returns rowCount > 0 so concurrent supersede races are detectable (two staff publishing simultaneously would see rowCount=0 on the loser and throw); PortalEtaPromiseService is dual-surface (staff-write + portal-read) in one class — the staff surface gates on tickets.manage / workorders.manage via AccessGate.assert and the portal surface uses the three-layer scope (assertUsable(account) + company_id match + assertSiteAccess when ticket has site_id, same pattern as 6.3–6.6); publishPromise wraps the insert + supersede in a single transaction so a concurrent supersede detection rolls back both the new row AND the chain stamp, rather than leaving the chain with two "current" rows (RuntimeException 'concurrent supersede detected' thrown loud so the caller can retry rather than silently racing); publishForTicket resolves company_id directly from tickets.company_id (tickets have a direct FK — unlike workorders), publishForWorkorder resolves via customers.company_id bridge (legacy workorders table has no company_id column, same bridge pattern as 6.3/6.4/6.5/6.6) and throws InvalidArgumentException 'no company binding' for personal-customer WOs so ETAs aren't orphaned into a row the portal will never be able to show; cancelCurrent is distinct from publish — it stamps superseded_at=NOW without inserting a successor (superseded_by_id NULL) so the portal can distinguish "we no longer have an ETA" from "here's the new ETA" — emits portal.eta.cancelled audit with optional reason (1000 char cap); validation surface: window_start_at + window_end_at parsed via DateTimeImmutable (rejects non-datetime strings), end >= start (equality allowed because a point-in-time "we'll be there at 3pm" is legitimate), span <= MAX_WINDOW_SPAN_HOURS (168h = 7 days; tighter windows force staff to commit rather than "sometime next month"), source ∈ ALLOWED_SOURCES, confidence 0-100 or null (numeric check rejects 'abc'), note trimmed + 1000 char cap + blank-to-null; portal read composeHistory returns {entity_type, entity_id, current: ?serialized, history: [serialized...]} where current is null if no unsuperseded row exists (vs. history still carrying cancelled rows) so the UI can render "no ETA yet" vs "ETA was cancelled" from one payload; is_current boolean in serializer makes the UI's current-vs-history distinction explicit per-row; staff read endpoints (listHistoryForTicketStaff + listHistoryForWorkorderStaff) gate on tickets.view / workorders.view respectively — staff dashboards render the same history the portal sees but can reach it for any ticket/WO they have view permission on; PortalEtaPromiseController is a thin HTTP facade (7 methods — 4 staff-write, 1 staff-read-ticket, 1 staff-read-WO, 2 portal-read); new staff routes module routes/modules/eta.php registered in routes/api.php module loader alongside the other 11 modules — 6 staff routes: POST /api/eta/tickets/{id}/promises, DELETE /api/eta/tickets/{id}/promises/current, GET /api/eta/tickets/{id}/promises, POST /api/eta/workorders/{id}/promises, DELETE /api/eta/workorders/{id}/promises/current, GET /api/eta/workorders/{id}/promises (staff auth via Middleware::auth(), gate inside service); portal reads added to routes/modules/portal.php: GET /api/portal/tickets/{id}/eta + GET /api/portal/workorders/{id}/eta (portalAuth-gated); the PortalEtaPromiseService is constructed twice — once in portal.php (portalAuth already live from Phase 6.1) and once in eta.php with a fresh PortalAuthService (the staff surface never invokes portal scope loaders, but the service's constructor still demands the dep — documented and acceptable because the constructor sig is unified across both surfaces); deliberately NOT integrating with TrafficAwareEtaService (Phase roadside dispatch's ETA calculator) for manual staff entry — that service is lat/lng-based route planning for roadside drivers while this is a customer-visible commitment layer; source='traffic_api' reserves the slot so a future cron can auto-populate ETAs from TrafficAwareEtaService without a schema change; 22-case behavior test (PortalEtaPromiseServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with EtaPermissiveGate subclass that grants everything by default and flips specific permissions per-test so the gate-denial path is covered without standing up a full RolePermissions stack; coverage: initial publish with full field set (window, source, confidence, note, audit), supersede chain (first + second publish, history newest-first, older row's superseded_by_id points to new row, is_current flag), validation matrix (missing start/end, end < start, span > 168h, bad source enum, confidence out of range, confidence non-numeric, note > 1000), staff gates (publishForTicket requires tickets.manage, publishForWorkorder requires workorders.manage, listHistoryForTicketStaff requires tickets.view), WO publish resolves company via customer bridge + rejects WO with no company binding, cancelCurrent supersedes without successor + retains history + audits reason + rejects when no current exists, portal three-layer scope (cross-company ticket reject, allowed_site_ids whitelist allows whitelisted sites + rejects out-of-whitelist, revoked account blocked, WO via customer.company_id bridge + cross-company WO reject), unknown/non-positive ids reject before repo writes, concurrent supersede detection (mimics two staff racing — manual UPDATE to supersede row between read and write triggers RuntimeException 'concurrent' on the loser), empty-history portal read, findCurrentForEntity returns only unsuperseded (third publish is current, cancel makes current null))_
- [x] White-label theming per major account _(6.8 — one new migration (127_portal_themes.sql) adds a portal_themes table with UNIQUE company_id as the enforcement point so admin upsert is idempotent (no dangling "draft" rows), plus UNIQUE custom_subdomain NULL and UNIQUE custom_domain NULL so two companies can never claim the same branded URL; subdomain slug validated to [a-z0-9-]{3,63} with no leading/trailing dash so we stay within DNS label rules (e.g. "acme" yields acme.portal.example.com), custom_domain is a fully-qualified host for vanity URLs like portal.acme.com (tenant owns the CNAME + cert — app just uses the Host header to figure out whose branding to serve); asset fields (logo_url / favicon_url / email_logo_url) are URLs not file bytes, so admin uploads via Phase 6.6's portal upload infra (or a CDN the tenant already has) and pastes the resulting URL — avoids growing the theme table unbounded with blob columns; color palette is five CHAR(7) columns (primary / secondary / accent / background / text) with strict #RRGGBB validation (three-digit #RGB is rejected to keep the palette unambiguous and to prevent mixing "short" and "long" hex in the CSS variable output); email override fields (email_from_name / email_from_address / email_reply_to) store per-tenant From/Reply-To for future NotificationService substitution on portal-originated email — this migration just stores the strings and admin UI binds them, but wiring the mailer to consume them is deferred until email template rendering moves through a tenant-aware path; support fields (support_phone / support_email / support_url) let tenants override default help-desk contacts so portal "Contact us" renders the tenant's actual phone/email rather than the platform's; footer_text is 500 chars for copyright/disclaimer strings; is_active toggle so admins can draft a theme and flip it live without deleting — and resolveByHost deliberately filters is_active=1 so inactive themes fall through to the platform default at the Host-lookup layer, not at the serialization layer; DELIBERATELY NO custom_css column — color palette + logos covers the 95% UX and avoids the XSS / CSS-parser-injection surface that arbitrary tenant-supplied CSS would introduce (tenants can't break out of the app shell, can't exfil data via CSS selectors, can't inject url(javascript:...) patterns); FK install (fk_portal_themes_company → companies ON DELETE CASCADE) is idempotent via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running the migration on a patched schema is a no-op rather than an error; ON DELETE CASCADE so purging a company wipes its theme row instead of dangling a broken company_id reference; PortalTheme model mirrors the table 1:1; PortalThemeRepository has findByCompany (primary upsert target), findBySubdomain (is_active=1 filter baked in), findByDomain (is_active=1 filter baked in), upsert (SELECT-by-company then INSERT-or-UPDATE so PUT doubles as create+update without requiring a separate POST endpoint), deleteByCompany — upsert is implemented as "find row by company_id, insert if missing else update by id" rather than INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE so it works the same on SQLite (tests) and MySQL (prod) without DB-specific SQL; PortalThemeService has three surfaces in one class — staff write (upsertTheme + deleteTheme + readForCompanyStaff), portal self-read (readForPortal), and public pre-auth (resolveByHost + publicResolveOrDefault); staff write gates on users.create (same authority required to provision portal accounts — if you can create portal users you can brand the portal they log into), staff read gates on users.view; PDOException with SQLSTATE 23000 (or message containing "duplicate"/"UNIQUE constraint") on upsert is caught and re-thrown as InvalidArgumentException 'custom_subdomain or custom_domain is already claimed by another company' so UNIQUE violations from a collision with another tenant's slug/domain surface as a legible 400 rather than a 500; readForPortal wraps findByCompany(account.company_id) with assertUsable(account) — portal users literally cannot ask for another company's theme because the only lookup key is account.company_id; readForPortal falls back to a platform default payload (is_default=true, generic "Customer Portal" display_name, all nulls) when no theme row exists so the frontend never has to branch on null — every GET returns a valid theme shape; resolveByHost is the pre-auth public path used by the login page — it strips ":port" suffix, tries exact FQDN match on custom_domain first (vanity domain wins over subdomain because a company with both means the vanity is more specific), then falls back to leftmost-label subdomain lookup only when the host has ≥2 dots (so bare "localhost" or "acme.localhost" can't be mistaken for a tenant slug — dev environments don't accidentally claim a slug), applies the same [a-z0-9-] regex before the DB hit to short-circuit obvious junk without a query; publicResolveOrDefault is the controller-facing wrapper that always returns a payload (resolveByHost || default) so the login page gets a consistent shape whether it's hitting a branded host or the catch-all; URL validation is strict — http/https only via regex before filter_var (blocks javascript:, data:, file: and other schemes that could render as <img src=> or background-image: in the portal shell), 512 char cap, filter_var(FILTER_VALIDATE_URL); domain validation uses filter_var(FILTER_VALIDATE_DOMAIN, FILTER_FLAG_HOSTNAME) which enforces DNS label rules (no leading/trailing dashes in labels) AND requires at least one dot so "localhost" is rejected as a bindable vanity domain; subdomain is force-lowercased before storage so "ACME-Corp" and "acme-corp" collide at the UNIQUE constraint rather than both succeeding and rendering inconsistently; email fields validated via FILTER_VALIDATE_EMAIL with 255 char cap; display_name required + 120 char cap; footer_text 500 char cap; support_phone 40 char cap; is_active coerces from any truthy input so admin UIs sending "1"/"true"/1 all work; audit events portal.theme.upserted + portal.theme.deleted carry company_id + has_subdomain + has_domain + has_logo + is_active — deliberately NOT logging the color hex values or URLs (not PII but also not particularly auditable — the row itself is the source of truth for "what did the theme look like on date X", an after-the-fact audit question would query the theme table with as-of timestamps once we add history); PortalThemeController is a thin HTTP facade (5 methods — upsertForCompany, getForCompany, deleteForCompany for admin, readForPortal for portal, publicResolveByHost for pre-auth); 6 new routes — public: GET /api/portal/theme (throttle-gated via Middleware::throttleStrict(10,60) in the same public group as /api/portal/auth/login so host-enumeration is rate-limited, always returns a payload), portal-scoped: GET /api/portal/theme/me (portalAuth-gated, returns the portal user's company theme or default), admin: GET /api/companies/{id}/portal-theme + PUT /api/companies/{id}/portal-theme + DELETE /api/companies/{id}/portal-theme (Middleware::auth() + users.create / users.view gates inside service); public GET /api/portal/theme uses Request.header('Host') directly — verified Request.php:179 normalizes header names so this works across dev/prod SAPIs (Apache, nginx-fpm, built-in PHP server); 33-case behavior test (PortalThemeServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with ThemePermissiveGate subclass + ThemeFakeCompanies + an anonymous PortalAuthService subclass — test gates on PDO::getAvailableDrivers('sqlite') and prints a clean skip in envs without pdo_sqlite; coverage: upsert creates initial row + audits, upsert is idempotent (same id on second call, only 1 row exists after two upserts, verified via direct COUNT(*)), display_name required + length cap, hex color validation (malformed, 3-digit short-form, wrong chars), subdomain validation (leading dash, too short, invalid chars, case-folded to lowercase), domain validation (bare hostname rejected, mixed-case lowercased), URL scheme validation (javascript: rejected, data: rejected), email validation, length caps on display_name + footer_text, unknown company + non-positive company_id rejected, users.create gate, UNIQUE conflicts on subdomain AND domain caught as InvalidArgumentException with "already claimed" message (not a 500), resolveByHost by exact domain + port-stripping + subdomain slug (≥2 dots), one-dot host not treated as slug, null/empty host returns null, inactive themes skipped at both domain and subdomain layers, publicResolveOrDefault falls back to platform default when no match, portal read uses account.company_id + falls back to default when no theme, portal read rejects unusable account, delete removes row + audits + rejects when no theme configured + users.create gate on delete, staff read null-when-absent + users.view gate)_

### Phase 7 — Fleet Maintenance
- [x] `fleet_units` + odometer/hours/VIN + assignment history _(7.1 — one new migration (128_fleet_units.sql) lays down three tables that together cover the fleet module's read/write shape: fleet_units for identity + denormalized current meter cache, fleet_unit_readings as an append-only meter history, and fleet_unit_assignments as an append-only driver/site/customer assignment history; fleet_units columns: company_id (tenancy anchor — reads + list filter scope through it), unit_number VARCHAR(40) with UNIQUE (company_id, unit_number) so two tenants can both have "T-101" but a single tenant can't duplicate, unit_type enum-via-VARCHAR(32) (truck/van/trailer/equipment/other — stored as string for schema-level readability rather than ENUM to make future additions a data migration not a DDL change), vin VARCHAR(30) NULL UNIQUE so dupe VINs fail at the DB layer AND across companies (a single physical vehicle can't be claimed by two tenants), year SMALLINT, make/model/trim/license_plate (all nullable because trailers + equipment often lack some), home_site_id nullable FK ON DELETE SET NULL to crm_sites (retires the FK on site deletion without cascading the unit), meter_type enum odometer/engine_hours/both, current_odometer INT + current_engine_hours DECIMAL(10,2) + odometer_last_read_at + engine_hours_last_read_at (denormalized cache bumped by updateMeterCache after each reading insert so list views don't have to correlate-subquery the readings table on every row), status enum active/out_of_service/retired, notes TEXT, created_by_user_id + created_at + updated_at ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP; three indexes — (company_id, status) for per-tenant filter reads, (home_site_id) for site-based fleet rollups; fleet_unit_readings is a pure append-only log (reading_type enum odometer/engine_hours, value DECIMAL(12,2) so engine hours can carry fractional precision and odometers store as whole ints from the service layer, recorded_at DATETIME captured separately from created_at so "when the tech read the meter" stays distinct from "when we recorded it in the app", source enum manual/workorder/import/telematics so future integrations can distinguish provenance, workorder_id nullable FK for readings taken during service work, notes + recorded_by_user_id + created_at); no update/delete methods on FleetUnitReadingRepository — corrections are done by recording a superseding reading (matches how telematics systems reconcile bad reads, and keeps the chain auditable without soft-delete noise); fleet_unit_assignments is also append-only (assignment_type enum driver/site/customer_rental/shop, assigned_user_id + assigned_site_id + customer_id all nullable with FK ON DELETE SET NULL so purging a user/site/customer doesn't break history, assigned_from DATETIME required + assigned_until DATETIME NULL — "current" is defined as assigned_until IS NULL and the service layer enforces at most one current per unit by transactionally closing the prior before opening the new); 6 idempotent FKs installed via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running the migration is a no-op rather than an error (fk_fleet_units_company CASCADE, fk_fleet_units_home_site SET NULL, fk_fleet_readings_unit CASCADE, fk_fleet_assign_unit CASCADE, fk_fleet_assign_site SET NULL, fk_fleet_assign_customer SET NULL);
