three models (FleetUnit, FleetUnitReading, FleetUnitAssignment) each carry enum class constants (TYPE_*, METER_*, STATUS_*, SOURCE_*) and ALLOWED_* arrays the service consumes for validation — no free-form enum strings anywhere in the service layer; three repositories, each with private COLUMNS const + hydrate(row) pattern so add/remove of columns touches one place: FleetUnitRepository.create/findById/findByVin/listForCompany (filters: status, home_site_id, query LIKE across unit_number+vin+license_plate+make+model, limit ≤500, offset) returns {data, total} with count+list in two prepared statements against the same WHERE, whole-row update (callers pass the full patch, so UNIQUE validation collapses into one INSERT/UPDATE attempt the service catches), updateMeterCache with COALESCE-based partial update so odometer-reading path bumps only odometer fields and engine-hours-reading path bumps only engine-hours fields through one SQL method; FleetUnitReadingRepository.create/findById/findLatestForUnit (ORDER BY recorded_at DESC, id DESC so same-timestamp reads disambiguate by insertion order)/listForUnit (optional readingType filter, limit ≤500); FleetUnitAssignmentRepository.create/findById/findCurrentForUnit (WHERE assigned_until IS NULL, ORDER BY assigned_from DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1)/listForUnit/closeCurrent which UPDATEs WHERE assigned_until IS NULL and returns rowCount > 0 so concurrent-close races are detectable at the service layer (two staff racing would both read "current" but only one succeeds; the loser throws); FleetUnitService is the single orchestration layer with 8 public methods spanning three surfaces — Unit CRUD (createUnit + updateUnit + retireUnit + getUnit + listForCompany), Readings (recordReading + listReadings), Assignments (assignUnit + endAssignment + listAssignments); gates via AccessGate.assert — fleet.view for reads, fleet.manage for writes, added to admin (via '*'), manager (full), technician (fleet.view + fleet.manage so techs can log readings when servicing units), and dispatcher (fleet.view for route planning context); createUnit catches PDOException SQLSTATE 23000 (also message contains "duplicate" or "UNIQUE constraint" for SQLite test compat) and rethrows as InvalidArgumentException 'unit_number or vin is already in use' so UNIQUE violations surface as legible 400s rather than 500s; updateUnit does merge-with-existing-then-validate so callers can send a partial PUT body without nulling fields they didn't pass (standard PHP-ish partial PUT shape); retireUnit is soft (flips status to retired — never hard-deletes because readings + assignments carry business history) AND closes the current assignment in the same call so the history doesn't carry "currently-assigned retired unit" forever; retireUnit is idempotent (re-calling on an already-retired unit is a no-op + no duplicate audit); recordReading enforces a monotonic invariant — if new value < latest reading for the same reading_type, reject with InvalidArgumentException listing old vs new formatted values unless caller passes allow_decrease=true in input (the override makes meter swaps/rollovers an informed decision rather than a fat-finger; the allow_decrease flag is echoed into the audit context so auditors can trace who overrode and when); recordReading wraps the reading insert + updateMeterCache in a transaction so a partial failure doesn't leave the cache out of sync with the history, and routes the cache bump through the reading_type-aware COALESCE path so engine-hours readings don't clobber odometer state; assignUnit transactionally calls closeCurrent($unitId, $new_assigned_from) then creates the new row so history stays contiguous — if user assigns a new driver without explicitly ending the prior assignment, the prior is automatically stamped closed at the new assignment's start time (no "everyone was driver for 12 hours" phantom overlap); validateAssignmentInput enforces assignment_type pins which target must be present (driver→assigned_user_id, site→assigned_site_id, customer_rental→customer_id) so callers can't create ambiguous "customer_rental with no customer" rows, AND validates that assigned_site_id + customer_id both resolve to the same company_id as the fleet unit (cross-company reference leak rejection — site/customer belonging to another tenant throws 'belongs to a different company'); VIN validation uses standard spec regex [A-HJ-NPR-Z0-9]+ (excludes I/O/Q letters per VIN spec because they're easily confused with 1/0/Q), uppercased before storage, max length 17 but doesn't insist on exactly 17 because pre-1981 vehicles legitimately have shorter VINs; year range 1900-2100, unit_number ≤40 chars, notes ≤500 chars, all enums validated against their model ALLOWED_* arrays; endAssignment throws InvalidArgumentException 'no active assignment' if there isn't one AND throws RuntimeException 'concurrent assignment close detected' if closeCurrent returns rowCount=0 (race with another staff closing between our findCurrent and our UPDATE) so the caller can refresh + retry rather than silently noop; audit events fleet.unit.created/updated/retired, fleet.reading.recorded, fleet.assignment.opened/ended all carry company_id + fleet_unit_id + relevant context (unit_number, assignment_type, reading_type, value, allow_decrease, etc.) — never PII; FleetUnitController is a thin HTTP facade (one-liner per method, 10 methods mirroring the service surface); new routes module routes/modules/fleet.php registered in routes/api.php module loader — 10 staff-auth routes (all Middleware::auth(), gate inside service): GET /api/fleet/units?company_id=X (list with filters), POST /api/fleet/units (body carries company_id since fleet is per-tenant and there's no platform-wide view), GET /api/fleet/units/{id} returns {unit, current_assignment} so the primary detail page loads in one request, PUT /api/fleet/units/{id}, DELETE /api/fleet/units/{id} (soft-retire), GET+POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/readings (list accepts reading_type + limit query params), GET+POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/assignments, POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/assignments/end; all list endpoints reject missing company_id query param at the HTTP boundary with Response::badRequest rather than letting the service throw — cheaper failure mode for the common "forgot the filter" mistake; 31-case behavior test (FleetUnitServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with FleetPermissiveGate subclass + FleetFakeCompanies/FleetFakeSites/FleetFakeCustomers + real FleetUnit*Repository instances against the in-memory schema so the transactional paths (recordReading commit/rollback + assignUnit close-then-open) are exercised end-to-end; coverage: createUnit default-fill (type=truck, meter=odometer, status=active) + audit emission, VIN lowercase-to-uppercase normalization, VIN with I letter rejected, VIN >17 chars rejected, year < 1900 rejected, unknown enum values (unit_type, meter_type, status) rejected, UNIQUE (company_id, unit_number) conflict caught as InvalidArgumentException, same unit_number allowed across different companies, duplicate VIN across companies rejected (single physical vehicle can't double-claim), cross-company home_site_id rejected, unknown company_id rejected, updateUnit partial patch preserves unchanged fields, retireUnit flips status + closes current assignment + audits + is idempotent, listForCompany pagination + query filter, requireUnit zero/negative/missing id rejection, recordReading inserts + bumps odometer cache + leaves engine-hours untouched, engine_hours reading bumps only engine cache, monotonic rejection with old-vs-new values in message, allow_decrease override accepted + flag echoed into audit context, missing value/recorded_at/reading_type rejected, negative value rejected, listReadings returns newest-first, assignUnit opens first assignment with is_current=true, second assignUnit closes prior atomically (prior row's assigned_until stamped at new assignment's assigned_from, is_current flips correctly, distinct ids), assignment_type pins target field (driver needs user, site needs site, rental needs customer), cross-company site/customer rejection, same-company customer rental accepted, endAssignment stamps current row + no-active rejection, fleet.manage denial blocks writes, fleet.view denial blocks reads)
- [x] Fleet-specific PM inheritance _(7.2 — one new migration (129_pm_fleet_bindings.sql) that extends the Phase 5.1 PM infrastructure with fleet-aware scoping without touching the existing pm_plans / pm_schedules surface: creates pm_fleet_bindings (id, plan_id FK CASCADE to pm_plans, company_id, scope_type VARCHAR(16) — 'unit' or 'unit_type', fleet_unit_id FK CASCADE to fleet_units when scope='unit', unit_type VARCHAR(32) when scope='unit_type', frequency_kind VARCHAR(30) DEFAULT 'meter' + frequency_config JSON reusing the Phase 5.2 shape so meter/calendar/fixed_interval/condition all just work, lead_time_days, starts_at DATE, is_active TINYINT, created_by_user_id, created_at, updated_at ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP) with four indexes — (company_id, scope_type, is_active) for the hot list path, (fleet_unit_id), (unit_type), (plan_id) for reverse-lookup when a plan is edited; AND adds fleet_unit_id INT UNSIGNED NULL column + idx_pm_schedules_fleet_unit index + fk_pm_schedules_fleet_unit CASCADE to fleet_units on pm_schedules via idempotent information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE guards so re-runs are no-ops (all three new FKs — fk_pm_fleet_bindings_plan, fk_pm_fleet_bindings_unit, fk_pm_schedules_fleet_unit — install idempotently and skip gracefully if fleet_units doesn't exist yet on pre-Phase-7.1 databases); crucial schema decision: fleet_unit_id gets its OWN column on pm_schedules rather than overloading asset_id (keeps fleet + site_asset scopes orthogonal, makes migration auditable, and means PmGeneratorService's existing site/asset codepath stays untouched — fleet schedules carry asset_id=NULL and occupy their own scope slot); PmFleetBinding model with SCOPE_UNIT / SCOPE_UNIT_TYPE constants + ALLOWED_SCOPES array consumed by the service for enum validation; PmFleetBindingRepository is a flat CRUD surface (COLUMNS const + hydrate()) because service-layer validation owns scope_type-pin invariants (unit scope must have fleet_unit_id, unit_type scope must have unit_type) — the key query is listApplicable(companyId, unitId, unitType) which returns bindings in precedence order 'ORDER BY (scope_type = :unit_scope) DESC, id ASC' so unit-specific bindings sort first then unit_type defaults second within the same result set (single SQL statement, no UNION); update() deliberately excludes plan_id/scope_type/fleet_unit_id/unit_type/company_id from the writable set so binding identity can't mutate — callers must delete+recreate to change scope (prevents "accidentally rescoping an active binding and orphaning the schedules it spawned"); PmFleetInheritanceService is the orchestration layer gating binding CRUD behind pm.manage / pm.view (reuses existing pm permissions so managers who already control PM plans also control fleet inheritance — no new permission surface to administer), validates scope coherence eagerly (unit scope requires fleet_unit_id, unit_type scope requires unit_type, scope enum validated against PmFleetBinding::ALLOWED_SCOPES), validates frequency_config shape per frequency_kind BEFORE writing so a bad meter config can't be persisted and break the hook later (meter requires interval_units>0 AND unit in miles/km/hours, fixed_interval requires one of interval_days/weeks/months/years > 0, calendar requires day_of_month|day_of_week|months_of_year), rejects cross-company (plan.company_id NOT NULL AND != binding.company_id throws, fleet_unit.company_id != binding.company_id throws), PDOException on create rewrapped as RuntimeException with underlying message preserved; two hook entry points that FleetUnitService calls: ensureSchedulesForUnit($actor, $unit) invoked after createUnit, walks listApplicable($unit->company_id, $unit->id, $unit->unit_type), pre-loads existing pm_schedules.search({fleet_unit_id, company_id}) into a plans-already-bound map keyed by plan_id, iterates bindings (already sorted unit-first by the SQL ORDER BY) and for each plan_id the FIRST binding hit wins — skipping any later unit_type default for the same plan so unit-specific binding wins inheritance precedence without duplicating rows; for each surviving binding calls PmFrequencyService::initialNextDue() then PmScheduleRepository::create() with fleet_unit_id set + frequency_config copied from the binding + status='active' + audit entry pm.fleet_binding.materialized; retired-unit short-circuit (no spawning schedules for a unit that's out of service) + idempotent on repeated calls (plans-already-bound check means second invocation spawns zero schedules); onReadingRecorded($unit, $reading) invoked after recordReading commits, short-circuits retired units (so a legacy reading backfilled into a retired unit can't resurrect a stale schedule), queries pm_schedules for this unit (active status + company_id filter), for each meter-kind schedule matches reading_type to frequency_config.unit via meterMatches (odometer advances miles|km schedules, engine_hours advances hours schedules, mismatched combos are no-ops so a truck with both meters doesn't accidentally trigger the wrong kind of service), delegates to Phase 5.2's PmFrequencyService::advanceForReading which mutates $schedule->frequency_config.baseline_reading in place (whole-multiples-of-interval so a single big reading jump still advances the right number of ticks), when advance returns a non-null date persists both next_due_at + the mutated frequency_config through PmScheduleRepository::update + emits pm.fleet.meter_triggered audit with reading_id + next_due_at for traceability; PmSchedule model gains `public ?int $fleet_unit_id = null;` property + PmScheduleRepository COLUMNS const includes fleet_unit_id + search() filter + create() INSERT + update() writable list all accept it so the generator cron (Phase 5.3) + compliance report (Phase 5.4) transparently handle fleet schedules alongside site/asset schedules; FleetUnitService constructor gains optional `?PmFleetInheritanceService $pmInheritance = null` dependency so tests + early deploys that don't wire PM still work — the hook calls are wrapped in try/catch(Throwable) and swallowed so a PM config glitch can't block unit creation or a valid meter reading from committing (the core fleet write has already committed before the hook fires; PM inheritance is best-effort observability, not a transactional dependency); PmFleetBindingController is a thin HTTP facade (create/update/delete/listForCompany mirroring the service surface, one-line delegation each); four new routes added to routes/modules/fleet.php (same auth group as Phase 7.1 fleet routes, namespaced under /api/pm/fleet-bindings since these records bind PM plans to fleet targets not fleet units themselves): GET /api/pm/fleet-bindings?company_id=X[&active_only=1], POST /api/pm/fleet-bindings, PUT /api/pm/fleet-bindings/{id}, DELETE /api/pm/fleet-bindings/{id}; DI wiring in routes/modules/fleet.php constructs PmFleetBindingRepository + PmScheduleRepository + PmPlanRepository + PmFrequencyService + PmFleetInheritanceService and injects the inheritance service into FleetUnitService so fleet CRUD and PM inheritance share the same request-scoped instances (no duplicate connections, consistent audit trail); 21-case behavior test (PmFleetInheritanceServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with PmFleetInMemoryConnection + PmFleetPermissiveGate + PmFleetFakeAudit + real Repository instances against in-memory schema for fleet_units/pm_plans/pm_schedules/pm_fleet_bindings — covers createBinding unit_type-scope happy path, scope-coherence rules (unit scope requires fleet_unit_id, unit_type scope requires unit_type), cross-company fleet_unit + cross-company plan rejection, meter frequency_config validation (missing interval_units, missing unit), pm.manage denial blocks create, updateBinding immutability of scope identity columns (scope_type/unit_type/fleet_unit_id can't change, frequency_config/lead_time_days/is_active can), deleteBinding removes row + emits pm.fleet_binding.deleted audit, listBindingsForCompany returns both scope types, ensureSchedulesForUnit materializes one schedule per binding, idempotent across repeated calls (second call spawns zero), unit-specific binding wins over unit_type default for the same plan (no duplicate, interval_units matches the unit-specific value), retired unit short-circuits to empty list, inactive binding short-circuits, onReadingRecorded odometer-trigger advances next_due_at to reading date + bumps baseline_reading by one interval, no-advance when threshold not crossed, engine-hours schedule matches engine_hours reading but NOT odometer reading (meterMatches isolation), retired-unit onReadingRecorded no-op even with past-threshold value, pm.view denial blocks list, pm.manage denial blocks delete; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green)
- [x] Downtime + out-of-service tracking _(7.3 — one new migration (130_fleet_unit_downtime.sql) lays down a single table fleet_unit_downtime that works as an append-only history of every time a unit goes out of service: id INT UNSIGNED AUTO_INCREMENT PK, fleet_unit_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL (the tenancy anchor flows transitively through fleet_units.company_id — same pattern as readings/assignments), reason VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'breakdown' stored as string not ENUM so future reasons are a data migration not DDL, started_at DATETIME NOT NULL (captured separately from created_at so "when the breakdown happened" stays distinct from "when we recorded it in the app"), ended_at DATETIME NULL — the NULL-means-open convention drives the at-most-one-open-window invariant, notes VARCHAR(1000) NULL for free-form description, started_by_user_id + ended_by_user_id INT UNSIGNED (ended nullable until close) for attribution, created_at + updated_at ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP; three indexes — (fleet_unit_id, ended_at) for the hot findOpenForUnit query (ended_at IS NULL scan), (fleet_unit_id, started_at) for chronological history list, (reason) for future "downtime by cause" rollups; one idempotent FK fk_fleet_downtime_unit CASCADE to fleet_units via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-runs no-op; FleetUnitDowntime model with REASON_BREAKDOWN/SCHEDULED/ACCIDENT/INSPECTION/OTHER constants + ALLOWED_REASONS array the service consumes for enum validation (same pattern as Phase 7.1 models — no free-form enum strings reach the SQL layer), NOTES_MAX_LEN=1000 constant so the cap lives alongside the column def; FleetUnitDowntimeRepository follows the COLUMNS/hydrate/CURRENT_TIMESTAMP pattern with five targeted methods: create() INSERT with all fields + CURRENT_TIMESTAMP for created_at/updated_at, findById() single-row read, findOpenForUnit(unitId) ORDER BY started_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1 (same-timestamp disambiguation by id), countOpenForUnit(unitId) SELECT COUNT(*) for the status-flip decision, listForUnit(unitId, limit<=500) newest-first, appendNotes(id, ?notes) targeted UPDATE that overwrites the notes column with bumped updated_at (named "append" semantically because the service pre-computes the merged string, but SQL-wise it's a plain UPDATE that keeps logic out of the query layer), closeOpenForUnit(unitId, endedAt, endedByUserId) UPDATEs WHERE ended_at IS NULL and returns stmt->rowCount() so the service can detect concurrent-close races — parallels the assignment closeCurrent pattern from Phase 7.1; FleetUnitDowntimeService is the single orchestration layer gating reads with fleet.view and writes with fleet.manage (reusing Phase 7.1 permission surface — a manager who can edit a unit can take it out of service, no new permission to administer); four public methods: startDowntime rejects retired units eagerly with "cannot start downtime on a retired unit — un-retire first" (retired units have no operational window to pull offline — forcing an explicit un-retire instead of silently flipping back to out_of_service prevents a confusing "why did my retired truck show as broken down" state), validates reason against ALLOWED_REASONS defaulting to breakdown when omitted, normalizes started_at via DateTimeImmutable or defaults to now, caps notes at NOTES_MAX_LEN, wraps the writes in a transaction that FIRST closes any prior-open window at the new start time (via closeOpenForUnit) so repeated starts without explicit ends don't leave phantom overlap — the prior window stamps ended_at at the new window's started_at, giving contiguous history — THEN creates the new row, THEN flips fleet_units.status to out_of_service via new FleetUnitRepository::setStatus targeted setter BUT only when the current status isn't already out_of_service (avoids an unnecessary UPDATE + audit flap on the common repeated-start path); audit pm.downtime.started with company_id + fleet_unit_id + reason + started_at; endDowntime requires exactly one open window — throws InvalidArgumentException "no open downtime on this unit" when findOpenForUnit returns null so the UI gets a legible 400 rather than a silent no-op; resolves ended_at from input (normalized via DateTimeImmutable or defaults to now) and validates ended_at >= started_at via strcmp (the started_at from the open row, not from the input — prevents a user from rewriting history with a backwards close) with "ended_at must be >= started_at" rejection; merges close-out notes into start-time notes with "\n" separator and truncates from the TAIL keeping freshest N bytes when over NOTES_MAX_LEN (close-out notes usually supersede start-time notes anyway — we'd rather lose context from the original "noticed transmission slipping" than lose the "replaced transmission assembly, road-tested 20mi" that tells us what happened); wraps closeOpenForUnit + optional appendNotes + setStatus in a transaction; closeOpenForUnit returning rowCount=0 throws RuntimeException "concurrent downtime close detected on unit {id}" so two techs racing to end the same window both don't quietly succeed — the loser gets a refresh-and-retry signal; flips status back to active via setStatus only when countOpenForUnit=0 AND current status === out_of_service — the AND-clause is load-bearing: a unit manually flipped to retired between our read and the close (or flipped via updateUnit's status field) stays retired (retired wins precedence), and a unit that's somehow in active state already doesn't get a redundant UPDATE; audit fleet.downtime.ended with ended_at for duration traceability; listDowntime and currentDowntime are read-only (gate fleet.view), require unit exists, return serialized rows — serialize() emits is_open (computed from ended_at IS NULL) + duration_minutes (computed from started_at to ended_at or now via DateTimeImmutable arithmetic, returns 0 on negative delta to handle clock skew gracefully) so the React side doesn't have to recompute downtime metrics client-side; FleetUnitRepository gains setStatus(id, status) targeted UPDATE that only touches the status column + updated_at — rather than reusing the full-row update() which re-validates VIN/unit_number UNIQUE on every status flip (would turn a simple "take unit offline" into a unique-constraint retry loop if another unit happened to share a VIN during import reconciliation); FleetUnitDowntimeController is a thin HTTP facade with four one-liner methods (start/end/listForUnit/current); four new routes in routes/modules/fleet.php under the same staff-auth group as Phase 7.1 routes: GET /api/fleet/units/{id}/downtime?limit=N (history list), GET /api/fleet/units/{id}/downtime/current (open window or null for detail views), POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/downtime/start (body: reason, started_at optional, notes optional), POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/downtime/end (body: ended_at optional, notes optional — appends to start-time notes); DI wiring constructs FleetUnitDowntimeRepository + FleetUnitDowntimeService + FleetUnitDowntimeController alongside the Phase 7.1/7.2 wiring so all three sub-phases share the same request-scoped FleetUnitRepository instance (no duplicate connections or inconsistent status reads across the start-downtime/record-reading/end-downtime sequence); 18-case behavior test (FleetUnitDowntimeServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with DowntimeInMemoryConnection + DowntimePermissiveGate + DowntimeFakeAudit + real Repository instances against the in-memory schema for fleet_units/fleet_unit_downtime so the transactional paths exercise end-to-end: startDowntime opens a window + flips status to out_of_service, default reason fills to breakdown when omitted, unknown reason rejected against ALLOWED_REASONS, retired-unit startDowntime rejected with un-retire message, repeated startDowntime transactionally closes the prior at the new start time so countOpenForUnit stays at 1, startDowntime emits fleet.downtime.started audit with context, endDowntime stamps ended_at + flips status back to active + duration_minutes computed correctly across multi-hour spans (390 for 6.5h), endDowntime rejected when no open window, endDowntime rejects ended_at before started_at, endDowntime on retired unit leaves status retired (retired-wins), close-out notes merge with start-time notes via \n, listDowntime returns newest-first, currentDowntime returns open-or-null correctly, fleet.manage denial blocks start + end, fleet.view denial blocks list, unknown unit id rejected, notes-over-cap rejected; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green)
- [x] Cost-per-mile/hour reports _(7.4 — one new migration (131_workorders_fleet_unit_id.sql) adds fleet_unit_id INT UNSIGNED NULL + idx_workorder_fleet_unit (fleet_unit_id, completed_at) composite index + fk_workorder_fleet_unit ON DELETE SET NULL to workorders via idempotent information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE (FK gated on fleet_units table existence so pre-Phase-7.1 databases don't fail); nullable column so non-fleet workorders (walk-in customers, one-off repairs, warranty claims) keep working unchanged — only fleet-tracked work populates the link; SET NULL on delete rather than CASCADE because workorder history is financial/legal record that must survive fleet unit retirement (purging a retired truck shouldn't erase its $40k of maintenance history); FleetCostReportRepository is a dedicated read-only aggregate layer (not slotted into FleetUnitRepository or WorkorderRepository because the queries bridge both tables and don't match either side's CRUD shape) exposing three methods: aggregateCostsForCompany(companyId, from, to) — SELECT with INNER JOIN to fleet_units for tenancy filter, filters to completed workorders (completed_at IS NOT NULL, fleet_unit_id IS NOT NULL, completed_at BETWEEN :from AND :to), computes labor_cost and parts_cost via correlated subqueries over workorder_items JOIN workorder_jobs (one subquery per workorder row) with UPPER(wi.type) = 'LABOR'/'PART' for case-insensitive matching across the UPPERCASE-dominated workorder pipeline and any legacy lowercase data from estimate bridge, GROUP BY w.id, w.fleet_unit_id keeps subquery correlation per-workorder then PHP folds the rows to per-unit so COUNT(DISTINCT w.id) stays accurate even when subqueries wouldn't SUM correctly in a flat GROUP BY; completed_at (not created_at) is the filter dimension because cost reports answer "what settled in Q3" not "what opened in Q3" — in-progress workorders intentionally excluded so the report reflects finished cost; readingDeltasForCompany(companyId, readingType, from, to) — SELECT ORDER BY fleet_unit_id ASC, recorded_at ASC, id ASC with PHP fold to {fleet_unit_id, first_value, first_at, last_value, last_at} since every subsequent row is later-or-equal the last_* slots get unconditional overwrite and the ORDER BY id ASC disambiguates same-timestamp readings; recorded_at (not created_at) is the sort key so backfilled readings land in the correct report window; fetchUnitIdentities(companyId, ids) — IN-list fetch for display metadata (unit_number, unit_type, status) joined onto report rows without pulling full FleetUnit models into the service layer; FleetCostReportService is the orchestration + presentation layer gating both report surfaces on fleet.view (read-only — no write paths, same permission as unit/reading/downtime reads so fleet.view users see all cost data for units they can see); two public methods costPerMile() / costPerHour() both delegate to private buildReport(readingType, unitLabel) parameterized by reading type ('odometer'→'miles', 'engine_hours'→'hours') and unit label so the shape + math stay identical, only the SI label and dictionary keys change; normalizeRange validates from/to as parseable dates via DateTimeImmutable, rejects from > to ("from must be <= to"), rejects span over MAX_WINDOW_DAYS=5y+1 ("date window exceeds N days — split into smaller reports") to cap the correlated-subquery blowup risk, expands shorthand YYYY-MM-DD to full-day boundaries (00:00:00 on from, 23:59:59 on to) so "from=2026-03-01&to=2026-03-31" captures the full month without the caller managing time components; companyId <= 0 rejected up front; merges the two aggregate result sets by fleet_unit_id union (a unit appears in the report when it has workorder cost OR readings in the window, not AND — so a truck racking up miles with no shop visits shows up with cost_per_mile=null to flag "something's falling off the preventive-maintenance calendar", and a shop-queen truck bleeding cost with no mileage shows up with cost_per_mile=null to flag "why are we spending this if it isn't moving"); computes delta = last_value - first_value (clamped to 0 for negative — handles reading corrections gracefully rather than reporting negative miles); cost_per_unit = total_cost / delta rounded to 4 decimals when delta > 0, NULL when delta <= 0 (caller renders "—" rather than "∞" for the divide-by-zero case — a unit with 1 reading or 0 readings genuinely has unknown utilization, not zero utilization, so the NULL is semantically correct); each row carries fleet_unit_id + unit_number + unit_type + status + total_cost + labor_cost + parts_cost + miles|hours + cost_per_mile|cost_per_hour + workorder_count + first_reading{value,at} + last_reading{value,at} so the UI can show both the rollup and the meter-delta evidence the rollup is based on; rows sorted by total_cost DESC so the most expensive units float to the top — matches how fleet managers triage cost reports (deal with the $10k truck before the $200 van); retired units included in historical reports (don't filter them out — a retired truck still racked up cost while it was alive, and excluding it would make "why was Q1 so expensive" unanswerable after fleet churn); fleet-wide totals row aggregates total_cost + labor_cost + parts_cost + miles|hours + unit_count across all rows AND computes fleet-wide cost_per_unit = SUM(cost) / SUM(usage) rather than AVG(cost_per_unit) per row — weighted by usage so a unit that ran 100 miles with $500 cost doesn't drown out a unit that ran 10,000 miles with $10k cost; all monetary values rounded to 2 decimals, usage values to 2 decimals, cost_per_unit to 4 decimals (4dp since a cost_per_mile of $0.0423 is meaningfully different from $0.0438 for fleet budgeting); FleetCostReportController is a thin two-method HTTP facade (costPerMile/costPerHour, one-liner delegation each); two new routes under the same staff-auth group as the rest of Phase 7, namespaced under /api/fleet/reports/ (not /api/fleet/units/ since reports span the fleet rather than targeting a single unit): GET /api/fleet/reports/cost-per-mile?company_id=X&from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD, GET /api/fleet/reports/cost-per-hour?company_id=X&from=&to=; both endpoints validate company_id + from + to at the HTTP boundary with Response::badRequest ('company_id query param is required', 'from and to query params are required (YYYY-MM-DD)') before hitting the service — cheaper failure for the common "forgot the filter" mistake; DI wiring in routes/modules/fleet.php constructs FleetCostReportRepository + FleetCostReportService + FleetCostReportController alongside Phase 7.1/7.2/7.3 components so all four sub-phases share the request-scoped connection + gate + audit; 18-case behavior test (FleetCostReportServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with CostReportInMemoryConnection + CostReportPermissiveGate + seeded fleet_units/fleet_unit_readings/workorders/workorder_jobs/workorder_items tables + real FleetCostReportRepository against the in-memory schema; coverage: costPerMile aggregates total_cost + labor/parts split across multiple workorders, costPerHour uses engine_hours reading type, odometer-only readings produce hours=0 + cost_per_hour=null (meterMatches isolation — a truck with odometer readings but no engine-hour log can't be assessed for cost-per-hour), single reading in window produces miles=0 + cost_per_mile=null (divide-by-zero guard), retired unit still appears in historical report with status=retired echoed, cross-company tenant leak blocked (units belonging to company 20 excluded when querying company 10 — repository-level JOIN filter), date range boundaries inclusive full-day (reading at 23:59 on last day included, workorder at 00:00:01 next day excluded), empty range returns 0 rows + zero totals + null cost_per_mile (no NaN), in-progress workorder (completed_at IS NULL) excluded even when fleet_unit_id set, multiple units sorted by total_cost DESC so expensive units first, totals aggregate correctly across units with weighted fleet-wide cost_per_mile (not simple average), from > to rejected, invalid date string rejected, window over MAX_WINDOW_DAYS rejected, companyId=0 rejected, fleet.view denial blocks report (UnauthorizedException), walk-in workorder with fleet_unit_id=NULL excluded from the aggregate, zero-delta readings still preserve first/last_reading metadata in the row (so the UI can show "read 5000 on 3/5, read 5000 on 3/20 — no movement"); skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green)
- [x] External vendor repair logs _(7.5 — one new migration (132_fleet_external_repairs.sql) creates a single table fleet_external_repairs that captures repair work done by outside vendors (dealer service, tire shops, tow operators, body shops, mobile mechanics) alongside the existing internal workorder pipeline: id PK, fleet_unit_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL (tenancy flows transitively through fleet_units.company_id — same pattern as readings/assignments/downtime), vendor_name VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL (free-form string not a vendors FK because these are often one-off vendors — "Bob's Towing in Reno" — that don't need first-class vendor records), vendor_invoice_number VARCHAR(80) NULL for matching external invoices against the log entry during reconciliation, category VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'repair' stored as string not ENUM so future categories are a data migration not DDL, service_date DATE NOT NULL (DATE not DATETIME — external invoices typically only carry the day of service, so the column type reflects the actual data precision rather than fabricating a fake time component), description VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL (what work was done, required because a repair log without description is useless for future triage), labor_cost/parts_cost/other_cost/total_cost DECIMAL(12,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.00 with total being the summed dollar number the vendor actually billed (split is for reporting, total is what hit the books), odometer_at_service INT UNSIGNED NULL + engine_hours_at_service DECIMAL(10,2) NULL captured separately because external invoices commonly record the meter at service — these double as companion meter readings posted into fleet_unit_readings by the service layer when provided, notes TEXT NULL for longer free-form context, attachment_path VARCHAR(255) NULL for future scan/PDF upload (the path column exists now; Phase 9 filesystem work will wire uploads), created_by_user_id INT UNSIGNED, created_at/updated_at ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP; three indexes — (fleet_unit_id, service_date) for the hot per-unit chronological list, (vendor_name) for "show me everything Bob's Towing has done for us" rollups, (category) for future category-based cost splits; one idempotent FK fk_fleet_ext_repair_unit CASCADE to fleet_units via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-runs no-op AND gracefully skip on pre-Phase-7.1 databases where fleet_units doesn't exist yet; FleetExternalRepair model defines category constants CATEGORY_REPAIR/MAINTENANCE/TIRES/TOWING/PAINT/WARRANTY/INSPECTION/OTHER + ALLOWED_CATEGORIES array the service consumes for enum validation (same pattern as all prior Phase 7 models) and field-length constants VENDOR_NAME_MAX_LEN=120 / VENDOR_INVOICE_MAX_LEN=80 / DESCRIPTION_MAX_LEN=500 / NOTES_MAX_LEN=2000 / ATTACHMENT_PATH_MAX_LEN=255 that live alongside the column definitions so a schema change ripples to service validation in one place; FleetExternalRepairRepository follows the COLUMNS/hydrate/CURRENT_TIMESTAMP pattern with CRUD + query surface: create() INSERT with all fields + CURRENT_TIMESTAMP for timestamps, findById() single-row read, listForUnit(unitId, limit<=500) ORDER BY service_date DESC, id DESC for newest-first per-unit history, listForCompany(companyId, filters) with optional vendor_name LIKE (case-insensitive via UPPER + LIKE pattern) + category exact match + service_date from/to range + limit<=500 — uses INNER JOIN to fleet_units for tenancy anchor (external_repairs carries fleet_unit_id not company_id directly, so the JOIN is the tenancy filter and prevents cross-company data leaks), update() whole-patch so partial-PUT callers collapse into one UPDATE with the service-layer merge-before-validate pattern, delete() by id, aggregateCostsForCompany(companyId, fromDate, toDate) — the integration hook for Phase 7.4's cost report — returns one row per fleet_unit_id with repair_count + total_cost + labor_cost + parts_cost + other_cost, filters WHERE fe.service_date BETWEEN :from AND :to (DATE comparison not DATETIME since service_date is a DATE column), INNER JOIN to fleet_units for tenancy, GROUP BY fe.fleet_unit_id for the per-unit rollup the cost report service then unions with workorder-derived cost; FleetExternalRepairService is the single orchestration layer gating writes on fleet.manage and reads on fleet.view (reuses Phase 7.1 permissions — anyone who can edit a unit can log external work against it); seven public methods — createRepair + updateRepair + deleteRepair + listForUnit + listForCompany + getRepair; validateInput enforces: vendor_name trim + required + <=VENDOR_NAME_MAX_LEN, vendor_invoice_number optional + <=VENDOR_INVOICE_MAX_LEN, category optional defaulting to 'repair' + validated against ALLOWED_CATEGORIES, service_date required + parseable via DateTimeImmutable + normalized to Y-m-d (strips any accidental time component from client-side DateTimeInput widgets), description trim + required + <=DESCRIPTION_MAX_LEN, labor/parts/other >= 0 rejection, total auto-computed from split when omitted (saves the client from an easy-to-miss sum) BUT when total IS provided it's validated to equal labor+parts+other within $0.01 tolerance — the tolerance catches client-side rounding drift (e.g., splitting $100 evenly three ways at 2dp) while still rejecting actual mismatches that would silently skew the cost report, odometer_at_service >= 0 + int-cast, engine_hours_at_service >= 0 + float-cast, notes <=NOTES_MAX_LEN, attachment_path <=ATTACHMENT_PATH_MAX_LEN; requireUnit resolves fleet_unit_id to a unit + asserts matching company_id to prevent cross-company reference leaks ("create a repair on unit 42 under company 10" when unit 42 belongs to company 20 throws 'fleet unit belongs to a different company'); createRepair wraps the repair insert + recordReadingsFromRepair in a transaction so a partial failure (e.g., reading insert throwing) rolls back the repair too — but the reading recording is itself wrapped in try/catch(Throwable) inside recordReadingsFromRepair so transient reading failures (e.g., concurrent lock, meter monotonicity trip) do NOT block the repair write; the net effect is "best-effort companion reading, but transactionally clean if it succeeds"; recordReadingsFromRepair walks odometer_at_service + engine_hours_at_service and for each provided value: fetches findLatestForUnit({fleet_unit_id, reading_type}) + skips silently when new value <= latest (the monotonic-skip — external invoices commonly arrive out of order, e.g., a tow bill from two weeks ago shows up after last week's dealer service, and we'd rather drop the stale reading than reject the repair or mutate history), otherwise creates a fleet_unit_reading with source='import', recorded_at=service_date 00:00:00, notes='External repair #{id} — {vendor}', then calls FleetUnitRepository::updateMeterCache via the reading_type-conditional signature (odometer: (id, (int)value, null, stamp, null); engine_hours: (id, null, value, null, stamp)) so the cached current_* fields on fleet_units stay consistent with the readings log; updateRepair does merge-with-existing-then-validate for partial-PUT semantics (same pattern as Phase 7.1 updateUnit), re-runs requireUnit tenancy check if fleet_unit_id changes (rejecting reparent across company), does NOT re-record readings on update to avoid double-posting when a correction patches only description or notes — corrections to the meter value are modeled as superseding readings, not mutating the repair's reading trail; deleteRepair is idempotent (findById returning null is a no-op + no duplicate audit) — matches retireUnit's idempotent-delete stance and protects against double-click / retry storms from the client; audit fleet.external_repair.created/updated/deleted all carry company_id + fleet_unit_id + vendor + total_cost for provenance; getRepair/listForUnit/listForCompany all go through requireUnit for the per-unit endpoints and serialize() the model to array with numeric casts (labor_cost/parts_cost/other_cost/total_cost as floats, odometer_at_service as int-or-null, engine_hours_at_service as float-or-null); FleetExternalRepairController is a thin seven-method HTTP facade (create/update/delete/listForUnit/listForCompany/get); six new routes in routes/modules/fleet.php under the same staff-auth group as the rest of Phase 7: GET /api/fleet/units/{id}/external-repairs[?limit=N] (per-unit history list), POST /api/fleet/units/{id}/external-repairs (body: vendor_name, service_date, category, description, costs, meter readings), GET /api/fleet/external-repairs (company-wide list with vendor/category/from/to/limit filters for "how much did we spend on tires last quarter" rollups), GET /api/fleet/external-repairs/{id} (detail), PUT /api/fleet/external-repairs/{id}, DELETE /api/fleet/external-repairs/{id}; DI wiring constructs FleetExternalRepairRepository + FleetExternalRepairService + FleetExternalRepairController alongside the rest of Phase 7's components so all five sub-phases share request-scoped FleetUnitRepository + FleetUnitReadingRepository instances — the external-repair service uses the SAME unit repo as the unit service, so meter cache updates flowing through updateMeterCache stay consistent across both codepaths; Phase 7.4 cost report integration: FleetCostReportService constructor gains an optional ?FleetExternalRepairRepository $externalRepairs = null parameter + both costPerMile()/costPerHour() public methods gain a bool $includeExternal = false parameter — when include_external=1 is passed in the report URL AND the external repo was DI'd in, the cost report unions per-unit external costs (keyed on service_date DATE range substr'd from the DATETIME report bounds) into the same rollup as internal workorder costs, which means unit rows in the include_external mode carry new fields internal_cost (workorder-derived cost) + external_cost (vendor-derived total) + external_other_cost (non-labor/non-parts external vendor charges like disposal fees, env surcharges, mobile call-out fees) + external_repair_count (how many external invoices hit in the window) so the UI can render "$10k total — $7k internal, $3k external across 4 vendor invoices" without re-querying the repair log; total_cost + labor_cost + parts_cost + cost_per_mile|cost_per_hour all compute on the combined internal+external denominator so cost-per-mile reflects true cost of ownership including outside work, not just shop-internal cost; external-only units (a van the shop has never touched but the dealer services exclusively) surface in the report with internal_cost=0 + external_cost>0 so retired-to-vendor-only units stay visible; 21-case behavior test (FleetExternalRepairServiceTest.php) uses the established SQLite-in-memory pattern with ExtRepairInMemoryConnection + ExtRepairPermissiveGate + ExtRepairFakeAudit + real Repository instances against seeded fleet_units/fleet_unit_readings/fleet_external_repairs/workorders/workorder_jobs/workorder_items schema (workorder tables seeded to exercise the 7.4 include_external union path); coverage: createRepair happy path + audit context, total auto-computed from labor+parts+other when omitted, total mismatch (labor+parts+other != supplied total beyond $0.01) rejected, unknown category rejected, required field validation for vendor_name/description/service_date, negative cost rejected, odometer_at_service provided auto-creates a fleet_unit_reading + bumps meter cache, older odometer_at_service than latest reading skips silently (monotonic guard — no exception raised, external invoices commonly arrive out of order), engine_hours_at_service creates a separate engine_hours reading, updateRepair partial patch preserves unchanged fields, unknown id update rejection, delete idempotent (second call no-op, no audit duplicate), listForUnit returns newest-first by service_date, listForCompany vendor/category/from/to filters combine correctly, cross-company unit reference rejected, unknown fleet_unit_id rejected, fleet.manage denial blocks writes, fleet.view denial blocks reads, cost report include_external=true unions per-unit external costs correctly (internal $500 + external $350 = $850 total with internal_cost/external_cost split fields populated), cost report surfaces external-only units (internal_cost=0, external_cost>0 for units with no workorders but vendor invoices), invalid service_date string rejected, vendor_name >120 chars rejected, getRepair serialization returns full hydrated row; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green; closes out the Fleet Maintenance section — Phase 7.1 through 7.5 now fully merged)

### Phase 8 — FIT Inspections compliance layer
- [x] Compliance-tagged templates per-division _(8.1 — one new migration (133_inspection_compliance.sql) lays down two new tables plus idempotent column extensions on inspection_items so the existing inspection CRUD pipeline carries regulatory metadata without any data-shape breakage: inspection_compliance_tags is a tag vocabulary table (id, code VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL — lowercased + char-class-validated in the service, label VARCHAR(160), description TEXT, regulatory_body VARCHAR(40) DEFAULT 'other' stored as string not ENUM so adding new bodies is a data migration not DDL, division_id INT UNSIGNED NULL with NULL meaning "global tag visible to every division" and non-null scoping to one service line, is_active + sort_order for the manager-facing picker, created_by_user_id + created_at + updated_at ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, UNIQUE (code, division_id) so two divisions can both have "state_safety" but one division can't duplicate it, INDEX (division_id, is_active) for the hot division-filtered picker path, INDEX (regulatory_body) for future "all DOT tags" rollups); inspection_template_compliance_tags is a thin many-to-many junction (template_id, tag_id, created_at, composite PK, INDEX (tag_id) for reverse "which templates carry this tag") so the same template can be tagged DOT + OSHA + state-safety simultaneously without a triplication of template rows; inspection_items gets seven new nullable columns all added idempotently via information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so re-running the migration is a no-op: severity VARCHAR(16) NULL (advisory/minor/major/critical — drives risk scoring in 8.4), compliance_tag_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL to inspection_compliance_tags so items can carry their OWN tag independent of the template's tag set (a template tagged "DOT annual" can still have one item tagged "EPA hazmat" for cross-regulatory items like diesel tank inspections), compliance_reference VARCHAR(120) NULL for the regulatory citation ("49 CFR 396.3"), requires_photo TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0 + requires_measurement TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0 (inspector obligation flags the completion service will enforce in 8.2/8.3), measurement_unit VARCHAR(24) NULL (mm, psi, V, etc.), pass_condition VARCHAR(240) NULL (human-readable criterion like "tread depth >= 4/32\""); four idempotent FKs install via information_schema guards (fk_insp_compliance_tag_division SET NULL to divisions guarded on divisions-table existence so pre-0.2 schemas don't fail, fk_insp_template_compliance_tag_template CASCADE to inspection_templates, fk_insp_template_compliance_tag_tag CASCADE to inspection_compliance_tags so deleting a tag removes all its bindings, fk_insp_item_compliance_tag SET NULL so deleting a tag leaves items with compliance_reference text intact but clears the FK); InspectionComplianceTag model carries REG_BODY_DOT/OSHA/EPA/FEDERAL/STATE/INSURANCE/INTERNAL/OTHER constants + ALLOWED_REGULATORY_BODIES array the service consumes for enum validation + CODE_MAX_LEN=64/LABEL_MAX_LEN=160/DESCRIPTION_MAX_LEN=2000 constants alongside the column defs so a schema change ripples to service validation in one place; InspectionItem model extended with SEVERITY_ADVISORY/MINOR/MAJOR/CRITICAL constants + ALLOWED_SEVERITIES + COMPLIANCE_REFERENCE_MAX_LEN/MEASUREMENT_UNIT_MAX_LEN/PASS_CONDITION_MAX_LEN constants + seven new typed public properties (severity/compliance_tag_id/compliance_reference/requires_photo/requires_measurement/measurement_unit/pass_condition) with safe nullable defaults so existing callsites that instantiate InspectionItem without the new fields keep working; InspectionComplianceTagRepository follows the COLUMNS const + hydrate pattern established in Phase 7 with create/findById/findByCode(code, ?divisionId) handling null-division lookups with a separate query (MySQL + SQLite both treat NULL in equality as non-matching so the null-divisionId path has its own IS NULL predicate) / listForDivision(divisionId, activeOnly) which always includes globals (division_id IS NULL OR division_id = :div) ordered by sort_order ASC then label ASC so the picker is deterministic / update() deliberately omits code + division_id from the writable set because those define the tag's identity and in-place rename would orphan bindings silently / delete() by id with CASCADE handling the junction cleanup at the FK layer / setTagsForTemplate(templateId, ids[]) implements replace-all semantics (DELETE all then INSERT dedup'd) so the HTTP PUT body is the authoritative binding set — callers don't manage attach/detach individually / listTagsForTemplate(templateId) / listTemplateIdsForAnyTag for future use by 8.3 / listTemplatesByCompliance(divisionId, tagCodes[], activeOnly) which JOINs inspection_templates + junction + tags with AND-combined filters (division scope includes NULL-division templates as global, tag codes AND filter), grouping results per-template with all bound tag_codes surfaced (not just matched ones) so the UI can show "this template matches your DOT filter AND also carries OSHA and state tags"; InspectionComplianceService is the orchestration layer gating reads on inspections.view and writes on inspections.manage (both already in the inspections.* wildcard for managers + technicians — no new permission surface to administer) with seven public methods: createTag (catches PDOException 23000 + duplicate/UNIQUE string match for SQLite test compat and rethrows as InvalidArgumentException "compliance tag code is already in use for this division" so UNIQUE violations surface as legible 400s), updateTag (merge-then-validate partial-PUT shape, omits code + division_id from the merged input so callers can't rename-in-place through the update path), deleteTag (idempotent — already-absent is a no-op + no duplicate audit so double-click / retry storms from the client don't surface 404s), listTags (optional divisionId + activeOnly), setTagsForTemplate (validates template exists + every tag_id exists + dedups the binding list + wraps the replace-all in a transaction so a partial failure rolls back to the prior binding set, emits inspection.compliance_tag.template_bound audit with the full tag_ids list, returns the post-bind tag list so the client gets a fresh snapshot without a second GET), listTagsForTemplate, listTemplatesByCompliance (delegates straight through to repository); validation rules enforced at the service layer: code trim + required + <=CODE_MAX_LEN + preg_match('/^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.\\-]*$/') + auto-lowercase so the vocabulary stays URL-safe + case-insensitive (a sloppy "DOT Annual" and a typo "dot_annual " don't end up as distinct tags), label trim + required + <=LABEL_MAX_LEN, description optional + <=DESCRIPTION_MAX_LEN, regulatory_body auto-lowercased + validated against ALLOWED_REGULATORY_BODIES defaulting to 'other' when omitted, division_id cast to positive int or null, is_active + sort_order bool + int casts with sort_order >= 0 guard; InspectionTemplateService.insertItems extended to write the seven new compliance columns (severity/compliance_tag_id/compliance_reference/requires_photo/requires_measurement/measurement_unit/pass_condition) from the template-CRUD payload with per-field validation delegated to private normalizeSeverity (case-insensitive + ALLOWED_SEVERITIES enum check) + trimOrNull (len-capped) helpers — all fields default to null/0 so existing template-create/update callers that don't populate compliance fields continue to insert unchanged, AND fetchItems extended to surface all seven fields in the template-with-sections response so the React side doesn't need a second query to render per-item regulatory metadata; route group in routes/api.php: InspectionTemplateService constructor gains the existing auditLogger (already in scope) so template_created/_updated/_deleted events flow to the audit store — the wiring was trivially null before Phase 8, keeping the parity across services; seven new routes under the existing /api/inspections staff-auth group: GET /api/inspections/compliance-tags?division_id=X&active_only=1, POST /api/inspections/compliance-tags, PUT /api/inspections/compliance-tags/{id}, DELETE /api/inspections/compliance-tags/{id}, GET /api/inspections/templates/{id}/compliance-tags, PUT /api/inspections/templates/{id}/compliance-tags (body: tag_ids[]), GET /api/inspections/templates/by-compliance?division_id=X&tag_codes=code1,code2&active_only=1 (tag_codes is comma-separated because query-array parsing across the curl/fetch/Axios stack is inconsistent enough that a flat string is safer); InspectionComplianceController is a thin seven-method HTTP facade (one-liner delegation per method) — the PUT template-tags route parses body.tag_ids into int[] before handing to the service so the HTTP layer absorbs the loose-typing that comes from JSON POSTs, the GET by-compliance route splits tag_codes on comma + trims + filters empties so "a, b,,c" produces ['a','b','c']; 22-case SQLite-in-memory behavior test (InspectionComplianceServiceTest.php) uses ComplianceInMemoryConnection + CompliancePermissiveGate + ComplianceFakeAudit + real InspectionComplianceTagRepository against seeded inspection_templates + inspection_compliance_tags + inspection_template_compliance_tags schema; UNIQUE(code, division_id) enforced via CREATE UNIQUE INDEX (code, COALESCE(division_id, 0)) in the test schema because SQLite treats NULLs as distinct in UNIQUE keys by default — COALESCEing to 0 collapses the null-division global tag into a single-slot unique row, matching the MySQL InnoDB behavior where NULL deliberately does not collide; coverage: createTag happy path + audit + default is_active/sort_order, code auto-lowercased + regulatory_body auto-lowercased, invalid code chars rejected (spaces, bangs, etc.), unknown regulatory_body rejected against ALLOWED list, duplicate (code, division_id) surfaces as InvalidArgumentException (not PDOException) via the PDO 23000 catch, same code across different divisions allowed, global (NULL division) duplicate rejected, empty code/label required-field rejection, updateTag partial patch preserves code + division_id + regulatory_body when not supplied + updates label + sort_order, updateTag unknown id rejected, deleteTag removes row + emits audit, deleteTag idempotent (no audit for already-absent), listTags for division includes global + own (not other-division tags), listTags activeOnly filters inactive, setTagsForTemplate binds multiple + listTagsForTemplate echoes them, setTagsForTemplate empty clears (replace-all), setTagsForTemplate rejects unknown tag id, setTagsForTemplate rejects unknown template id, setTagsForTemplate dedups repeat ids, listTemplatesByCompliance filters by division + tag code AND-combined, activeOnly respects inactive templates, surfaces all bound tag_codes per template not just the matched filter, inspections.manage denial blocks writes, inspections.view denial blocks reads, setTagsForTemplate gated on inspections.manage; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green)
- [x] Deficiency → auto-WO/quote generation _(8.2 — one new migration (134_inspection_auto_generation_policies.sql) lays down two tables that complete the inspection→estimate bridge's missing leg: inspection_auto_generation_policies holds the rule set (id, division_id NULL with NULL meaning "global policy applied to every report" and non-null scoping to one service line so a fleet shop's DOT-centric policy doesn't fire on auto-repair inspections, name VARCHAR(160) for the admin UI, trigger_severity VARCHAR(16) DEFAULT 'high' aligned with the runtime severity vocabulary emitted by InspectionEstimateBridgeService::evaluateItemFailure — low/medium/high/critical — not the item-level advisory/minor/major/critical from 8.1 because policies are evaluated against the real-world response not the template-configured floor, compliance_tag_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL to inspection_compliance_tags so a policy can narrow to "only DOT-tagged items" or wildcard across tags when NULL, target_kind VARCHAR(32) DEFAULT 'auto' with three allowed values (estimate for a brand-new standalone quote, subestimate_on_workorder for an in-flight job's additional work, auto which resolves to subestimate when the report is linked to an open workorder and falls back to standalone estimate otherwise — the usual default covering both "walk-in inspection" and "second failure mid-service" cases from one policy), min_failed_items INT DEFAULT 1 so a policy can require "at least 3 critical findings" before auto-quoting (avoids premature auto-generation on a single-item fluke), require_customer_approval TINYINT DEFAULT 1 (informational marker surfaced in generated estimate notes — Phase 8.3 will promote this into the state machine), auto_assign_to_technician_id NULL FK SET NULL to users for future dispatch-integrated auto-assign, is_active + sort_order + created_by_user_id + timestamps, INDEX (division_id, is_active, sort_order) for the hot "list active policies for this report's division" path, INDEX (compliance_tag_id) for reverse "which policies bind to this tag" lookups); inspection_auto_generation_runs is the idempotency + audit trail table (id, policy_id FK CASCADE so deleting a policy sweeps its run history, inspection_report_id FK CASCADE, status VARCHAR(24) DEFAULT 'triggered' with values triggered/skipped/failed for run-outcome tracking, estimate_id + sub_estimate_id + workorder_id nullable linkbacks to whatever the run produced so the UI can show "this sub-estimate was auto-generated by policy X from report Y", items_generated INT for reporting, note TEXT NULL for failure reasons or skip explanations, triggered_by_user_id NULL for manual-fire attribution, UNIQUE (policy_id, inspection_report_id) so the same policy fires at most once per report even if evaluateReport is called 100 times — this is the idempotency guarantee that lets the completion hook and the manual retry button share one code path without worrying about duplicate generation); four idempotent FKs install via information_schema guards (fk_insp_auto_gen_policy_division SET NULL to divisions guarded on divisions-table existence, fk_insp_auto_gen_policy_tag SET NULL to inspection_compliance_tags, fk_insp_auto_gen_policy_technician SET NULL to users, fk_insp_auto_gen_run_policy CASCADE, fk_insp_auto_gen_run_report CASCADE); InspectionAutoGenerationPolicy model carries SEVERITY_LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL + ALLOWED_SEVERITIES + a SEVERITY_ORDER map (low=1, medium=2, high=3, critical=4) the service consumes for >= threshold comparisons without hardcoding the order everywhere + TARGET_ESTIMATE/SUBESTIMATE_ON_WORKORDER/AUTO + ALLOWED_TARGET_KINDS + NAME_MAX_LEN=160 alongside typed public properties with safe defaults (trigger_severity='high', target_kind='auto', min_failed_items=1, require_customer_approval=true, is_active=true); InspectionAutoGenerationPolicyRepository follows the COLUMNS const + hydrate pattern with create/findById/update (deliberately omits division_id from the writable set — the repo doc-comment explains it is an organizational/filter field baked into existing run markers and changing it mid-life risks orphaning them, so rename-via-delete+recreate forces downstream impact to surface) / delete / listForDivision(divisionId, activeOnly) which always includes globals (division_id IS NULL OR division_id = :div) ordered by sort_order ASC then id ASC for deterministic UI / hasRun(policyId, reportId) the idempotency check / recordRun(data[]) the insert-returning-id for the run marker / listRunsForReport(reportId) for "show me the auto-generation history of this report" UI; InspectionDeficiencyAutoService is the orchestration layer gating policy CRUD + manual evaluation on inspections.manage (reads on inspections.view — both already in the inspections.* wildcard so no new permission surface) with seven public methods: createPolicy / updatePolicy (merge-then-validate partial-PUT, strips division_id + created_by_user_id from the update set so repository update() signature stays tight) / deletePolicy (idempotent already-absent no-op) / listPolicies / evaluateReport (the manual entry point — gated, delegates to private runEvaluation) / evaluateReportOnCompletion (the hook entry point called from InspectionCompletionService::complete — wraps runEvaluation in try/catch Throwable so a bad policy never blocks report completion, emits inspection.auto_policy.hook_failure audit with the exception message for forensic triage) / listRunsForReport; validation rules enforced at the service layer: name trim + required + <=NAME_MAX_LEN, trigger_severity auto-lowercased + ALLOWED enum check, target_kind auto-lowercased + ALLOWED enum check, division_id positive int or null, compliance_tag_id positive int (validated as existing via assertComplianceTagExists FK-parity so the service catches broken refs before the INSERT attempts the FK), min_failed_items >= 1, auto_assign_to_technician_id positive int or null, booleans + sort_order (>=0); the core runEvaluation pipeline resolves the report's division context via a LEFT JOIN (inspection_templates for division_id — reports don't carry division directly, the template does — and workorders for an open workorder linked via the report's estimate_id so "inspection during active service" → sub-estimate path fires naturally), calls InspectionEstimateBridgeService::identifyFailedItems (reuses the existing evaluateItemFailure severity mapping so auto and manual paths can't drift), fetches per-item compliance_tag_id in one batched IN(...) query keyed by report_item_id (avoids N+1 when filtering by tag), loads active policies via listForDivision (globals + this division's), and for each policy checks hasRun (idempotency) + filters failed items by severity >= trigger_severity (using SEVERITY_ORDER for numeric comparison, skipping already_converted items from the bridge) + tag match (compliance_tag_id NULL = wildcard, non-null = exact match) + min_failed_items threshold before calling executePolicy which dispatches on target_kind; executePolicy resolves 'auto' via resolveTargetKind (subestimate when a workorder is linked, standalone estimate otherwise — documented inline so the behavior isn't a hidden surprise), calls InspectionEstimateBridgeService::createEstimateFromFailedItems for standalone or ::addToWorkorder for sub-estimate (reuses the bridge's existing happy-path so auto and manual paths share one transaction/audit/conversion-tracking implementation — if the bridge's internal logic evolves, both callers benefit automatically), records the run marker with status triggered/skipped/failed + estimate_id/sub_estimate_id/workorder_id/items_generated/note, and wraps the bridge call in try/catch Throwable so a FK constraint violation or estimate-number collision lands as a status='failed' run row with the exception message truncated to 500 chars instead of bubbling and blocking other policies for the same report; subestimate_on_workorder + no workorder linked produces a 'skipped' run with an explanatory note (the policy is kept active so the next inspection on a workorder-linked report still fires it — skipping isn't disabling); InspectionCompletionService.complete extended with an optional ?InspectionDeficiencyAutoService constructor param + setAutoService() setter (the setter pattern handles the chicken-and-egg where both services need each other at route-wiring time without circular DI gymnastics) + a one-line evaluateReportOnCompletion call immediately after the inspection.report_completed audit emission so the hook fires on the transaction-committed post-complete state — best-effort wrapping inside evaluateReportOnCompletion ensures a policy misconfiguration can't block inspection completion; InspectionDeficiencyAutoController is a thin six-method HTTP facade; six new routes under the existing /api/inspections staff-auth group: GET /api/inspections/auto-policies?division_id=X&active_only=1, POST /api/inspections/auto-policies, PUT /api/inspections/{id}/auto-runs (run history for the report detail UI); route wiring hoists the bridge service construction above its bridge-controller use so auto-service + bridge-controller share - [x] Failed-item escalation rules _(8.3 — one new migration (135_inspection_escalation_rules.sql) introduces the human-in-the-loop layer on top of 8.2's machine-auto-quote default: some failures (critical brake findings on a DOT inspection, safety-flagged fleet units, hazmat tank integrity issues) MUST route to a named person or role for sign-off regardless of whether a quote gets auto-generated — 8.3 models those obligations as first-class escalation records with acknowledge/resolve lifecycle + per-user or per-role routing + best-effort notification dispatch; two tables: inspection_escalation_rules is the rule vocabulary (id, division_id NULL with NULL meaning "global rule across all divisions", name VARCHAR(160), trigger_severity VARCHAR(16) DEFAULT 'critical' using the runtime severity vocabulary emitted by InspectionEstimateBridgeService::evaluateItemFailure — low/medium/high/critical — matching 8.2's choice for the same reason: rules evaluate real-world responses not template floors, compliance_tag_id INT UNSIGNED NULL for "only DOT items" / "only EPA items" narrowing, assign_to_user_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL to users for direct user routing (the DOT officer on this shift, the fleet manager, etc.), assign_to_role VARCHAR(32) NULL for role-queue routing ("whoever is acting as dispatcher right now"), notify_via VARCHAR(16) NULL with three values email/sms/internal — email attempts NotificationDispatcher::sendMail to the routed user's email, sms is currently recorded-as-failed because the users table has no phone column in the core schema (the service explicitly documents this and tags the rule as "awaiting SMS integration"), internal is a record-only mode that surfaces via listOpenForRole/listOpenForMe queues without attempting external dispatch — useful for teams that run their own Slack/Teams bots that poll the API, notification_template VARCHAR(120) NULL for the template key passed to NotificationDispatcher, priority VARCHAR(16) DEFAULT 'normal' with low/normal/high/urgent for UI sort + notification formatting, require_acknowledgment TINYINT DEFAULT 1 informational for the UI + future SLA surface in Phase 8.4, is_active + sort_order + created_by_user_id + timestamps, four indexes: (division_id, is_active, sort_order) for the hot active-rules-for-division lookup, (compliance_tag_id) for reverse "which rules bind to this tag", (assign_to_user_id) + (assign_to_role) for the open-queue-for-assignee paths); inspection_escalations is the per-failed-item record table (id, rule_id FK CASCADE so deleting a rule sweeps its open escalations, inspection_report_id FK CASCADE, inspection_report_item_id FK CASCADE so deleting a report item also clears its escalation (though since report items are insert-only in practice this is a safety belt not an expected path), priority + severity echoed from the rule+item at creation time so the record is self-contained if the rule later changes, assigned_to_user_id + assigned_to_role echoed too so reassignment of a rule post-creation doesn't silently redirect open queues, status VARCHAR(24) DEFAULT 'pending' with three states pending/acknowledged/resolved — forward-only progression, resolve implies acknowledgment (either explicit or side-effect), notification_status VARCHAR(24) NULL with pending/sent/failed/skipped values written by the dispatch side-car, notification_error VARCHAR(500) NULL for the failure reason ("no email on user", "simulated SMTP failure", "SMS not supported for user-routed escalations"), acknowledged_by_user_id/at + resolved_by_user_id/at + resolution_note TEXT for the lifecycle timestamps and the free-form resolution commentary, created_by_user_id NULL for manual-fire attribution, UNIQUE (rule_id, inspection_report_id, inspection_report_item_id) — the idempotency guarantee that lets the completion hook and manual re-runs share one codepath without duplicate escalation creation, three indexes: (inspection_report_id) for "show all escalations for this report" detail UI, (status, priority) for the open-queue ORDER-BY path, (assigned_to_user_id, status) + (assigned_to_role, status) for listOpenForMe/listOpenForRole hot paths); seven idempotent FKs install via information_schema guards (fk_insp_escalation_rule_division SET NULL guarded on divisions-table existence, fk_insp_escalation_rule_tag SET NULL to inspection_compliance_tags, fk_insp_escalation_rule_user SET NULL to users, fk_insp_escalation_rule CASCADE from escalation→rule, fk_insp_escalation_report CASCADE, fk_insp_escalation_report_item CASCADE, fk_insp_escalation_assignee SET NULL to users); InspectionEscalationRule model carries SEVERITY_LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL + ALLOWED_SEVERITIES + SEVERITY_ORDER map (low=1, medium=2, high=3, critical=4 — same shape as 8.2's policy model so severity comparisons stay idiomatic across both services), PRIORITY_LOW/NORMAL/HIGH/URGENT + ALLOWED_PRIORITIES, NOTIFY_VIA_EMAIL/SMS/INTERNAL + ALLOWED_NOTIFY_VIA, NAME_MAX_LEN=160 + ROLE_MAX_LEN=32 + TEMPLATE_MAX_LEN=120 constants alongside typed public properties with safe defaults (trigger_severity='critical' — the conservative default, only escalate on truly high-stakes findings, not every minor issue), priority='normal', require_acknowledgment=true, is_active=true; InspectionEscalation model carries STATUS_PENDING/ACKNOWLEDGED/RESOLVED + ALLOWED_STATUSES + NOTIFY_STATUS_PENDING/SENT/FAILED/SKIPPED + RESOLUTION_NOTE_MAX_LEN=2000 alongside typed public properties for the full lifecycle-tracking row; InspectionEscalationRepository is a unified CRUD layer for both tables (one repo instead of two because the tables share a tight parent-child relationship and the service always touches both — splitting would force trivial coordination without a real separation-of-concerns win): rule surface — createRule / findRuleById / updateRule (division_id omitted from writable set, same rationale as 8.2) / deleteRule / listRulesForDivision(divisionId, activeOnly) including globals; escalation surface — hasEscalation(ruleId, reportId, itemId) the UNIQUE-backed idempotency check, createEscalation, findEscalationById, listEscalationsForReport (ORDER BY id ASC for deterministic chronology), listOpenEscalationsForUser(userId) (status IN (pending, acknowledged) — resolved drops out of open queues), listOpenEscalationsForRole(role) (same open filter), markAcknowledged(id, userId) / markResolved(id, userId, note) / updateNotificationStatus(id, status, error) — the last one is the dispatch side-car's write path, separate from the lifecycle update so notification retries don't stomp on status transitions; InspectionEscalationService is the orchestration layer gating rule CRUD + manual evaluateReport on inspections.manage (reads + lifecycle on inspections.view because anyone who can see inspections should be able to acknowledge/resolve their own queue — making acknowledge/resolve require manage would centralize the work on admins which defeats the whole point of role-queue routing) with thirteen public methods: createRule / updateRule (merge-then-validate partial-PUT, strips division_id + created_by_user_id before update) / deleteRule (idempotent already-absent no-op) / listRules / evaluateReport (manual entry point — gated, delegates to private runEvaluation) / evaluateReportOnCompletion (hook entry point called from InspectionCompletionService::complete — wraps runEvaluation in try/catch Throwable + emits inspection.escalation.hook_failure audit) / listEscalationsForReport / listOpenForMe (the actor's queue) / listOpenForRole(role) / acknowledge (idempotent — already-acknowledged is a no-op returning the current serialization without duplicate audit) / resolve (idempotent for already-resolved, validates note length, resolves from both pending and acknowledged states); validation rules enforced at the service layer: name trim + required + <=NAME_MAX_LEN, trigger_severity auto-lowercased + ALLOWED enum check, priority auto-lowercased + ALLOWED enum check, notify_via auto-lowercased + ALLOWED enum check (optional field), notification_template <=TEMPLATE_MAX_LEN (optional), division_id positive int or null, compliance_tag_id positive int validated via assertComplianceTagExists FK-parity, assign_to_user_id positive int or null, assign_to_role auto-lowercased + <=ROLE_MAX_LEN, at-least-one-of-user-or-role check (a rule with neither can't route anywhere — caught up-front with "rule must set at least one of assign_to_user_id or assign_to_role"), booleans + sort_order (>=0); the runEvaluation pipeline resolves the report's division context via a LEFT JOIN to inspection_templates (same as 8.2 because reports don't carry division directly), calls InspectionEstimateBridgeService::identifyFailedItems (same reason as 8.2 — reuses evaluateItemFailure so severity stays consistent across all three inspection services), fetches per-item compliance_tag_id via one batched IN(...) query, loads active rules via listRulesForDivision (globals + this division), and for each rule × each failed item combo: filters by severity >= trigger_severity (using SEVERITY_ORDER), filters by compliance_tag_id match (NULL = wildcard, non-null = exact), checks hasEscalation (UNIQUE-backed idempotency) then calls createEscalationRecord which inserts the row + emits inspection.escalation.created audit + dispatches the notification via dispatchNotification; dispatchNotification is the best-effort side-car — only fires when notify_via is set, routes internal→skipped (record-only), missing dispatcher→skipped (explanatory error), missing template→skipped, role-only rules→skipped (can't resolve an email/phone for a role without a user — the service documents this and tags the escalation "rule is role-routed; no user email/phone to notify" so the UI can surface a clear "someone should acknowledge this via the queue" hint), missing user→failed, missing email on user→failed ("user has no email"), sms→failed with "SMS not supported for user-routed escalations (no phone on user)" since the core users table has no phone column, email→sendMail attempt wrapped in try/catch(Throwable) with the exception message truncated to 490 chars stored as notification_error — crucially, dispatch failure does NOT block escalation creation, the escalation is already persisted before dispatch runs, so a misconfigured SMTP or missing template produces an escalation with notification_status=failed that the UI can surface for retry rather than blocking compliance workflow; resolveTargetKind analog (not needed here — escalations don't route to entities, only to users/roles); InspectionCompletionService extended with an optional ?InspectionEscalationService constructor param + setEscalationService setter (same pattern as 8.2's autoService) + a one-line evaluateReportOnCompletion call after 8.2's autoService call so both hook paths fire in order after inspection.report_completed audit emission — each is independently best-effort so a 8.2 failure doesn't block 8.3 and vice versa; InspectionEscalationController is a thin ten-method HTTP facade; ten new routes under the existing /api/inspections staff-auth group: GET /api/inspections/escalation-rules?division_id=X&active_only=1, POST /api/inspections/escalation-rules, PUT /api/inspections/escalation-rules/{id}, DELETE /api/inspections/escalation-rules/{id}, POST /api/inspections/{id}/escalate (manual fire), GET /api/inspections/{id}/escalations (per-report list for the report detail UI), GET /api/inspections/escalations/mine (the actor's open queue), GET /api/inspections/escalations/role/{role} (role-queue view for "dispatcher queue" / "DOT officer queue" UIs), POST /api/inspections/escalations/{id}/acknowledge, POST /api/inspections/escalations/{id}/resolve (body: note); route wiring constructs a request-scoped NotificationDispatcher from config/notifications.php + TemplateEngine + NotificationLogRepository + auditLogger (wrapped in try/catch so a missing notifications config leaves the service running with no notifier — it records "no notification dispatcher configured" as skipped reason and the escalation is still created); 32-case SQLite-in-memory behavior test (InspectionEscalationServiceTest.php) uses EscalationInMemoryConnection + EscalationPermissiveGate + EscalationFakeAudit + EscalationFakeNotifier (a NotificationDispatcher subclass that records sendMail calls in an array and supports throwOnMail for dispatch-failure simulation) + a full schema set including the two new 8.3 tables with UNIQUE(rule_id, inspection_report_id, inspection_report_item_id) matched in-test schema + NOW() sqliteCreateFunction stub for bridge portability;
