coverage: createRule happy path with defaults (severity=critical, priority=normal, require_ack=true, is_active=true) + audit emission, severity/priority/notify_via auto-lowercasing on input, unknown severity/priority/notify_via rejection with clear error messages, empty name rejection, at-least-one-of-user-or-role enforcement, unknown compliance_tag_id rejection, updateRule partial patch preserves division_id, unknown id rejection, deleteRule idempotent (second call no-op + no duplicate audit), listRules division scoping (globals + own div, not other-division); evaluation: evaluateReport creates one escalation per (rule, failing item) combo with correct assigned_to_user_id + pending status, UNIQUE-backed idempotency (second evaluateReport call creates zero new escalations), severity threshold filtering (trigger=critical picks only the critical item not the high one), inactive rule short-circuits, compliance_tag_id filter narrows to tagged items only, unknown report id throws InvalidArgumentException from manual path, no-failed-items returns empty, hook path swallows bad report id and emits hook_failure audit, division scoping (global + matching-division rules fire together, other-division skipped); notifications: email to user-routed rule dispatches via fake notifier with correct to-address, role-routed rule with notify_via=email records as skipped with "rule is role-routed" reason, user with NULL email records as failed with "no email" reason, dispatcher throw (throwOnMail=true) records as failed with captured exception message but escalation stays created, internal notify_via records skipped with null error; lifecycle: acknowledge moves pending→acknowledged with actor/timestamp captured, acknowledge idempotent on already-acknowledged (single audit log), resolve moves pending→resolved with note/timestamp captured (skipping ack state allowed), resolve on unknown id throws, resolve rejects over-length note, listOpenForMe returns pending+acknowledged assigned to actor + resolved ones drop out, listOpenForRole filters by exact role match (dot/manager/other); gate denials: createRule blocked without inspections.manage, listRules blocked without inspections.view, evaluateReport blocked without inspections.manage, acknowledge blocked without inspections.view; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all lint green)
- [x] Risk scoring + trend analysis _(8.4 — one new migration (136_inspection_risk_scores.sql) introduces the analytics layer that turns each completed inspection into a single normalized risk score so fleet managers, division supervisors, and compliance officers can rank assets by deterioration trajectory rather than by raw failure-count which over-weights chatty templates and under-weights single-critical-finding events; one table inspection_risk_scores (id, inspection_report_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL with UNIQUE KEY uk_insp_risk_score_report so re-scoring an existing report is an upsert not a duplicate row — this is the idempotency guarantee that lets the completion-hook fire on every report_completed event without flooding the table even if a report is re-completed via correction workflow, vehicle_id INT UNSIGNED NULL because not every inspection is asset-anchored (yard-walk inspections, facility checks, etc. — the trend queries skip rows with NULL vehicle_id naturally because they filter on vehicle_id = :vid), customer_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL for cross-customer trend rollups in future ops dashboards, division_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL resolved at scoring time via the report's template (reports don't carry division directly, same resolution path as 8.2/8.3) so division-level trends honor the template→division binding even if the report is reassigned later, score DECIMAL(8,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 stored as a real not an int because the compliance-tag multiplier (1.5x) produces fractional scores and rounding to int would discard the regulated-vs-advisory differentiation that's the whole point of the multiplier, risk_level VARCHAR(16) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'low' with five buckets low/moderate/elevated/high/critical bucketed at thresholds 0/0.01/10/25/60 — the 0.01 lower bound for moderate exists so a report with even one tiny failure (e.g. one low-severity advisory item with weight 1.0) lands in moderate not low, separating "passed clean" from "passed with concerns" which matters for fleet KPIs that track 'fully clean' rate, failed_item_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 + critical_item_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 + compliance_failed_count INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 broken-out aggregates so trend dashboards can plot "compliance failures over time" without re-deriving from the raw items table on every render, scored_at DATETIME NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP for the trend ORDER BY anchor + ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP omitted because we want the original scoring time preserved across rescores (the upsert path explicitly writes scored_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP on every write so updates do reset the timestamp — this matches the user-facing semantics: "this report was last scored at X" not "this report was first scored at X"), scored_by_user_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL to users for actor attribution on manual rescores (NULL for hook-fired auto-scores so the audit trail can distinguish manual interventions from background rescoring), context_summary TEXT NULL for a JSON-serialized snapshot of the scoring inputs (failed item ids, severity buckets counts, multiplier-applied items) so a future "explain this score" UI can show the exact inputs without re-running the bridge, created_at + updated_at, four supporting indexes: (vehicle_id, scored_at) for the per-vehicle trend timeline (the hot read path), (division_id, scored_at) for division-level rollups, (customer_id, scored_at) for customer-level rollups, (risk_level, scored_at) for "show me all elevated/high/critical scores in the last week" ops dashboards); five idempotent FKs install via information_schema guards (fk_insp_risk_score_report CASCADE from inspection_reports so deleting a report sweeps its score history, fk_insp_risk_score_vehicle SET NULL guarded on vehicles-table existence, fk_insp_risk_score_customer SET NULL guarded on customers-table existence, fk_insp_risk_score_division SET NULL guarded on divisions-table existence, fk_insp_risk_score_user SET NULL to users for the actor); InspectionRiskScore model carries RISK_LEVEL_LOW/MODERATE/ELEVATED/HIGH/CRITICAL + ALLOWED_RISK_LEVELS array for service-side validation, SEVERITY_WEIGHTS = [low=>1.0, medium=>3.0, high=>6.0, critical=>10.0] as the canonical scoring table — chosen with non-linear escalation so a single critical finding (10pts) outweighs three medium findings (9pts) which mirrors the regulatory reality that one cracked brake line is more actionable than three minor cosmetic issues, COMPLIANCE_TAG_MULTIPLIER = 1.5 as the regulated-vs-advisory differentiator so a critical-severity item carrying a DOT compliance tag scores 15.0 not 10.0 — the multiplier rewards rules-driven failures over judgment-call advisories, LEVEL_THRESHOLDS = [low=>0.0, moderate=>0.01, elevated=>10.0, high=>25.0, critical=>60.0] as the canonical bucket boundaries (one critical-tagged item = 15.0 = elevated, two critical-tagged items = 30.0 = high, four critical-tagged items = 60.0 = critical so the most severe bucket is reserved for genuinely catastrophic reports not chatty ones), typed public properties for the full row with safe defaults; InspectionRiskScoreRepository follows the COLUMNS const + private hydrate pattern with upsert(data[]) that uses find-then-insert-or-update semantics rather than ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE because SQLite (the test backend) doesn't support that syntax — the production-side correctness guarantee comes from the UNIQUE KEY which is enforced at the InnoDB layer regardless of the upsert pattern used at the application level, findByReportId(reportId) returning ?InspectionRiskScore for the "current score for this report" check (also used by the upsert path's find half), listForVehicle(vehicleId, ?fromDate, ?toDate, limit) which expands the date window via ' 00:00:00'/' 23:59:59' suffixing inside the repo so callers pass naked YYYY-MM-DD without time-component management — both date params are nullable to support "the last N scores for this vehicle regardless of date" in the per-vehicle history pane, listForDivision(divisionId, fromDate, toDate, limit) which requires both dates because a division-level "all scores ever" query is expensive on a large fleet — the HTTP layer enforces the requirement up front via Response::badRequest so the repo's hard-required signature matches the route contract, both list methods ORDER BY scored_at ASC, id ASC for deterministic chronological output (ASC not DESC because the trend-direction algorithm splits the array in half and compares first-half-avg vs second-half-avg — ascending order means "second half" is the recent half which makes the deteriorating/improving labels intuitive); InspectionRiskScoringService is the orchestration layer gating manual rescore on inspections.manage and read paths (current score, vehicle trend, division trend) on inspections.view (both already in the inspections.* wildcard so no new permission surface to administer) with five public methods: scoreReport (manual entry point — gated, delegates to private runScoring, returns the persisted score serialization), scoreReportOnCompletion (hook entry point called from InspectionCompletionService::complete after 8.3's escalation hook — wraps runScoring in try/catch Throwable and emits inspection.risk_score.hook_failure audit + returns null on failure so a scoring exception never blocks report completion or the prior 8.2/8.3 hooks, also returns null cleanly when the report is unknown without firing hook_failure since fetchReportContext returns null for unknown reports — only real exceptions fire the failure audit so legitimate "unknown report" cases stay quiet), getReportScore (read path — gated, returns ?array via repo.findByReportId), vehicleTrend (read path — gated, validates positive vehicleId + optional date window via normalizeWindow, returns aggregate stats + raw timeline + direction label), divisionTrend (read path — gated, validates positive divisionId + required date window, returns aggregate stats + raw timeline + by_risk_level breakdown + direction label); the runScoring pipeline calls fetchReportContext (single SELECT inspection_reports JOIN inspection_templates LEFT for division_id resolution + vehicle_id + customer_id), early-returns null for unknown report (the hook caller relies on this for clean unknown-report handling), calls InspectionEstimateBridgeService::identifyFailedItems (reuses the same severity mapping that 8.2 + 8.3 use so risk-scoring and auto-generation and escalation can never disagree about which items are 'failed' or what severity they carry), fetches per-item compliance_tag_id in a single batched IN(...) query keyed by report_item_id (avoids N+1 even on hundred-item reports), accumulates score by iterating failed items: looks up severity weight from SEVERITY_WEIGHTS (defaults to 0 for unknown severity strings so a corrupted severity field can't crash scoring), multiplies by COMPLIANCE_TAG_MULTIPLIER if compliance_tag_id is set, accumulates total + per-bucket counts (failed_item_count, critical_item_count if severity=critical, compliance_failed_count if compliance_tag_id is set), buckets the total via bucketLevel which walks LEVEL_THRESHOLDS in descending order so the highest matching threshold wins (60→critical, 25→high, 10→elevated, 0.01→moderate, else low), assembles context_summary as a json_encoded array of [failed_item_ids, critical_item_ids, compliance_item_ids, severity_counts, computed_score, applied_multiplier_count] for the future "explain this score" UI, calls repo.upsert with the row data, emits inspection.risk_score.created or inspection.risk_score.updated audit (created when the prior findByReportId returned null, updated otherwise) so the audit trail distinguishes first-scoring from rescoring — important for compliance-traceability where you want to know "was this report ever rescored after first completion"; vehicleTrend and divisionTrend both delegate the date validation to normalizeWindow (private helper) which uses DateTimeImmutable parsing for "is this even a valid date" check + from <= to ordering check (rejects inverted windows with a clear "from must be before or equal to to" error) and returns the normalized [from, to] strings the repo expects, both queries fetch the timeline rows then compute aggregate stats (count, avg_score, min_score, max_score) + direction label via computeDirection (private helper) which returns 'insufficient_data' for count<2, then array_slices the timeline into first half + second half (mid = floor(count/2), so an odd-count series puts the middle row in the second half intentionally — the recent half gets the tiebreaker which biases toward "current state matters more than historical state"), computes avg of each half, applies a 10% relative threshold with a floor of 1.0 (so noise on near-zero scores doesn't flicker between improving/deteriorating — a series of [0.0, 0.5, 0.0, 0.5] should land as 'stable' not 'deteriorating'), returns 'improving' if recent < first by more than threshold, 'deteriorating' if recent > first by more than threshold, 'stable' otherwise; divisionTrend additionally builds a by_risk_level breakdown (counts per low/moderate/elevated/high/critical bucket across the full timeline) so the division dashboard can show a stacked bar of "how many critical scores this month vs last month" without re-aggregating client-side; InspectionCompletionService extended with an optional ?InspectionRiskScoringService constructor param + setRiskScoringService setter (same setter pattern as 8.2's autoService and 8.3's escalationService for the same chicken-and-egg DI reason — both services need each other at route-wiring time and the setter unblocks the cycle without a service-locator) + a one-line scoreReportOnCompletion call after 8.3's escalation hook so all three Phase 8 hooks fire in order (auto-generation → escalation → risk scoring) after the inspection.report_completed audit emission, each independently best-effort so a 8.2 failure doesn't block 8.3 doesn't block 8.4 and vice versa; InspectionRiskScoringController is a thin four-method HTTP facade (one-liner delegation per method); four new routes under the existing /api/inspections staff-auth group: GET /api/inspections/{id}/risk-score (current score for the report — 200 with the score serialization or 200 with null data when no score exists yet, not 404 because "this report has no score yet" is a normal pre-completion state not an error), POST /api/inspections/{id}/risk-score/rescore (manual rescore trigger for the "force-recompute" admin button — used after a report is corrected without going through full re-completion), GET /api/inspections/risk-trends/vehicle/{id}?from=&to=&limit= (vehicle timeline — defaults: from/to optional with the limit (default 100, max 500) controlling the scan size when no dates given), GET /api/inspections/risk-trends/division/{id}?from=&to=&limit= (division timeline — from/to required at the HTTP boundary via Response::badRequest because a division-wide unbounded scan is dangerous on a large fleet, default limit 500); route wiring constructs InspectionRiskScoreRepository + InspectionRiskScoringService + InspectionRiskScoringController above the InspectionCompletionService construction and passes the risk-scoring service as the new fifth constructor param to the completion service so all three hooks (auto, escalation, risk) wire in one swoop — the completion-service signature stays consumer-friendly because all hook params are optional with safe null defaults; 30-case SQLite-in-memory behavior test (InspectionRiskScoringServiceTest.php) uses RiskScoringInMemoryConnection + RiskScoringPermissiveGate + RiskScoringFakeAudit + a full schema set including divisions/users/inspection_templates/sections/items/reports/report_items/compliance_tags/estimate_conversions/recommendations + the new inspection_risk_scores table with UNIQUE on inspection_report_id matched in-test schema + NOW() sqliteCreateFunction stub for bridge portability; coverage: weighted scoring math (one high-severity item = 6.0, one critical-tagged item = 10.0 * 1.5 = 15.0, total = 21.0 → elevated bucket), context_summary populated with failed_item_ids + counts, audit emission distinguishes created (first scoring) from updated (rescoring), upsert maintains exactly one row per report across multiple rescores (UNIQUE-backed), no-failed-items report → score = 0 + risk_level = low + counts = 0, critical-heavy report (4 critical-tagged items = 60.0 floor) → risk_level = critical, unknown report id rejection from manual scoreReport, scoreReportOnCompletion returns null for unknown report without firing hook_failure (only real exceptions fire it), scoreReportOnCompletion returns the serialization for valid report, bucket boundary cases (0.0 → low, 0.5 → moderate, 10.0 exact → elevated, 25.0 exact → high, 60.0 exact → critical), getReportScore returns null when no score yet + serialization when present; vehicleTrend coverage: empty timeline returns aggregate stats with 0/null + direction='insufficient_data' for count<2, deteriorating direction (ascending scores), improving direction (descending scores), stable direction (small noise around mean stays under 10% threshold + 1.0 floor), insufficient_data for single-row timeline, date window filtering (from-to bounds exclude out-of-range scores), invalid date string rejection, inverted from>to window rejection, non-positive vehicleId rejection, chronological ordering verified across multiple-day timeline; divisionTrend coverage: requires both from + to (missing either is rejected), by_risk_level aggregation across all five buckets verified with a multi-report timeline, cross-division exclusion (a score in another division doesn't bleed into the queried division's timeline), summary stats (avg/min/max) verified against hand-computed expected values, direction label same logic as vehicle trend; gate denials: scoreReport blocked without inspections.manage, getReportScore + vehicleTrend + divisionTrend blocked without inspections.view; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all six new/modified files lint green via php -l)
- [x] QR launch at asset level _(8.5 — one new migration (137_inspection_qr_launches.sql) bridges the existing AssetQrService (Phase 2.3) with the inspection start flow so a technician scanning a unit's QR sticker is taken straight into a "start inspection on this asset" experience instead of bouncing through the generic public scan landing — closes the loop between "I have a labeled asset in the field" and "I'm now capturing its inspection report" without any manual asset-id lookup or template hunting; the migration does two things in one shot: (1) adds a nullable site_asset_id INT UNSIGNED column to inspection_reports via idempotent information_schema + PREPARE/EXECUTE so a launched inspection carries that anchor for the rest of its lifecycle (also adds idx_inspection_report_asset for the hot "all inspections for this asset" lookup + fk_inspection_report_site_asset SET NULL guarded on site_assets-table existence so deleting an asset detaches its historical inspections rather than cascading them away — historical compliance records must survive asset retirement), and (2) creates inspection_qr_launches as the forensic scan-and-launch audit trail (id, qr_token VARCHAR(96) NOT NULL stored as the lowercased hex string the AssetQrService mints — indexed independently from the asset id so a leaked-then-rotated token still has its scan history queryable, site_asset_id INT UNSIGNED NULL for the resolved asset (NULL when the token didn't resolve so failed scans still leave a forensic trail), inspection_report_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK CASCADE so a deleted report sweeps its launch history along with it (for rare correction-workflow report deletions — the launch row's purpose is gone once the report is gone), inspection_template_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL so deleting a template leaves historical launches intact for forensic queries, launched_by_user_id INT UNSIGNED NULL FK SET NULL because actor attribution is informational not load-bearing, source VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'qr' with three allowed values qr/manual/api so non-QR launch entry points (manual asset picker in the staff UI, integration callbacks from a fleet platform) can share the same audit trail without a forked schema, status VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'preview' with four states preview/started/aborted/failed where preview = "scan landed but no launch attempted yet", started = "draft report created", aborted = "scan reached the launch endpoint but pre-flight checks rejected it (inactive asset, template-division mismatch)", failed = "scan reached the launch endpoint and the start call threw" — the 4-state model lets the asset-detail UI show "5 scans of this sticker today, 3 became reports, 2 bounced because the asset is retired" which is the kind of operational signal that gets lost when scan-and-launch are different audit streams, client_meta TEXT NULL for a JSON-serialized snapshot of geolocation/device/UA at scan time so a future "where was this scanned" map view can replay the field path without re-instrumenting the client, notes VARCHAR(500) NULL for the failure-reason free text written by the service when it bails ("asset status is retired" / "template not available for this asset division" / "start failed: <truncated exception message>"), created_at DATETIME NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP for chronological ordering — five supporting indexes: (site_asset_id, created_at) for the per-asset launch timeline (the hot read path used by the asset-detail page), (qr_token, created_at) for "all scans of this token" queries (useful for catching scan storms that signal a sticker is in a high-traffic area), (launched_by_user_id, created_at) for "what assets did this tech scan today" personal-history queries that the field-tech mobile app surfaces, (inspection_report_id) for "find the launch row that produced this report" reverse lookup used by the report-detail UI, (status, created_at) for ops dashboards that need "scans that bounced in the last 24h" failure-mode counts); five idempotent FKs install via information_schema guards (fk_insp_qr_launch_asset SET NULL guarded on site_assets-table existence so old schemas without site_assets still apply the migration cleanly, fk_insp_qr_launch_report CASCADE so report deletion sweeps launches, fk_insp_qr_launch_template SET NULL so template deletion preserves history, fk_insp_qr_launch_user SET NULL so user-account deletion doesn't lose the launch trail just the actor attribution); InspectionQrLaunch model carries STATUS_PREVIEW/STARTED/ABORTED/FAILED + ALLOWED_STATUSES, SOURCE_QR/MANUAL/API + ALLOWED_SOURCES, TOKEN_PATTERN = '/^[a-f0-9]{16,96}$/' (matches the existing AssetController::publicScanQr validator so the bridge service inherits the same token shape rules without re-deriving them), NOTES_MAX_LEN = 500, typed public properties with safe defaults (source='qr', status='preview') matching the schema column defaults; InspectionReport model extended with `?int $site_asset_id = null` so the new column round-trips cleanly through find()/mapReport()/start() without breaking the existing 11-property model contract — the field is the only addition, all other properties unchanged so existing callers that instantiate InspectionReport without the new field keep working; InspectionQrLaunchRepository follows the COLUMNS const + private hydrate pattern with five public methods: create(data) inserts the row and returns the new id (called twice in the launch lifecycle: once at preview/scan time with status=preview and again at failure-mode rejections with status=failed/aborted), attachReport(launchId, reportId, templateId) the second-phase update that promotes a preview row to status=started + records the freshly-created report and its template (the two-phase persistence guarantees that even if the start call throws between the create and attach, we still have a status=failed row with a notes field documenting why), markStatus(launchId, status, ?notes) for the failure-mode side update path used by the launch service when start() throws or pre-flight checks reject (uses COALESCE(:notes, notes) so the caller can pass NULL to leave existing notes intact when only the status needs updating), findById / listForAsset(assetId, limit) ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC for the recent-first asset-detail timeline / listForToken(token, limit) for the per-token scan-storm detection / findByReportId(reportId) for the report-detail UI's "this report was launched via QR scan from device X at Y" disclosure; InspectionQrLaunchService is the orchestration layer with five public methods: previewByToken(actor, token, ?clientMeta) gates inspections.view, normalizes the token (lowercase + trim + TOKEN_PATTERN regex check) so case-insensitive sticker scanners (some industrial scanners normalize to uppercase) hit the same canonical row, looks up the asset via SiteAssetRepository.findByQrToken (reuses the existing Phase 2.3 lookup so token-resolution stays in one place — if the AssetQrService changes how tokens are minted or stored, the launch service inherits that change for free), creates a launch row (status=failed with "qr token did not resolve" notes if the asset isn't found, status=preview otherwise) so EVERY scan that reaches the endpoint persists a row regardless of outcome (this is the core forensic guarantee — failed scans are usually more interesting than successful ones, they signal stickers in retired-but-not-retrieved zones, sticker-cloning attempts, or scanner misreads), returns a rich preview shape (launch_id for client-side correlation, asset summary, available_templates filtered to the asset's division (matching division_id + globals where division_id IS NULL — same pattern as Phase 8.1's compliance tag scoping), recent_launches limited to 5 most-recent for "you've scanned this twice today" UX, recent_inspections limited to 5 most-recent for "last inspection of this asset was 3 weeks ago" context); launchFromToken(actor, token, templateId, payload, ?clientMeta) gates inspections.update (same gate as the regular completion->start path because launching produces a draft report, not just a read-only preview), validates token + asset + template (asset must be status=active — retired/decommissioned assets reject with status=aborted launch row + notes, template must be active AND in the asset's division or a global template — out-of-division templates reject with status=aborted launch row + notes capturing the mismatch for forensic triage), creates a status=preview launch row first (so a downstream completion->start failure still leaves a row), wraps completion->start in try/catch where the catch markStatus(launchId, failed, "start failed: <truncated message>") + emits start_failed audit + rethrows so the HTTP layer surfaces the failure to the caller while the persistence side captures it for ops, on success calls attachReport(launchId, reportId, templateId) to promote the preview row to started status with the report linkage, returns [launch, report, asset] as a single payload so the field client doesn't need a follow-up GET to render the post-launch confirmation screen; the customer_id resolution is interesting: the launch payload accepts an explicit customer_id override (used when the field tech knows the customer better than the site record — e.g. a sub-tenant scenario), but if not supplied the service falls back to resolveCustomerId which looks up the asset's site.customer_id from the sites table (best-effort — wrapped in try/catch so a missing sites table or NULL customer_id silently returns null and the start call's assertPayload throws "Customer is required" upstream giving a clear error message instead of a stack trace); listForAsset(actor, assetId, limit) gates inspections.view + validates positive id, returns serialized launch list reverse-chronological; listForToken(actor, token, limit) same gate + token normalization for "all scans of this sticker" forensic queries; findForReport(actor, reportId) gates inspections.view, returns the launch that produced the report (or null) so the report-detail page can disclose the launch context; serialization deliberately surfaces qr_token_prefix instead of the full token (substr first 8 chars + ellipsis when length > 8) so a leaked API response can't enable a follow-up scan attempt with the captured token — the full token only ever lives in the database and the original physical sticker; client_meta is JSON-encoded with a 4000-char cap so a malicious or buggy client can't fill the database with arbitrary blob payloads, and JSON-decoded on serialization back so callers see structured data not opaque strings; redactToken doubles as the audit-log token form so even the audit_logs table never holds a full scannable token (defense-in-depth — the audit log is itself a target for credential mining); InspectionCompletionService.start extended with one new INSERT field (site_asset_id) plumbed from the payload via !empty cast (matching the existing nullable-int handling of vehicle_id/estimate_id/appointment_id), and mapReport extended with one new row hydration line so find() returns the asset id back to the caller — these two changes are additive and don't touch any existing field handling so all existing start/find consumers keep working with NULL site_asset_id (which is what they always implicitly had); InspectionQrLaunchController is a thin four-method HTTP facade (preview/launch/listForAsset/findForReport — one-liner delegation per method); four new routes under the existing /api/inspections staff-auth group: POST /api/inspections/qr/preview (body: {token, client_meta?}) returns the rich preview shape and persists the scanned-only row, POST /api/inspections/qr/launch (body: {token, template_id, customer_id?, vehicle_id?, estimate_id?, appointment_id?, summary?, client_meta?}) creates the draft report and returns [launch, report, asset], GET /api/inspections/qr/launches/asset/{id}?limit= for the per-asset history view used by the asset-detail page, GET /api/inspections/qr/launches/report/{id} for the per-report launch lookup used by the report-detail page; route wiring constructs InspectionQrLaunchRepository + a request-scoped SiteAssetRepository (constructed inline since SiteAssetRepository is not in api.php's existing DI graph) + InspectionQrLaunchService + InspectionQrLaunchController in one block immediately after the risk-trends division route, reusing the inspection_completionService instance constructed earlier (Phase 8.4) so the launch service threads through the same hook chain (auto-generation/escalation/risk-scoring fire on the launched report's eventual completion automatically); HTTP-layer validation in the route handler does basic token + template_id required checks via Response::badRequest before delegating so the service's InvalidArgumentException doesn't surface as a 500; 28-case SQLite-in-memory behavior test (InspectionQrLaunchServiceTest.php) uses QrLaunchInMemoryConnection + QrLaunchPermissiveGate + QrLaunchFakeAudit + a full schema set including divisions/users/customers/sites + the full Phase 2.x site_assets schema (every column matched so the SiteAssetRepository's COLUMNS-driven SELECT works without column-not-found errors) + inspection_templates with division_id from Phase 0.x + inspection_reports with site_asset_id from this migration + the new inspection_qr_launches table; coverage: previewByToken happy path resolves token + returns asset + filters templates by division (matching division-id template + global template included, other-division template excluded, inactive template excluded), preview persists a status=preview launch row + audit log, preview accepts and persists client_meta as JSON-encoded blob, preview unresolved-token rejects with InvalidArgumentException + persists a status=failed launch row + emits unresolved_token audit, preview invalid-token-format rejection (non-hex chars), preview returns recent_inspections joined by site_asset_id (verified with a pre-seeded completed report), preview no-division asset shows all active templates (no division filter), preview lowercases token before lookup (uppercase scanner input matches lowercase-stored token), preview emits redacted token in serialization (qr_token_prefix is <=9 chars and starts with the token's first 8 hex digits — full token never leaks), preview audit log uses redacted token form; launchFromToken happy path creates a draft report with site_asset_id propagated + customer_id auto-resolved from the asset's site.customer_id + status=draft + status=started launch row + audit emission, launch attaches report to the existing preview row (single launch row per launch attempt, not duplicated), explicit customer_id in payload overrides the site auto-resolution, optional vehicle_id + summary fields propagate to the report, launch unresolved-token rejects + persists status=failed launch row, launch inactive (status=retired) asset rejects with status=aborted launch row + notes mentioning the asset status, launch out-of-division template rejects with status=aborted launch row + notes mentioning the mismatch + the rejected template_id captured for forensic triage, launch inactive template rejects, launch global (NULL division) template works on any-division asset, launch zero-templateid rejected; listForAsset returns reverse-chronological + non-positive id rejected; findForReport returns launch or null for unknown report; listForToken returns history filtered by token; gate denials: previewByToken blocked without inspections.view, launchFromToken blocked without inspections.update, listForAsset/findForReport blocked without inspections.view; skips cleanly on environments without pdo_sqlite (same guard as all prior phases); all seven new/modified files lint green via php -l + autoload-verified via ReflectionClass instantiation against the project autoloader)

### Phase 9 — Capital Planner
- [x] Aging asset report (from Phase 2) _(9.1 — `AgingAssetReportService` + `CapitalPlanController` under `src/Services/CapitalPlan/`; `SiteAssetRepository::listForReport` joins `sites` so cross-site rollups land in one query, hard-capped at 10k assets and excluding `retired`/`decommissioned` by default. Three scopes — `reportForCompany` / `reportForDivision` / `reportForPortfolio` — all reuse `AssetLifecycleService::scoreAsset` so the ok/watch/action/urgent thresholds match the per-site Phase 2.5 view. New surface is the **capex_horizon** roll-up (`overdue` / `next_12mo` / `next_24mo` / `next_60mo` / `beyond_or_unscheduled`) bucketed off the asset's `replace_by_date`, plus a **by_site** breakdown sorted urgent→action→max_risk and a top-25 highest-risk asset list with site context. Read perms: `assets.view` for company/division scopes (delegated to existing AssetController gate), new `capital_plans.view` for portfolio-wide scope (added to manager + accounting; admin via `*`). New module wired via `routes/modules/capital_plan.php` → 3 endpoints (`GET /api/capital-plan/aging/{company,division}/{id}`, `GET /api/capital-plan/aging/portfolio`). Test coverage: 19-case `AgingAssetReportServiceTest` (SQLite-gated, consistent with prior phases) — company/division/portfolio scope filtering, retired-asset exclusion, all five capex buckets, by_site sort order, top-risk cap + sort, category attribution agreeing with lifecycle service, empty-scope and unknown-scope graceful handling, `assets.view`/`capital_plans.view` gate denials, generated_at ISO timestamp.)_
- [x] Lifecycle scoring model + replacement estimates _(9.2 — migration 138 `capital_scoring_models` (division_id NULL = global, weights + thresholds + annual_inflation_rate, idempotent + FK guarded). New `CapitalScoringModel` model + `CapitalScoringModelRepository` with a 5-step resolution chain (division-default → any-division-row → global-default → any-global-row → built-in `fallback()` matching historical Phase 2.5 numbers) and a transactional `setDefault` that clears `is_default` on siblings within the same `(division_id)` cohort only. Repository clamps weights to [0, 1], thresholds to [0, 100], inflation to [-0.5, 0.5]; `normalizedWeights()` re-scales to sum=1.0 so a hand-edited row can't push risk past 100. `AssetLifecycleService::scoreAsset` and `reportForSite` accept an optional `?CapitalScoringModel`, drive risk math from `normalizedWeights()`, and pull category bounds from the model's thresholds; built-in fallback preserves backward compat with all existing callers. New `projectedReplacementCostCents(baseCents, yearsOut, ?model)` forward-projects stored estimates by `(1 + annual_inflation_rate) ^ years` (years clamped 0–100). `AgingAssetReportService` resolves the model per scope (division-specific for division reports, global for company/portfolio), surfaces `scoring_model{id, name, division_id, weights, thresholds, annual_inflation_rate}` in every payload, attaches `projected_replacement_cents` to each scored row, and adds a parallel `capex_horizon_projected` summary for inflated dollars. Per-site lifecycle endpoint (`GET /api/sites/{id}/assets/lifecycle`) now resolves the site's division → model so the per-site view stays consistent with the aging report's categorization. New routes: `GET/POST /api/capital-plan/scoring-models`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/capital-plan/scoring-models/{id}`, `POST /api/capital-plan/scoring-models/{id}/default`. Read perm `capital_plans.view`, write perm `capital_plans.manage` (manager + admin via `*`; accounting reads only). Test coverage: 27-case `CapitalScoringModelTest` (SQLite-gated) — fallback model defaults, normalized weights including zero-sum fallback, repo create/update/delete with weight + threshold + inflation clamping, all five resolution-order paths, setDefault scoping (siblings cleared, other divisions untouched, global cohort handling), AssetLifecycleService driven by custom thresholds + custom weights, projectedReplacementCostCents at default + custom rates, null-base / nonpositive-base / extreme-years-out edge cases, AgingAssetReportService picks up division-specific model + surfaces metadata + populates projected horizon, and 5 controller permission/validation gates including round-tripped setDefault.)_
- [x] Multi-year budget planner + what-if scenarios _(9.3 — migration 139 lays down three idempotent tables: `capital_plans` (id, name, scope_type='company'|'division'|'portfolio', scope_id NULL for portfolio, base_year SMALLINT, horizon_years TINYINT default 5, scoring_model_id nullable so a planner can pin a model snapshot independent of the global default, status='draft'|'published'|'archived' for the lock-baseline-then-share lifecycle, notes, created_by_user_id, timestamps; FKs to `capital_scoring_models` + `users` guarded on referenced-table existence so the migration runs cleanly on partially-set-up DBs); `capital_plan_scenarios` (capital_plan_id with CASCADE delete so a plan deletion sweeps all its variants, name, is_baseline TINYINT for the auto-minted untouchable variant, global_options TEXT JSON for cross-cutting transforms, notes, timestamps; UNIQUE (capital_plan_id, name) so the planner can't accidentally double-name a what-if); `capital_plan_scenario_overrides` (scenario_id CASCADE, site_asset_id, defer_months INT (negative pulls forward), pin_to_year SMALLINT (mutually exclusive with defer — pin wins), replacement_estimate_cents_override INT (forces a dollar amount that supersedes the asset row's stored estimate), excluded TINYINT (drop the asset from the scenario entirely), notes, created_at; UNIQUE (scenario_id, site_asset_id) so the upsert path collapses double-toggles into a single row; FK to site_assets guarded on table existence). Three new typed `BaseModel` subclasses — `CapitalPlan` with SCOPE_/STATUS_ const vocab, `CapitalPlanScenario` carrying the JSON-decoded `global_options` array, `CapitalPlanScenarioOverride` (booleans hydrated explicitly because BaseModel's bool cast doesn't trigger on int-from-DB without an explicit hydrate step). `CapitalPlanRepository` follows the COLUMNS const + private hydrate pattern with three logical surfaces (plans, scenarios, overrides) all sharing one PDO connection; plan listing supports scope filters + status + paginated limit/offset (default 100, capped 500); `findBaselineScenario(planId)` is a one-row helper for the Baseline lookup; `upsertOverride(scenarioId, assetId, data)` does find-then-insert-or-update so toggling the same asset twice replaces the prior row instead of duplicating it (the UNIQUE backstop catches a race even if the application-level check loses); `encodeOptions` accepts arrays OR pre-encoded JSON strings (validated to parse before persisting) so future API/CLI callers have flexibility; `nullableString` collapses empty strings to NULL so blank notes don't leave artifact whitespace. `CapitalPlanService` is the orchestration heart of 9.3 with eleven public methods: `createPlan` gates `capital_plans.manage`, validates scope_type vocabulary + scope_id-required-for-non-portfolio rule, persists the plan, then **auto-mints the Baseline scenario** in the same call so the planner can immediately compute without a separate POST (Baseline carries `is_baseline=1` and a fixed name; downstream writes go to new scenarios, not Baseline); `updatePlan` re-validates scope when scope fields change so an in-flight rename to an invalid scope can't slip through; `deletePlan` cascades through the FK chain (plan → scenarios → overrides) at the DB layer so the service just issues the top-level DELETE; `addScenario` strips any user-supplied `is_baseline` flag because Baseline is a system-controlled artifact (only the auto-mint at plan create is allowed to set it) — silent strip rather than reject because the field is informational, not load-bearing; `deleteScenario` rejects Baseline with an explicit error so the auto-mint can never disappear out from under the plan; `setOverride` also rejects Baseline as the override target (overrides are what-if mutations and Baseline is the un-mutated reference) — both protections funnel planners toward the correct mental model; `computeScenario` is the chart-payload builder, gated `capital_plans.view` (read perm because computing doesn't mutate state — same gate as listing). The compute pipeline: (1) loads scope assets via `SiteAssetRepository::listForReport` honoring company_id/division_id filters with portfolio = no filter (limit 10000 same ceiling as the aging report so the planner can't silently truncate at a lower number); (2) resolves the scoring model via three-step chain (plan.scoring_model_id explicit → division-active model for division-scoped plans → global active model); (3) honors `global_options.inflation_rate_override` by cloning the model with a swapped rate clamped to [-0.5, 0.5] same bound the repo enforces; (4) for each asset: scores via `AssetLifecycleService::scoreAsset` to get category + replace_by_date, looks up the override (excluded → skip + bump excluded counter, else compute replace_year via priority chain pin → defer_months → defer_all_months, then `accelerate_urgent_to_year` only pulls urgent items *forward* never delays), resolves the estimate (override.replacement_estimate_cents_override wins over asset row's value), projects to the spend year via `lifecycle->projectedReplacementCostCents` using the inflation-overridden model; (5) buckets into `years[base_year..base_year+horizon-1]` plus `overdue` (replace_year < base_year) and `beyond` (replace_year > end_year) tail buckets; (6) emits both `raw_cents` (today's dollars) and `projected_cents` (inflated to spend year) per bucket so the UI can chart either lens. The compute payload includes a `counts` block (assets_in_scope, excluded, pinned, deferred, with_overrides) so the planner can confirm at-a-glance which knobs are touching how many assets — operational signal that prevents "I made a what-if and nothing changed; did my override save?" frustration. `compareScenarios(planId, scenarioIds=[])` is the convenience wrapper for the side-by-side compare view: empty `scenarioIds` returns every scenario for the plan including Baseline, non-empty filters to the requested subset; both shapes return scenarios as a list under `data.scenarios` so the UI can iterate uniformly. Scenario `global_options` round-trip as PHP arrays (BaseModel decodes the JSON column on hydrate via `?array` typing) so the service layer can read `$scenario->global_options['defer_all_months']` directly without a re-decode step — the three recognized keys are `defer_all_months` (int months, applies to non-overridden + non-pinned assets), `accelerate_urgent_to_year` (clamped to >= base_year, only pulls forward), `inflation_rate_override` (clamped to [-0.5, 0.5]); unknown keys are silently ignored so additive future options don't break existing scenarios. `CapitalPlanController` extended with twelve thin facade methods covering plan CRUD + scenario add/update/delete + override list/set/remove + computeScenario + compareScenarios; the previously-optional `?CapitalPlanService` constructor param is now wired in route registration so all 9.3 endpoints have access. Thirteen new routes under the existing /api/capital-plan group: `GET /api/capital-plan/plans` (filter scope_type/status, paginated), `POST /api/capital-plan/plans` (auto-creates Baseline), `GET /api/capital-plan/plans/{id}` (returns plan + scenarios bundle so the planner UI can render the variant tabs in one call), `PUT /api/capital-plan/plans/{id}`, `DELETE /api/capital-plan/plans/{id}`, `POST /api/capital-plan/plans/{id}/scenarios`, `PUT /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}`, `DELETE /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}` (Baseline-protected at the service layer), `GET /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}/overrides`, `POST /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}/overrides` (upsert by site_asset_id from body), `DELETE /api/capital-plan/overrides/{id}`, `GET /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}/compute` (the primary chart endpoint), `GET /api/capital-plan/plans/{id}/compare?scenario_ids=1,2,3` (side-by-side; CSV scenario_ids parsed + numeric-only filtered so a malicious `id=1;DROP TABLE` token gets dropped at the route layer not the SQL layer). Read perm `capital_plans.view`, write perm `capital_plans.manage` (manager + admin via `*`; accounting reads compute/compare results but cannot mutate plans). Test coverage: 28-case `CapitalPlanServiceTest` (SQLite-gated, consistent with prior phases) — plan lifecycle (auto-Baseline minted at create, scope_type vocabulary enforced, scope_id required for non-portfolio, portfolio allows null scope_id, manage perm gating, view perm gating on list, update persists changes, delete actually removes); scenario CRUD (is_baseline flag stripped from user payloads, Baseline cannot be deleted, non-Baseline can be deleted, global_options round-trip as array, override-on-Baseline rejected); compute math (baseline buckets by replace_year correctly across multi-year horizons, overdue assets land in overdue bucket, far-future assets land in beyond bucket, company scope excludes other companies, division scope excludes other divisions, retired assets excluded from compute even within scope); per-asset overrides (excluded drops asset from totals + bumps counter, pin_to_year overrides baseline year + records pin in overrides_applied, defer_months shifts replace_year correctly across month boundaries, estimate override replaces asset estimate, pin wins when both pin + defer set, upsertOverride overwrites previous instead of duplicating); global options (defer_all_months shifts unoverridden assets, per-asset override takes priority over global defer, accelerate_urgent_to_year pulls only urgent forward, accelerate never delays an already-earlier asset); inflation projection (scoring model rate honored: 100k * 1.10^2 = 121000, scenario inflation_rate_override replaces model rate: 100k * 1.20^2 = 144000); compare (returns all scenarios when no filter, filters to requested subset); scoring model resolution (explicit scoring_model_id wins, division plan picks up division-active model); counts metadata (assets_in_scope + pinned + deferred + excluded + with_overrides all populated correctly across mixed override types). All seven new/modified files lint green via `php -l`; autoload regenerated successfully (composer dump-autoload -o reports 923 classes).)_
- [x] Customer-facing recommendation PDF _(9.4 — `CapitalRecommendationPdfGenerator` extends the existing `PdfService` (the same dompdf-backed base class that powers Phase 1's invoice PDF, Phase 5's lien notice + storage form PDFs, and Phase 8.x's inspection report PDF) so the new generator inherits the proven HTML→PDF rendering path, the shared CSS baseline (header/table/status pill scaffolding the other three generators already lean on), and the same dompdf/Options bootstrap; the constructor explicitly delegates up to `PdfService::__construct()` so the standard "dompdf not installed" RuntimeException fires at construction time rather than at render time, matching the discovery semantics every other generator already exposes. Crucially the generator is **pure data-in, HTML/PDF-out** — no Connection, no PDO, no settings repo coupling — because the input it consumes (`array $payload` shaped exactly like `CapitalPlanService::computeScenario`'s return value) already carries every field the document needs (plan/scenario/scoring_model identity, year-bucketed totals, asset lines with category + risk + bucket + projection); this design choice deliberately mirrors `LienNoticePdfGenerator`'s pre-computed-input pattern (vs. `InspectionPdfGenerator`'s connection-coupled pattern) so the same generator can render a live scenario today, a saved snapshot tomorrow, or a hand-built fixture in tests without standing up the database or the planner service. Two public methods: `generate(payload, settings, ?scopeLabel)` returns the binary PDF (calls dompdf), and `buildHtml(payload, settings, ?scopeLabel)` returns the rendered HTML — `buildHtml` is public on purpose so tests can assert on rendered structure without dompdf installed (the test bootstrap doesn't require dompdf to exercise the document layout, which is where 90% of regression risk lives anyway). Document layout is five sections in single-column flow: (1) **header band** with the shop's name + address + phone on the left and "CAPITAL REPLACEMENT RECOMMENDATION" + plan name + scenario name + generated_at timestamp on the right (Baseline scenarios get a small `badge-baseline` pill next to the scenario name so a customer reading the doc immediately knows whether they're looking at the un-mutated reference baseline or a what-if variant — the visual distinction matters because Baseline carries no opinion while a custom scenario is the operator's recommended plan); (2) **executive summary** in a left-bordered callout with two columns — left side carries scope (auto-described from `scope_type`/`scope_id` as "Entire portfolio", "Company #N", or "Division #N" when no human label is supplied; the route layer honors a `?scope_label=` query param so an integration-aware caller can pass the resolved name — "Acme Industries — Portland Plant" — instead of the bare scope id), planning horizon as a year range, the scoring model's annual inflation rate as a pct, and asset count; right side carries total replacement (today's dollars), total projected (inflated), and applied-overrides counts broken out by pinned/deferred/excluded so a customer can see at a glance "this scenario touches 3 of your 47 assets"; below the two-column summary is a category mix strip with four colored pills (Urgent/Action/Watch/OK counts) computed from the asset lines so the document shows the operational risk distribution before drilling into specific assets — this is the at-a-glance "is the fleet healthy" header signal; (3) **year-by-year capex breakdown table** with four columns (Period | Assets | Today's Dollars | Projected) and three logical row types — an Overdue row at the top (highlighted with a soft red background so the reader's eye lands on past-due replacements first; row is suppressed entirely when `overdue.asset_count == 0` because an empty overdue row reads as alarming when there's no actual problem), one row per horizon year (the years[] list comes from compute in absolute-year keys so they sort naturally), a Beyond row at the bottom (also conditionally suppressed) labeled with the actual end-of-horizon year so "Beyond horizon (after 2030)" is unambiguous, and a Total footer row that picks up totals.asset_count + totals.raw_cents + totals.projected_cents — note the totals come from the compute payload, not summed at PDF time, so the document and the planner UI are guaranteed to agree byte-for-byte on the totals line; (4) **priority replacements table** showing the top-25 highest-priority asset lines (TOP_RECOMMENDATIONS_LIMIT const so callers can introspect the cap), sorted by a deterministic priority key — primary sort by category rank (urgent=4 > action=3 > watch=2 > ok=1) so an urgent low-risk-score asset still beats a watch high-risk-score asset (a customer reading the doc shouldn't see a "watch" item ranked above an "urgent" one — that contradicts the operational categorization the rest of the system uses), secondary sort by raw risk score desc, tertiary tie-break by projected_cents desc so when two equally-risky assets land in the same row a more expensive replacement floats up; columns are Asset | Site | Category (colored pill matching the executive summary palette so visual continuity carries through) | Replace Year (uses the bucket label "Overdue"/"Beyond" instead of the literal year for tail-bucket assets so a 2024 overdue asset shows as "Overdue" not "2024" — the human-meaningful label) | Projected Cost (dash when has_estimate is false, money formatted otherwise — the explicit dash signals "we don't know what this will cost yet" rather than misleadingly showing $0); each row carries a `*` mark next to the asset name when `has_override == true` with a tooltip-equivalent legend at the bottom of the section ("* Asset has a scenario-specific override — pinned year, deferred, or custom estimate") so a customer flipping to this section can immediately tell which line items the operator hand-touched vs. which fall out of the raw lifecycle scoring; (5) **footer disclaimer** — the legally-important line that explicitly states the recommendation is a projection ("Actual replacement timing may vary based on operational performance, vendor availability, and budget approvals") plus a re-statement of the inflation assumption used for the projected column ("an annual inflation rate of N%") so a reader reviewing the projected column has the assumption in their hand, not three pages back; this footer scope is intentional — it never makes promises, it always reaffirms the math, and it never references any internal terminology a customer wouldn't recognize. CSS lives in two style blocks: the `getBaseStyles()` already defined on PdfService (gives us font/header/table/totals/status-pill defaults) plus a `getRecommendationStyles()` override that adds the four category pills (urgent=red/action=orange/watch=yellow/ok=green matching the planner UI's palette so a customer who's seen the screen recognizes the document's color language), the baseline badge, the row-overdue/row-beyond/row-total backgrounds, and the override-mark + override-legend styling; both blocks are appended to the document head so Dompdf has all rule definitions before it begins layout. **HTML escaping**: every text-bearing field flows through a `private esc()` wrapper around `htmlspecialchars(ENT_QUOTES | ENT_SUBSTITUTE, 'UTF-8')` so a maliciously named asset ("Compressor</td><td>EXFIL"), a hostile shop name pulled from settings, or a deliberately exotic plan name with `<script>` tags can't break out of the document structure; this is defense-in-depth — the planner already validates names on write, but the PDF generator treats every input as untrusted so a future bypass at the persistence layer doesn't become a stored-XSS-in-PDF vulnerability or, worse, a DOMPDF parser crash. **Money formatting** uses `number_format($cents/100, 2, '.', ',')` with explicit handling for negatives (renders as `-$150.00` not `$-150.00`) so a future refund-style override (negative replacement cost) renders cleanly; this matters because while the current scenario compute can't produce negative dollar amounts, the override schema permits a negative `replacement_estimate_cents_override` and a planner editing in a salvage credit shouldn't crash or render confusingly. `CapitalPlanService` constructor extended with an optional `?CapitalRecommendationPdfGenerator $pdfGenerator = null` (default null preserves backward compat for any caller still constructing the service with five args — there are none in the codebase but the optional-default pattern matches the rest of Phase 9's service evolution); new public method `renderRecommendationPdf(actor, scenarioId, shopSettings, ?scopeLabel, ?now)` first verifies the generator was wired (LogicException if not — fast-fail with a developer-actionable message rather than a confusing null deref deep inside dompdf), then calls `computeScenario(actor, scenarioId, now)` (which itself gates `capital_plans.view` so no separate gate call is needed at the PDF entry point — same gate/scope/error semantics as the JSON compute endpoint), then hands the payload to `pdfGenerator->generate()` and returns the binary PDF body. **The pivot**: the PDF reuses computeScenario verbatim. This is the load-bearing design choice — there's no parallel code path computing year buckets for the PDF, no separate sort logic, no PDF-only inflation pass. The document and the chart consume the exact same data structure at the exact same moment, which guarantees they agree byte-for-byte and eliminates an entire class of "the chart said $500k but the PDF said $480k" support tickets that would otherwise be inevitable. `CapitalPlanController` extended with one new thin facade method `recommendationPdf(actor, scenarioId, shopSettings, ?scopeLabel)` that delegates to the service after the standard `requirePlannerDeps()` guard. Wire-up in `routes/modules/capital_plan.php`: the generator is constructed in a try/catch so an environment without dompdf (some lightweight CI containers, dev sandboxes) doesn't crash the entire route module's bootstrap — failed construction leaves `$recommendationPdf = null` which is then passed through to the service; a request to the recommendation endpoint in such an environment will surface the generator's LogicException as a 500 (acceptable: dompdf is a hard runtime dependency for this endpoint, but the rest of Phase 9 — aging report, scoring models, plans, scenarios, compute — keeps working). One new route under the existing /api/capital-plan group: `GET /api/capital-plan/scenarios/{id}/recommendation.pdf` (the `.pdf` extension in the path is a UX affordance — when a browser hits this URL the inline-disposition + Content-Type let the user save with a meaningful filename rather than a numeric scenario id alone; matches the inspection PDF route's `/api/inspections/{id}/pdf` precedent). The route handler reads three shop fields (shop.name, shop.address, shop.phone) from the SettingsRepository with config fallback (matches the inspection PDF route pattern and the wider settings precedence the rest of the codebase already established — DB settings beat config defaults), accepts an optional `?scope_label=` query param (string only, empty-string treated as null so a `&scope_label=` with no value falls back to the auto-described scope rather than rendering an empty header), then returns `Response::make($pdf, 200, ['Content-Type' => 'application/pdf', 'Content-Disposition' => 'inline; filename="capital-recommendation-{id}.pdf"'])` — inline disposition (vs. attachment) so the customer's browser previews the PDF in-place which is the better UX for a one-off recommendation read; if a tenant wants force-download semantics they can flip the disposition with one line, but the default is the gentler experience. Read perm `capital_plans.view` (delegated through computeScenario's gate — accounting can pull recommendation PDFs without `manage`, which is the right scoping because reading a projection isn't a mutation). Test coverage: 62-assertion `CapitalRecommendationPdfTest` (no SQLite or dompdf required — the test exercises `buildHtml()` directly via a `FakeGenerator` subclass that skips the parent's dompdf-availability check, plus reflection-based assertions on the service/controller wiring); coverage spans header content (shop name/address/phone + plan + scenario + generated timestamp formatting), the Baseline badge appearing only for `is_baseline=true` (regression test for not just rendering "badge-baseline" anywhere in the doc — the assertion explicitly checks for the inline `class="badge badge-baseline"` because the CSS rule itself contains the same substring; this is the kind of test that catches a future refactor that accidentally always renders the badge), executive summary surfacing horizon range + length-in-years + inflation pct + asset count + total raw + total projected + override count breakdown (pinned/deferred/excluded with the exact format string the current document uses), category mix pills counting from the asset lines (verified for all four categories with a fixture that mixes urgent/action/watch/ok), year-by-year table including overdue + every horizon year + beyond + grand total, conditional suppression of overdue and beyond rows when their asset_count is zero (regression test against the "this scenario has no overdue assets but the doc still shows an Overdue row with $0" misleading display), top-recommendations section (header text, sort priority — verified explicitly that highest-risk urgent floats above lower-risk urgent which floats above any action which floats above any watch regardless of risk score), tail-bucket label rendering (overdue assets show "Overdue" instead of their literal pre-base-year, beyond assets show "Beyond" instead of their literal post-end-year), override mark presence + legend section, top-recommendations cap honored at TOP_RECOMMENDATIONS_LIMIT (40-asset fixture verifies exactly 25 rows render, not 24, not 26 — which catches both off-by-one and a future change to the limit constant that the test would then need updating with), category-rank-beats-risk-score sort verified against a fixture where the urgent asset has the lowest risk score (10) and the watch asset has the highest (99) and yet the urgent still floats above the watch (this is the case where naive risk-only sort would invert), explicit `?scope_label=` query value rendered in place of the auto-description, all three auto-described scope types (portfolio/division/company) verified, HTML escaping (script tags + ampersands + bold tags in plan name + scenario name + shop name all converted to entities — verified positively that the literal injection text does NOT appear in the rendered HTML and verified positively that the entity-encoded form DOES), footer disclaimer present + carries inflation rate, negative-cents formatting (sign placement before $), missing-estimate row renders em-dash for projected cost, and reflection-based assertions on the service constructor (pdfGenerator param exists, is optional, defaults to null) + service method signature (renderRecommendationPdf is public, accepts actor + scenarioId + shopSettings + scopeLabel) + controller method existence (recommendationPdf method present + public). The reflection layer is intentional: it gives us the wiring guarantees without needing a real PDO + sqlite stack just to verify the service constructor accepts the new optional dep — exactly the right level of test for a wiring change. All four new/modified files lint green via `php -l`; autoload regenerated successfully (composer dump-autoload -o reports 924 classes); CapitalRecommendationPdfGenerator + CapitalPlanService + CapitalPlanController all loadable via ReflectionClass against the project autoloader. Phase 9 is complete — aging asset report (9.1) → tunable lifecycle scoring + replacement estimates (9.2) → multi-year budget planner with what-if scenarios + overrides + global options (9.3) → customer-facing recommendation PDF that closes the loop by handing the operator a printable artifact to share with stakeholders (9.4). The capital planner module now spans data ingest (asset lifecycle scoring), modeling (per-division weights/thresholds/inflation), planning (multi-year scenarios with overrides), and customer delivery (recommendation PDF) — every layer the original spec called for is shipped.)_

### Phase 10 — Smart additions & polish
- [x] Subcontractor management _(10.1 — migration 140 lays down three idempotent tables: `subcontractors` (vendor master with company_name VARCHAR(160) required, contact_name + email + phone + full address block + tax_id (W9/EIN) + payment_terms + hourly_rate_cents nullable, status enum-shape `active`|`inactive`|`suspended`, insurance_expires_at DATE for COI tracking, notes TEXT, timestamps; three indexes — status for the assignability filter the WO routing UI runs every page-load, insurance_expires_at so the dashboard "subs with expired COI" widget can scan without a table sweep, and company_name for the search-as-you-type picker — explicitly chosen because the alphabetical sort path will be the hot read pattern and a BTREE index covers both equality search and LIKE-prefix queries cheaply); `subcontractor_divisions` M2M join (subcontractor_id CASCADE so deleting a vendor sweeps their division approvals automatically, division_id with FK guarded on the `divisions` table existing so the migration runs cleanly on a partially-set-up DB, UNIQUE (subcontractor_id, division_id) so an accidental double-add via API races collapses into a single approved row, plus a forward index on division_id for the "all subs approved for HVAC" query that the picker runs); `subcontractor_assignments` (subcontractor_id RESTRICT — explicitly chosen over CASCADE because deleting a vendor mid-job should fail loudly so the operator can resolve open assignments first rather than silently orphaning work-order history, workorder_id CASCADE guarded on the `workorders` table existing, status with a six-state vocabulary (pending/accepted/declined/in_progress/completed/cancelled) covering the full sub-lifecycle, lifecycle timestamps assigned_at + accepted_at + declined_at + completed_at + cancelled_at all individually nullable so the audit trail can show "vendor declined at 14:32 on 2026-04-24" without inventing fake timestamps for states the assignment never reached, estimated_cost_cents + actual_cost_cents so finance can reconcile the invoice the sub submits against what was budgeted, description + internal_notes (description is sub-facing scope, internal_notes is staff-only commentary — separated so a future "share assignment with sub via email" flow doesn't leak the internal commentary), assigned_by_user_id SET NULL guarded on `users` so deleting a manager doesn't blow away the assignment audit trail, rating TINYINT (1-5) + rating_notes for the post-completion scorecard that feeds vendor performance reports). Two typed `BaseModel` subclasses — `Subcontractor` carrying STATUSES const for vocabulary validation + the auto-hydrated `division_ids` array (zero-default so a fresh model from the repo always has the field even before the service composes division approvals onto it); `SubcontractorAssignment` carrying STATUSES + an `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` const map (`accepted` ← pending only; `declined` ← pending only; `in_progress` ← pending OR accepted; `completed` ← accepted OR in_progress; `cancelled` ← pending OR accepted OR in_progress) — declarative state machine that the service consults on every transition request so illegal jumps (e.g. completed → pending, declined → completed) get rejected at the validation layer rather than producing nonsensical lifecycle audit trails. `SubcontractorRepository` follows the COLUMNS-const + private-hydrate pattern with three logical surfaces (subcontractors, division approvals, assignments) sharing one PDO connection; `listSubcontractors` supports status + search (LIKE across company_name/contact_name/email — three-column OR with one bound `q` param) + division filter (LEFT JOIN through subcontractor_divisions when division_id present, GROUP BY id so the join doesn't multiply rows) + paginated limit/offset (default 100, capped 500 matching the rest of the codebase); `setDivisionsForSubcontractor` is the atomic replace path — wraps DELETE + per-id INSERT in a transaction, dedupes the input array first to keep the UNIQUE backstop calm, and rolls back on any error so a partial replace can never leave the M2M in an inconsistent half-applied state; assignment writes go through narrow casting helpers (`castAssignmentColumn` for status/cost/rating/notes/timestamp; `castSubColumn` for the vendor block) that collapse empty-string inputs to NULL on nullable columns so blank form submissions don't leave artifact whitespace and don't trigger UNIQUE collisions on the email column when both vendors leave it blank. `SubcontractorService` orchestrates the gates + validation + state machine: `subcontractors.view` for read paths, `subcontractors.manage` for write paths (manager + admin via `*` get both; dispatcher gets both because dispatch is the operational owner of "who is assigned to this work order"; technician gets view-only because techs need to know who's coming but shouldn't be modifying vendor master records or creating assignments; accounting gets view-only for cost reconciliation against vendor invoices). `validateSubcontractorPayload` enforces required-on-create company_name, never-blank-on-update company_name, email format via `filter_var(... FILTER_VALIDATE_EMAIL)` so a malformed email like "jane@@example" gets rejected before it lands in the DB, status vocabulary check against the model's STATUSES const so the same vocabulary is single-sourced, ISO date shape on insurance_expires_at via a regex prefix match (full strict parsing happens at the DB layer, but the regex catches obviously-wrong inputs like "soon" before they reach the prepared statement), and non-negative hourly_rate_cents (a negative rate is operationally meaningless and the schema doesn't sign it). `createSubcontractor` and `updateSubcontractor` both accept a `division_ids` array in the same payload so a single API call atomically creates/updates the vendor record + sets their division approvals — the alternative would be two calls, but two calls means a half-failed create leaves either an unauthorized vendor or orphaned division rows; the combined call wraps both in the repo transaction (insert/update first, then setDivisions which is itself transactional). `createAssignment` is the load-bearing assignability gate: explicitly rejects creating an assignment against a non-active subcontractor (status === 'active' check) so a suspended vendor whose insurance lapsed can't accidentally be pulled into new work, stamps `assigned_by_user_id` from the actor so the audit trail records who created the assignment, forces status='pending' on creation regardless of payload (the only way to skip pending is to immediately transition, which goes through the state-machine path that records the timestamp). `updateAssignment` is the cost/notes/rating mutation path that **explicitly strips** status + accepted_at + declined_at + completed_at + cancelled_at from the input payload — these fields can only be moved through `transitionAssignment` so the lifecycle timestamps and the state machine stay in lockstep; rating is range-validated [1,5] inclusive with explicit error messages so a misclick or a malicious 0/6 doesn't poison the future scorecard view; the strip-then-update pattern means a UI can safely PATCH the whole assignment object without worrying about which fields are mutable through which path, and the service enforces the boundary. `transitionAssignment(actor, id, targetStatus, ?now)` is the state-machine arbiter — rejects unknown statuses, explicitly rejects ever transitioning back to pending (terminal-only-forward semantics: once a sub has acknowledged a job, the audit trail can't pretend they hadn't), consults `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS[targetStatus]` to verify the existing status is in the allowed-from list (so completed → pending, declined → completed, cancelled → in_progress all get rejected with explicit error messages), then on success persists the new status AND the matching lifecycle timestamp via a `match` on the target status (accepted → accepted_at, declined → declined_at, completed → completed_at, cancelled → cancelled_at) so the service is the only writer of those columns and a future audit query can trust they were set atomically with the corresponding state change. The `?DateTimeImmutable $now` param is for tests — production calls leave it null and let the service generate `new DateTimeImmutable()` at transition time; tests pass deterministic timestamps so the assertions can check exact stamp values. `findAssignable(actor, ?divisionId, ?candidateIds)` is the picker-feeder: status-active filter always on, optional division-id filter (consults the M2M so only subs approved for that division surface), optional candidateIds restriction (used by the "you previously worked with these subs" UI surface to highlight known-good vendors first); always composes division_ids onto each returned vendor so the picker can show "approved for: HVAC, Plumbing" badges without a per-row API call. The composition happens via `listDivisionsForSubcontractor` per row — N+1 by design at the read layer because the typical assignable list is < 100 vendors and the per-vendor query is one indexed lookup; if this becomes a hot read path it can be batched into a single GROUP_CONCAT but premature optimization isn't warranted yet. `SubcontractorController` is the thin HTTP facade with eleven methods covering vendor CRUD + assignable picker + per-WO assignment list/create + assignment update/transition/delete; `transitionAssignment` validates that the request body carries a `status` key and raises InvalidArgumentException if absent so the route layer's error middleware can emit a 400 instead of leaking a generic 500; `createAssignment` overwrites any payload-supplied workorder_id with the path-supplied id so a malicious request can't post `{"workorder_id": 999}` to `POST /api/workorders/100/subcontractors` to create the assignment against the wrong WO. New module wired via `routes/modules/subcontractors.php` and registered in `routes/api.php`'s loader array (last entry after `capital_plan`). Eleven routes total: `GET /api/subcontractors` (filters status/search/division_id, paginated), `GET /api/subcontractors/assignable` (active + optional division — picker-feeder), `POST /api/subcontractors`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/subcontractors/{id}`, `GET/POST /api/workorders/{id}/subcontractors` (per-WO assignment list + create — workorder id in path, not body, so URL-keyed authorization can be added later without payload schema changes), `GET /api/subcontractor-assignments` (cross-cutting list — filter by status or subcontractor_id, used by the "show all open subcontractor work" dashboard view), `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/subcontractor-assignments/{id}`, `POST /api/subcontractor-assignments/{id}/transition` (state-machine entry point — body carries `status`). Permission additions to `config/auth.php`: `subcontractors.view` + `subcontractors.manage` added to manager (full owner of the master list); both added to dispatcher (dispatch creates and manages assignments operationally — vendor-master edits via dispatch are intentional because in field-service shops the dispatcher is often the same person tracking sub performance); `subcontractors.view` added to technician (read-only — needs to see who's working their WO but shouldn't mutate vendor records); `subcontractors.view` added to accounting (read-only — reads cost reconciliation data without write access). Test coverage: 28-case `SubcontractorServiceTest` (SQLite-gated, consistent with prior phases) — model status const vocabulary + ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS map shape, repo CRUD round-trip (create + find + update preserves untouched columns + delete), invalid-status normalization to 'active' default, division M2M atomic replace (set [1,2] → [2,3] verifies old rows truly removed, set [] wipes all, dedupe collapses [1,1,2,2,2] → [1,2]), service validation gates (company_name required on create + never-blank on update, malformed email rejected, unknown status rejected, bad date shape rejected, negative rate rejected), service composes division_ids on the returned model after create, both view + manage permission denials surface as UnauthorizedException at the service boundary, assignability filter excludes inactive + suspended subs (only 'active' returned), assignability filter scopes to division when supplied (HVAC sub only surfaces when division=1, doesn't leak to division=2 or 3), assignment creation rejects suspended subs with explicit error, assignment creation stamps assigned_by_user_id from actor, transition pending→accepted stamps accepted_at + leaves declined_at null, transition pending→declined stamps declined_at, full happy-path lifecycle accepted→in_progress→completed all stamping the right timestamps, illegal transition completed→pending rejected, illegal transition declined→completed rejected (declined is operationally terminal — operator must reassign rather than resurrect), cancellation reachable from pending OR accepted OR in_progress with cancelled_at stamped in all three cases, updateAssignment strips status + lifecycle timestamps from the payload (verified by explicitly trying to update status='completed' through the update path and confirming it stays pending), rating range validation [1,5] inclusive (0 and 6 both rejected, valid 4 + rating_notes accepted and persisted), per-workorder assignment list groups correctly (3 assignments split 2/1 across two WOs), controller returns the data envelope shape consistent with the rest of the API, controller transitionAssignment requires status in body (raises InvalidArgumentException when omitted), controller createAssignment uses the path workorder_id and ignores any body workorder_id (body 999 + path 101 → assignment created against 101), list filters by status and search (active filter + Acme search + Plumbing search all return correct counts). All eight new/modified files lint green via `php -l`; autoload regenerated successfully — Subcontractor + SubcontractorAssignment + SubcontractorRepository + SubcontractorService + SubcontractorController all loadable via ReflectionClass against the project autoloader.)_
- [x] Change order workflow _(10.2 — migration 141 lays down two idempotent tables: `workorder_change_orders` (workorder_id INT NOT NULL with FK CASCADE so deleting the parent WO sweeps its CO graph automatically — guarded on the `workorders` table existing so the migration runs cleanly on a partially-set-up DB;
