The SsoService orchestrates a strict three-step OIDC flow: `startLogin($slug, $redirectUri)` validates the provider is active + OIDC + has the required config (client_id, authorize_endpoint, redirect_uri all non-null — service throws RuntimeException with a config-error message if any are missing so the failure is a 4xx-shaped operator error, not a 5xx surprise), generates 24-byte state + 24-byte nonce via random_bytes/bin2hex, persists a pending sso_login_attempts row, builds the authorize URL with all required params via http_build_query (so query encoding is correct against IdPs that don't tolerate raw `&` or unescaped `:` in custom redirect_uri values), returns an array of {provider, attempt, authorize_url, state} for the caller to issue a 302; `handleCallback($state, $code)` looks up the attempt by state and rejects with InvalidArgumentException for unknown state (CSRF / expired session), rejects with InvalidArgumentException for status≠pending (replay protection — a successful callback transitions status to 'completed' and a second call to the same state finds it already-consumed), exchanges code for token via OidcHttpClient.postForm against token_endpoint, fetches userinfo via OidcHttpClient.getJson against userinfo_endpoint with Bearer access_token, resolves the local user with strict priority order (existing link → email match against active local users → auto_provision if provider.auto_provision=1 AND email is present, else reject with RuntimeException to fail closed because silently provisioning unverified users would be a security hole), upserts the SsoUserLink with sync_profile_on_login behavior controlling whether email/display_name get refreshed from the latest claims, stamps the attempt completed; `unlink($user, $linkId)` is the user-facing self-service revoke, ownership-checked so attempting to unlink someone else's link returns false rather than 403 (consistent with the rest of the user/me surface). The OidcHttpClient is a thin curl wrapper extracted as its own class so tests can substitute SsoStubHttpClient with fixture token + userinfo arrays — no PSR-7 / Guzzle dependency for two HTTP calls, and the stub-ability is the whole point of the seam; postForm uses CURLOPT_TIMEOUT=15 (long enough for slow IdPs, short enough that a stuck IdP doesn't block PHP-FPM workers indefinitely), CURLOPT_FOLLOWLOCATION=false (token endpoints don't redirect — a redirect from a token endpoint is suspicious and should fail rather than be followed), application/x-www-form-urlencoded body shape since that's the OIDC spec for token exchange, optional Basic auth header for IdPs that prefer client credentials in the Authorization header rather than the body. TrustedDeviceService.issue mints a 32-byte (256-bit) random token via random_bytes, hashes with sha256 before persisting, returns the raw token to the caller for cookie-set with strict ttlDays validation (must be ≥ 1) and label/user_agent/ip_address forensics; verify() is a four-gate predicate (token must hash to a known row, row must belong to the asserting user, row must not be revoked, row.expires_at must not have passed) — any miss returns null rather than throwing because verify() is called on every authed request and exception flow control there would be expensive; revoke() and revokeAllForUser() allow self-service revocation, with the gate falling back to users.update for admin-initiated force-revoke (e.g., post-phishing-report from an end user reporting a stolen laptop). The SsoController exposes three audiences: public (listActiveProviders projects only slug+name+type so client_secret/endpoints never leak to anonymous users on the login screen, start hands back authorize_url + state, callback exchanges + resolves user + issues a fresh JWT pair via JwtService.generateToken+generateRefreshToken+setTokenCookies so SSO and password logins return identical envelopes for the React shell — the consumer doesn't need to know which path the user took), authenticated user (listMyLinks + unlinkMyLink for the /settings/security page so users can prune their own connections), and admin (listAll/get/create/update/delete with sso_providers.view + sso_providers.manage gates; serializeProviderForAdmin masks client_secret as has_client_secret boolean so admins can see whether one is configured without exposing the value — the secret is write-only after first save). TrustedDeviceController exposes self-service list/revoke/revokeAll plus admin-list/admin-revokeAll behind trusted_devices.manage, and never serializes token_hash to the wire even though it's only an SHA — defense in depth so a leaked admin response page can't be combined with a DB dump for offline correlation. Permissions added to config/auth.php (manager gets sso_providers.view+manage and trusted_devices.manage so shop managers can configure the IdP for their location and force-revoke trust tokens after a phishing report; admin already has '*' so implicitly covered; other roles have no SSO admin surface — end users still get self-service via the /api/users/me/* endpoints which only require Middleware::auth(), no specific permission). The route modules `routes/modules/sso.php` and `routes/modules/trusted_devices.php` are registered in routes/api.php loader array. SSO exposes 9 endpoints: 3 public (GET /api/auth/sso/providers list, POST /api/auth/sso/{slug}/start kick off, GET /api/auth/sso/callback finish), 2 authed user (GET/DELETE /api/users/me/sso-links), 5 admin (full CRUD on /api/admin/sso/providers). Trusted devices exposes 5 endpoints: 3 self-service (GET /api/users/me/trusted-devices, DELETE /api/users/me/trusted-devices to revoke all, DELETE /api/users/me/trusted-devices/{id} to revoke one), 2 admin (GET/DELETE /api/users/{userId}/trusted-devices). Cron integration: a new `bin/cron/auth-sweep.php` script bootstraps a Connection (no actor — the sweep is pure DB maintenance with no permission decisions), instantiates SsoLoginAttemptRepository + TrustedDeviceRepository, runs expireStale with a 1-hour cutoff (pending OIDC flows that didn't complete in an hour are dead — IdP authorize flows rarely take more than a few minutes in practice; we don't go shorter to leave room for users who get distracted between clicking the SSO button and actually completing MFA on the IdP side), runs purgeExpired to hard-delete past-expiry trust rows. Registered in bin/cron/run.php under key 'auth-sweep' with schedule '30 3 * * *' (daily at 3:30 AM, after retention at 3:00 so the two don't compete on the hot tables). The 19-case test suite in `tests/SsoAndTrustedDeviceTest.php` covers SsoProviderRepository CRUD with metadata JSON round-trip and is_active filtering, SsoLoginAttemptRepository complete/fail/expireStale lifecycle, SsoUserLinkRepository syncProfile + touchLogin + delete, SsoService.startLogin builds authorize URL with all required OIDC params and persists pending attempt, startLogin rejects unknown slug with InvalidArgumentException, startLogin rejects type=saml with RuntimeException, handleCallback all three resolution paths (existing link → user resolved without creating a new link, email match → user resolved AND link auto-created, auto_provision → new user created with provider.default_role applied), handleCallback no-match-no-autoprovision rejection with RuntimeException so silent provisioning doesn't happen, sync_profile_on_login=true updates link.email+display_name on subsequent login, state-replay rejection with InvalidArgumentException because the second call finds status=completed not pending, unlink owner-only enforcement (non-owner returns false rather than throwing), TrustedDeviceService.issue→verify happy path including hash-not-raw persistence and last_used_at stamping, verify rejects wrong user / unknown token / expired row / revoked row, revoke gate fallback for non-owners (permissive gate allows; gate.denials['users.update']=true forces UnauthorizedException), revokeAllForUser bulk revocation, purgeExpired hard-delete of past-expiry rows. SQLite-gated like the rest of the Phase 10 suite — the existing pdo_sqlite-not-available environment skips cleanly. Tests use SsoStubUserRepository (in-memory implementation extending the real UserRepository, overriding find/findByEmail/create + adding seed() — needed because the real repo uses MySQL NOW() in INSERTs which SQLite doesn't accept) and SsoStubHttpClient (extends OidcHttpClient with public token/userinfo response arrays so each test sets the fixture inline) so no MySQL or network dependency. SAML support is intentionally bounded out of this PR — the type column + service-layer switch is plumbed so adding an Saml2Service that handles the redirect/POST-binding dance is a follow-up that doesn't require any caller-side changes. With this in place, four cross-cutting backlog items are now complete (Phase 0/divisions, multi-vehicle/CRM, retention+SOC, SSO+MFA), and the remaining backlog is Reporting/BI dashboards, Mobile PWA parity, and the third-party integrations stack (QuickBooks/Xero, mapping, IoT, telecom monitoring, access-control platforms).)_
- [x] Integrations: QuickBooks/Xero, mapping, IoT, telecom monitoring, access control platforms _(Cross-cutting — migration 151 lays down three idempotent tables (`third_party_integrations`, `integration_sync_logs`, `integration_webhook_events`) that together stand up a unified third-party integration framework: encrypted credential storage, scheduled sync execution, webhook receipt with deduplication, sync log audit, and a gated lifecycle API — backed by an in-code adapter registry that ships seven concrete adapters covering the five categories called out in the backlog (accounting, mapping, IoT/telematics, telecom monitoring, access control). The motivation is that prior phases produced repeated one-off integration hooks scattered across services (QuickBooks invoice export living inside InvoiceService, Mapbox tokens read via env in TrackingService, Geotab webhook handler bolted onto a controller), each with bespoke credential handling, no shared sync history, no webhook dedup, and no consistent gate against accidental cross-tenant exposure of integration credentials. This phase replaces that with a single seam: every integration is one row in `third_party_integrations` referencing one provider_key, with credentials encrypted at rest under the existing FieldCipher/libsodium key, settings stored as JSON, sync cadence in minutes, and last/next sync timestamps for the cron runner; every adapter is a class implementing `IntegrationAdapterInterface` (providerKey/displayName/category/credentialFields/settingFields/testConnection/sync/defaultCadenceMinutes/description); every webhook delivery is a row in `integration_webhook_events` deduplicated on (provider_key, sha256(provider_key||raw_body)) so an IdP retransmitting the same event 5 times produces 1 row; every sync run (manual, scheduled, or webhook-triggered) is one row in `integration_sync_logs` started→finished with records_in/records_out/duration_ms/error_message/summary captured atomically. Adding a new vendor is one new adapter class registered in the `IntegrationAdapterRegistry`; the API, cron, gate, encryption, and audit surfaces all pick it up without touching the route layer. The schema: `third_party_integrations` (id PK, provider_key VARCHAR(80) — the catalog identifier 'quickbooks_online'/'xero'/'google_maps'/'mapbox'/'generic_telematics'/'telecom_monitoring'/'access_control'; name VARCHAR(160) for display; category VARCHAR(40) — 'accounting'|'mapping'|'iot'|'telecom'|'access_control' so management UIs can group by category without parsing provider_key prefixes; status VARCHAR(20) — 'pending'|'connected'|'error'|'disabled' for the connection lifecycle (pending=registered but not yet validated, connected=last test/sync succeeded, error=last test/sync failed and last_sync_error captured the body, disabled=operator opted out without deletion); credentials TEXT holding the FieldCipher-encrypted JSON blob (libsodium secretbox, base64(nonce||ciphertext) — never inspected by the repository, only by IntegrationService.decryptCredentials at sync/test prep time so plaintext never leaves the service-layer scope); settings JSON for non-sensitive operator-configurable knobs (realm_id, base_url, vehicles_path templates, etc — not encrypted because they're not secrets and operators want to read them in management UIs); sync_cadence_minutes INT defaulting from the adapter's defaultCadenceMinutes (5 for access control to keep badge revocation timely, 15 for telematics, 30 for telecom call logs, 1440 for mapping geocoding which is daily probe only, configurable per-row so an operator can dial it down on a noisy IdP); last_sync_at/last_sync_status/last_sync_error denormalised onto the integration itself so the management UI renders "last sync: success, 2026-04-25 14:00, 47 records" without joining the logs table; next_sync_at the cron's primary scan key (NULL = never auto-sync, populated = due-when-now>=value); owner_user_id INT FK SET NULL on users so the operator who registered the integration is preserved through user deletion as audit-only data; UNIQUE on provider_key + slug-style stable identifiers prevent the same provider being registered twice). `integration_sync_logs` (id PK; integration_id INT FK CASCADE on the integration so a deleted integration sweeps its history because log rows referencing nothing are noise; triggered_by VARCHAR(20) — 'manual'|'scheduled'|'webhook' so analytics can pivot 'how many of last quarter's syncs were automated'; user_id INT FK SET NULL — the human who triggered manually, NULL for cron; direction VARCHAR(20) — 'pull'|'push'|'both' so adapters that push (e.g. invoice → QuickBooks) and pull (e.g. accounting → invoice writeback) can both surface the right semantic; status 'running'|'succeeded'|'failed' — running is transient (set at start, overwritten at finish), so a row stuck in running after a process crash is itself a forensic signal worth surfacing; records_in/records_out INT NULLs (NULL when run failed before counting; 0 when nothing matched the cursor; positive when actual work happened); duration_ms INT for performance regression detection; error_message TEXT for the exception body when failed; summary JSON for arbitrary structured payload (per-entity counts, cursor advanced-to timestamps, pagination metadata); started_at/finished_at DATETIME — deliberately separate columns so a finished_at NULL with a started_at != NULL flags the running-then-crashed case distinctly). `integration_webhook_events` (id PK; integration_id INT FK SET NULL because a webhook delivered for a since-deleted integration still merits forensic preservation; provider_key VARCHAR(80) so an unsolicited webhook with no matching integration_id row still gets recorded for review; event_type VARCHAR(120) for the IdP-supplied event name; external_id VARCHAR(120) for the IdP's stable event identifier where present; payload_hash CHAR(64) — sha256(provider_key||'|'||raw_body) — combined with provider_key in a UNIQUE INDEX so retransmits return the existing row id rather than insert a duplicate; raw_payload LONGTEXT (LONG so 10MB Square-style payloads fit); headers JSON; status 'received'|'processed'|'failed'|'duplicate'). All FKs guarded on referenced-table existence via information_schema lookups so the migration runs cleanly on partially-set-up databases. The `IntegrationAdapterInterface` is intentionally narrow: providerKey()/displayName()/category()/description() for catalog metadata; credentialFields() returns an array of [name → ['label', 'type'(string|password), 'required']] so the registration UI can render the right form per provider; settingFields() same shape for non-secret fields; testConnection(creds, settings) returns ['ok' => bool, 'message' => string, 'meta' => array] for the validate-on-create path that surfaces failure messages directly to operators; sync(creds, settings, $since DateTimeImmutable|null) returns ['records_in' => int, 'records_out' => int, 'summary' => array] — the since cursor is null on first run and last_sync_at thereafter so adapters can do incremental pulls (Xero If-Modified-Since header, QuickBooks MetaData.LastUpdatedTime > since); defaultCadenceMinutes() advises the per-row default. `AbstractIntegrationAdapter` provides a `__construct(?callable $httpClient = null)` so test harnesses inject a closure returning fixture responses without hitting the network; the constructor falls back to a curl-based default when null. Helper methods `requireCredential($creds, $key)` throws InvalidArgumentException for missing required values, `setting($settings, $key, $default)` is the safe getter, `request($method, $url, $options)` wraps the injected httpClient (or curl) with consistent timeout/header handling, `decodeJson($body)` is the shared JSON unwrap. Seven concrete adapters: `QuickBooksOnlineAdapter` — OAuth 2.0 (client_id + client_secret + refresh_token credentials; realm_id + environment(sandbox|production) settings); testConnection POSTs oauth.platform.intuit.com/oauth2/v1/tokens/bearer with the refresh_token grant to mint an access_token then GETs /v3/company/{realm}/companyinfo/{realm}; sync issues count(*) queries against the Invoice and Customer entities with WHERE MetaData.LastUpdatedTime > since and reports records_in. `XeroAdapter` — OAuth 2.0 (client_id + client_secret + refresh_token credentials; tenant_id setting); mints token via identity.xero.com/connect/token; testConnection GETs the Organisation endpoint with Authorization: Bearer + Xero-tenant-id header; sync uses If-Modified-Since to short-circuit unchanged tenants (304 → records_in=0). `GoogleMapsAdapter` — single api_key credential with no OAuth; cadence 1440 (daily probe only because the Maps API is request-not-sync — we just verify the key still works); testConnection probes maps.googleapis.com/maps/api/geocode/json with a hardcoded address and asserts ZERO_RESULTS or OK status (anything else surfaces the API error message verbatim). `MapboxAdapter` — single access_token credential plus username setting; hits api.mapbox.com/tokens/v2/{username} for validation. `GenericTelematicsAdapter` — vendor-neutral (Geotab/Samsara/Verizon/Fleetio shape) — api_key credential + base_url + vehicles_path + trips_path + auth_header settings; the auth_header is a templated string like "Bearer {api_key}" with {api_key} substituted at request time so the same adapter handles 'Bearer X' and 'Token X' and 'X-Api-Key: X' shapes by config; cadence 15min default; sync walks the configured trips_path and accepts any of the three common envelope shapes ({data: [...]}, {items: [...]}, bare-array) so it handles Geotab and Samsara off the same code path. `TelecomMonitoringAdapter` — vendor-neutral telecom (Twilio/Vonage/Bandwidth shape) — account_sid + auth_token credentials + base_url + auth_scheme(basic|bearer) + calls_path settings; cadence 30min so call/SMS log pulls don't burn quota. `AccessControlAdapter` — vendor-neutral access control (Brivo/Kisi/HID Origo shape) — api_key credential + base_url + site_id + events_path settings; events_path templates {site_id} so `/sites/{site_id}/events` resolves correctly per row; cadence 5min default because badge revocation timeliness matters for security. The seven adapters are all stateless — every call receives credentials and settings, never reads env or DB directly — so they're trivially testable and trivially poolable. The `IntegrationAdapterRegistry` holds the in-code catalog: register() rejects duplicate provider_key with RuntimeException at boot so a copy-paste bug in the registration list fails fast; has/get/listAll/listByCategory/describeAll provide the read surface; describeAll() is the catalog endpoint's source of truth, returning [provider_key, display_name, category, description, credential_fields, setting_fields, default_cadence_minutes] per registered adapter so the React shell renders the right registration form per provider. The repositories follow the COLUMNS const + WRITABLE allowlist + private hydrate pattern: `ThirdPartyIntegrationRepository` find/findByProviderKey/listAll/listByCategory/listDue (filtered by status='connected' AND next_sync_at <= now ordered by next_sync_at ASC so oldest-due fires first)/create/update/delete plus an atomic `recordSync(id, status, error, finishedAt, nextSyncAt)` four-field update that's called from IntegrationService.runSync at the very end so the integration's denormalised summary stays in sync with the latest log row. encode() JSON-encodes settings (credentials are encrypted by the service layer before reaching the repo so encode() doesn't need to touch the encryption boundary), castColumn() rehydrates settings JSON via json_decode and casts INT columns explicitly (sync_cadence_minutes, owner_user_id) so PHP's loose typing doesn't bleed string ints into business logic. `IntegrationSyncLogRepository` start (insert running row, return id) / finish (six-field update including summary JSON) split mirroring ReportExecutionRepository so the lifecycle row never goes missing on crash mid-sync. `IntegrationWebhookEventRepository` record() computes sha256(providerKey + '|' + rawBody) then short-circuits to an existing row id via findByHash before INSERT so retransmits are O(1) lookup; markProcessed/markFailed transition status terminally. `IntegrationService` is the orchestration layer with five core methods: `register(User, payload)` gates on `integrations.manage`, validates required credential fields against the adapter's credentialFields() spec (rejects with InvalidArgumentException when a required field is missing — the validate-at-edge stance, no silent default-substitution), JSON-encodes the credentials map, runs the JSON through cipher.encrypt() so the persisted blob is libsodium ciphertext, seeds next_sync_at = now() when the cadence > 0 so the cron picks up new integrations on its next tick. `update(User, id, payload)` is the partial-update path; re-encrypts only when payload contains a credentials key (so an operator updating only sync_cadence_minutes doesn't trigger a full re-encrypt + new nonce). `testConnection(User, id)` decrypts, calls adapter.testConnection(), flips integration status to 'connected' on ok=true and 'error' (with last_sync_error captured) on ok=false or thrown exception — the status change persists so the management UI renders the live health state without an extra hit. `runSync(?User, id, triggeredBy, context)` is the atomic sync orchestration: gate-asserts integrations.run when User is non-null (cron mode passes null and skips the assertion — same pattern as RetentionRunner.runAllActive); writes a sync log start row with triggered_by + direction; calls adapter.sync(creds, settings, since=last_sync_at); writes the log finish row with records_in/records_out/duration_ms/summary; calls repo.recordSync(integration_id, status, error, finishedAt, nextSyncAt) to update last_sync_at/status/error and advance next_sync_at by the cadence — and critically advances next_sync_at even on failure to prevent a broken integration from tight-looping the cron tick by tick (operator must explicitly retry or fix config to break out of the cadence-throttled retry pattern); on success unconditionally bumps integration.status to 'connected' so a previously-error integration that recovers transitions cleanly without a manual re-test. `processDueSyncs(?DateTimeImmutable now)` is the cron entry: calls repo.listDue(now), iterates every due integration and calls runSync() in cron mode, returns a results array of [integration_id, status, records_in, error] per integration so the cron script can print per-row summaries. `disconnect(User, id)` flips status=disabled and clears next_sync_at so the cron stops scanning the row but the audit history stays intact. The private `decryptCredentials` is the only place plaintext exists in memory; private `computeNextSync` does $from->modify("+{$cadence} minutes"). The `IntegrationWebhookService` exposes receive(provider, rawBody, headers) — the cheap-persist path that records the event_type and external_id from headers/body without invoking the adapter (so webhook handlers return 202 in milliseconds even when adapters are slow); processPending(limit) sweeps unprocessed rows for adapter-specific dispatch (a follow-up that wires per-provider webhook handlers slots in here without touching the route layer). The `IntegrationController` exposes 12 methods: listProviders/describeProvider (catalog endpoints — what's available to register), listIntegrations/getIntegration (registered integrations on the shop), register/update/test/sync/disconnect/delete (lifecycle), listLogs/listWebhookEvents (audit), receiveWebhook (the webhook landing endpoint outside the auth group). Critical: `toApi()` strips the credentials blob from every response and replaces it with a boolean has_credentials field — so even a leaked admin response page can't be combined with a key-leak to recover credentials, and the React shell never has a path to render the encrypted blob. The `routes/modules/integrations.php` module registers 13 routes; the webhook endpoint POST /api/integrations/webhooks/{provider} is OUTSIDE the Middleware::auth() group (returns 202 with event_id so the IdP gets a fast ACK and the dedup table absorbs retransmits), the other 12 endpoints under Middleware::auth() with service-layer permission gates. The route module instantiates and registers all seven adapters in the registry on boot so the catalog endpoint reflects the live set without a separate registration phase. Permissions added in `config/auth.php` across two roles (admin already has '*' so it's implicitly covered): manager gets integrations.view + integrations.manage (managers own the third-party integration surface for the shop — they configure the IdP credentials, run manual syncs, disconnect failing integrations, review webhook events for triage); accounting gets integrations.view (accounting consumes the QuickBooks/Xero accounting-category integrations for reconciliation reads but credential rotation stays with admins/managers because the bookkeeper rotating an OAuth refresh_token would be a privilege-escalation footgun). Other roles are intentionally excluded — technicians and dispatchers and parts have no business reading or writing third-party integration config. Cron integration: a new `bin/cron/integration-sync.php` script bootstraps a Connection + RolePermissions/AccessGate (gate is in scope but the runner passes null user to runSync so cron mode bypasses the assertion), instantiates the registry with all seven adapters, instantiates IntegrationService, calls processDueSyncs() and prints one summary line per integration (integration_id, status, records_in or error) plus a closing summary (X ok, Y failed, Z total). Exits 0 on full success and 1 if any integration failed so cron-monitor systems can alert. Registered in bin/cron/run.php under key 'integration-sync' with schedule '*/5 * * * *' (every 5 minutes — integrations with cadence < 5min still resolve correctly because the runner walks all due rows in a single tick, but the global tick is rate-limited to once per 5min so a misbehaving IdP can't burn quota unbounded). Test coverage in `tests/IntegrationsTest.php` follows the established SQLite + sodium-gated pattern (skips cleanly when pdo_sqlite or sodium is unavailable, as in this environment) and covers 23+ distinct cases: ThirdPartyIntegrationRepository CRUD round-trip with settings JSON serialisation through encode/cast pipeline, repo.findByProviderKey returns the right row, repo.listByCategory scopes correctly across multiple categories, repo.listDue scoping in the four-row scenario (connected+due fires, connected+future doesn't, disabled+due doesn't, connected+null next_sync_at doesn't), IntegrationSyncLogRepository start→finish writes status/records_in/records_out/duration_ms correctly, IntegrationWebhookEventRepository record dedup (second call with same provider_key+raw_body returns the existing row id, no duplicate insert), webhook markProcessed transitions status terminally, IntegrationAdapterRegistry register rejects duplicates with RuntimeException, registry.describeAll produces the contract-shaped metadata for the catalog API, IntegrationService.register rejects unknown provider with InvalidArgumentException, register rejects missing required credential field, register denied by gate when integrations.manage absent, register encrypts credentials at rest (test asserts the literal credential value 'super-secret' does NOT appear in the stored blob and that decrypting via FieldCipher round-trips to the original JSON), testConnection success flips status='connected', testConnection failure flips status='error' and captures last_sync_error, runSync happy path increments records_in and writes a succeeded log row, runSync failure path captures the exception in error_message AND advances next_sync_at by cadence so the integration doesn't tight-loop, runSync second run passes since=last_sync_at to the adapter (verifying the incremental cursor), processDueSyncs scoping (only connected+due rows are processed), disconnect flips status=disabled and clears next_sync_at, IntegrationWebhookService dedup (second receive of same body returns same event_id), processPending sweeps the right rows, AbstractIntegrationAdapter HTTP callable injection (uses MapboxAdapter with an injected closure to verify URL formation and Bearer header without hitting the network), sweep over all seven built-in adapters asserting providerKey/displayName/category/credentialFields/settingFields/defaultCadenceMinutes are non-empty and the contract holds. Tests use `IntegrationsInMemoryConnection` (SQLite in-memory variant of Connection), `IntegrationsPermissiveGate` (extends AccessGate, allows everything by default with a denials map for selective rejection), and `FakeIntegrationAdapter` (extends AbstractIntegrationAdapter, captures syncCalls/testCalls for inspection, has shouldFailTest/shouldThrowSync flags for failure-path tests) so no MySQL or network dependency. Lint-clean across all 21 new PHP files (3 models, 3 repositories, 1 interface + 1 abstract adapter + 1 registry + 7 concrete adapters in src/Services/Integrations/ThirdParty, 1 IntegrationService, 1 IntegrationWebhookService, 1 IntegrationController, 1 route module, 1 cron script), test file syntax-clean and skip-gated. The migration is incremental on top of 150 (Reporting/BI) with no schema dependencies beyond the pre-existing users table. With this in place, six cross-cutting backlog items are now complete (Phase 0/divisions, multi-vehicle/CRM, retention+SOC, SSO+MFA, Reporting/BI, third-party integrations) and the only remaining backlog is Mobile PWA parity — every other Phase 10 cross-cutting workstream is shipped.)_
- [x] Retention policies + SOC logging _(Cross-cutting — migration 148 introduces three idempotent tables (`data_retention_policies`, `data_retention_runs`, `security_events`) that together replace the hardcoded retention behavior in `bin/cron/data-cleanup.php` with a configurable, per-entity policy engine AND establish a SOC-style security event log distinct from the generic `audit_logs` surface. The motivation for two separate logs is that they serve different consumers with different access patterns: `audit_logs` (already in 003_audit_logs.sql) records every domain mutation across every business entity for traceability — high write volume, mostly informational, queried by entity-centric reads ("what happened to invoice 412?"); `security_events` records SOC-relevant activity only — auth success/failure, lockouts, privilege escalations, admin actions, suspicious requests — lower write volume but each row is something an analyst should be able to triage, queried by severity/actor/event_type pivots that would otherwise compete with audit reads on the same hot index pages. Keeping the two surfaces independent lets each be indexed and retained separately. The `data_retention_policies` table holds one row per entity type (UNIQUE on entity_type so misconfiguration like "two windows on audit_logs" is caught at insert) carrying entity_type, table_name, timestamp_column (defaulting 'created_at' but configurable for tables that use a different lifecycle column), retention_days INT, action VARCHAR(20) ('delete' or 'archive'), archive_table_name VARCHAR(80) NULL (only required for archive action), is_active TINYINT for opt-out without deletion, last_run_at/last_run_status/last_run_records denormalised onto the policy itself for the management UI to render "last run: success, 1,247 rows, 2026-04-25 03:00" without joining the runs history, and notes TEXT for free-form documentation per policy. The `data_retention_runs` table is the run history — one row per execution, with policy_id INT FK CASCADE (a deleted policy sweeps its history because run rows are meaningless without their policy context), started_at/completed_at DATETIME, status VARCHAR(20) (running/success/failed/skipped/dry_run), records_examined and records_affected INT NULLs (NULL when the run failed before counting; 0 when nothing matched the cutoff; positive when actual work was done), dry_run TINYINT, error_message TEXT for the exception body when status=failed, and triggered_by_user_id INT NULL FK SET NULL pointing at the operator who manually ran a policy from the admin UI (NULL for cron-triggered runs since cron has no logged-in user). The `security_events` table uses BIGINT id (volume can dwarf audit_logs in active SOC scenarios — failed-login storms can produce thousands of rows in a single attack window), event_type VARCHAR(80), severity VARCHAR(20) (info/warning/critical with the runtime defaulting unknown values to info), actor_user_id and target_user_id INT NULL both FK SET NULL on the users table because user deletion shouldn't break the security log — anonymising the actor preserves the event for incident review, and a "who did this to whom" model lets us record both sides ("admin A demoted user B" — A in actor, B in target), ip_address VARCHAR(45) sized for IPv6 (existing AuthService already collects IPv6-shaped client IPs through the new IpAddressResolver), user_agent VARCHAR(500), request_path VARCHAR(500), and context JSON for arbitrary structured payloads (the logger scrubs sensitive keys like password/token/secret before persistence). Indexes are tuned for the three primary query patterns: idx_se_event for "all events of type X" (the SOC dashboard's event-type breakdown), idx_se_actor for "what did user N do recently" (the primary forensic query), idx_se_severity for "critical events first" (the SOC inbox view), and idx_se_created for time-window queries ("show me events in the last hour" — the live SOC view that ticks every minute). All four FKs (drr→drp, drr→users, se→users actor, se→users target) are guarded on referenced-table existence via information_schema lookups so the migration runs cleanly on partially-set-up databases. The migration ends with INSERT IGNORE seeds for five default policies that mirror the old hardcoded behavior in `bin/cron/data-cleanup.php`: audit_logs at 730 days (2 years matches SOC compliance windows), notification_logs at 90 days (delivery dispute window), reminder_logs at 90 days (matches notifications), security_events_info at 365 days (1-year sliding window for info-severity SOC events; the runner is intentionally configured to leave warning/critical rows alone so they live forever absent a separate policy targeting them — a deliberate "never accidentally delete a critical incident" stance), and payment_sessions at 1 day (replacing the existing hardcoded 24-hour DELETE). Models follow the established BaseModel pattern: `DataRetentionPolicy` ships ACTIONS = ['delete', 'archive'] and RUN_STATUSES = ['success', 'failed', 'skipped', 'dry_run']; `DataRetentionRun` ships STATUSES = ['running', 'success', 'failed', 'skipped', 'dry_run'] (note 'running' is a transient state set at start() and never persisted as a final state — complete() always overwrites with one of the terminal four); `SecurityEvent` ships SEVERITIES + a comprehensive set of EVENT_* constants covering the standard SOC vocabulary (LOGIN_SUCCESS, LOGIN_FAILURE, LOGOUT, PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED/COMPLETED, PASSWORD_CHANGED, TWO_FACTOR_ENABLED/DISABLED/FAILED, ACCOUNT_LOCKED/UNLOCKED, PRIVILEGE_GRANTED/REVOKED, ROLE_CHANGED, ADMIN_ACTION, RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED, SUSPICIOUS_REQUEST, API_KEY_CREATED/REVOKED, DATA_EXPORT, DATA_RETENTION_RUN). The constants are intentionally not enforced as a closed set at the logger level — callers can record domain-specific event types without first registering them — but using them keeps the common ones consistent across writers and dashboards. The repositories `RetentionPolicyRepository` and `RetentionRunRepository` follow the COLUMNS const + WRITABLE allowlist + private hydrate pattern; the policy repo offers find/findByEntity/listAll/listActive for the runner and the management UI, plus `recordRunSummary()` which is called by the runner after each policy run to update last_run_at/status/records on the policy row itself. The runs repo offers start (creates a row with status=running), complete (transitions to one of the four terminal statuses with examined/affected/error fields), listForPolicy (per-policy run history for the detail view) and listRecent (cross-policy timeline for the SOC dashboard's "retention activity" widget). `SecurityEventRepository` offers create/find plus listFiltered and countFiltered keyed on event_type/severity/actor_user_id/target_user_id/since (a Y-m-d H:i:s lower bound) for the SOC dashboard's filterable event grid, plus aggregateBySeverity for the headline "by severity" pie chart. The retention runner `RetentionRunner` is the workflow orchestrator: gated on `retention.run` permission when called with an actor, but accepts null actor for cron mode (the cron script bypasses the gate because cron has filesystem-level access already and there's no logged-in user to assert against). `runById()` looks up a single policy and runs it; `runAllActive()` iterates every active policy and runs each in isolation (per-policy try/catch so one broken policy doesn't break the whole cron run). The core `runPolicy()` method: starts a runs row with status=running, checks tableExists() against the source table (driver-aware: queries information_schema on MySQL, sqlite_master on SQLite — both kept supported because the SQLite-gated test harness needs the same code path), finalises with status=skipped if the table is missing (a deliberate "missing table is not an error" stance because the seeded defaults reference tables that may legitimately not exist on partially-set-up databases — better to skip than crash the cron loop), computes the cutoff timestamp by subtracting retention_days from now, counts candidates with a parameterised SELECT COUNT(*), and short-circuits with status=dry_run if requested or status=success+0/0 if examined=0. Otherwise it dispatches on action: delete issues a parameterised DELETE WHERE timestamp_column < cutoff and reports rowCount; archive runs the copy-then-delete dance inside a PDO transaction (joined to any outer transaction via inTransaction() guard) — INSERT INTO archive SELECT * FROM source WHERE timestamp < cutoff, then DELETE FROM source WHERE timestamp < cutoff, with rollback on any exception so we never end up with a half-archived state. Identifier safety is handled by `quoteIdent()` which validates the identifier against `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$` (rejects anything containing whitespace, semicolons, quotes, or other shell-style injection vectors) and quotes with backticks for MySQL or double-quotes for SQLite — if a malformed table_name is configured by an admin (typo, fat-fingered space, paste of an SQL statement), the validation fails and the runner records the error to the run's error_message rather than executing dangerous SQL. Combined with the parameterised cutoff binding, this gives end-to-end SQL safety even though the table/column names by their nature can't be parameter-bound. The finalize() helper is called from every terminal branch (success/skipped/dry_run/failed) and does two things: completes the run row with the final status, examined, affected, and error fields; and updates the policy row's denormalised last_run_at/status/records summary. Failures from inside runPolicy() are caught, finalised with status=failed + error_message, and re-thrown so the calling code (controller or cron) can surface the exception — but runAllActive() catches per-policy failures and continues, since cron mode wants to persist forward through transient errors. The controllers `RetentionController` and `SecurityEventController` return the standard `{data: ...}` envelope. RetentionController offers listPolicies/getPolicy (with bundled recent_runs)/createPolicy/updatePolicy/deletePolicy/runPolicy/runAll/listRuns; createPolicy/updatePolicy validate the payload (action ∈ ACTIONS; retention_days ≥ 0; archive action requires archive_table_name; entity_type and table_name required on create); each gated on the appropriate permission (.view for reads, .manage for writes, .run delegated to the runner which asserts itself). SecurityEventController offers index (filtered list with total/limit/offset for pagination)/show/summary (severity aggregate)/record (manual SOC entry from admin tools); record is gated on `security_events.manage` while the read paths use `security_events.view` so we can distinguish "admin recorded a triage note" from automated logger writes. The route modules `routes/modules/retention.php` and `routes/modules/security_events.php` are registered in the routes/api.php loader array. Retention exposes 8 endpoints under Middleware::auth(): GET /api/retention/policies (list), GET /api/retention/policies/{id} (detail with recent runs), POST /api/retention/policies (create), PUT /api/retention/policies/{id} (update), DELETE /api/retention/policies/{id} (delete), POST /api/retention/policies/{id}/run (run one, optional dry_run flag in body), POST /api/retention/run-all (run every active policy), GET /api/retention/runs (cross-policy run history). Security exposes 4 endpoints: GET /api/security-events (filtered list), GET /api/security-events/summary (severity aggregate), GET /api/security-events/{id} (detail), POST /api/security-events (manual record). Permissions added in `config/auth.php` across three roles (admin already has '*' so it's implicitly covered): manager gets full read/write/run on retention plus read+manage on security_events (managers own the SOC log + retention policy surface for the shop, can edit policies, run them ad-hoc with dry-run, and record manual SOC entries for incident triage); dispatcher gets security_events.view only (dispatchers triage security events when an on-shift user reports trouble — read-only because manage stays with managers and admins); accounting gets retention.view + security_events.view (accounting reads retention policies and SOC events for compliance audits — SOX/SOC2 trails reference exactly which retention windows are in force at audit time, and accounting also correlates security events against transaction audits during reconciliation). Cron integration: a new `bin/cron/retention-runner.php` script bootstraps a Connection + RolePermissions/AccessGate (passed null actor inside, so the gate is bypassed in cron mode while still being available to the runner constructor for code-path consistency), instantiates the runner, and calls `runAllActive(null, $dryRun)` honoring an optional `--dry-run` flag. The script prints a per-policy summary line (policy_id, status, examined, affected, error) and a closing summary (X ok, Y failed, Z records affected), exits 0 on full success and 1 if any policy failed so cron-monitor systems can alert. The new job is registered in `bin/cron/run.php` under key 'retention-runner' with schedule '0 3 * * *' — daily at 3 AM, an hour after data-cleanup at 2 AM, so the two don't compete on the same hot tables. The existing data-cleanup script is intentionally left in place rather than ripped out: it still handles non-database cleanups (rate-limit files via RateLimiter::cleanup, expired password reset tokens, expired email verification tokens) that don't fit the per-table retention model, and the retention runner now owns the database-side cleanup. As policies prove out in production, individual SQL deletes can be migrated from data-cleanup.php into the policies table one at a time. SecurityEventLogger is the runtime entry point for writing security events: it takes an event_type string, a severity (defaulting to info, with invalid values silently coerced back to info), and an options bag accepting actor (User), target_user_id (int), ip_address/user_agent/request_path strings, context array, and request (Request — auto-fills ip/ua/path from `getClientIp()`/header('User-Agent')/path()). The scrub() helper recursively walks the context array and replaces any value at a key matching the redact list (password, password_confirmation, token, totp_code, secret) with '[REDACTED]' before persistence — the redact set is intentionally smaller than AuditLogger's because security events shouldn't be storing arbitrary user payloads in the first place; if domain code finds itself wanting to log a password into a security event, the redaction is the safety net that catches the mistake. Test coverage in `tests/RetentionAndSecurityEventsTest.php` follows the established SQLite-gated pattern (skips cleanly when pdo_sqlite is unavailable, as in this environment) and covers 24 distinct cases: model constants (action/severity/event-type sets), policy repo CRUD with active filter and update toggling, run repo start+complete lifecycle, runner deletes rows older than cutoff while leaving fresh rows alone (verifies records_examined and records_affected match physical deletions and last_run_at gets stamped on the policy), runner dry-run counts examined but doesn't delete (verifies row count unchanged after dry run), runner zero-affected when no rows match the cutoff, runner skipped status when source table is missing (the safety net for partially-set-up databases), runner archive path copies into archive table then deletes from source (verifies live count drops and archive count rises by the exact same number), runner archive without archive_table_name finalises the run row with status=failed + error_message AND re-throws for callers to surface, runner identifier-validation rejects malformed table names by short-circuiting through tableExists() (the bad identifier never reaches DELETE because the parameterised information_schema lookup harmlessly returns 0), runner runAllActive iterates only active policies and calls them once each, runner gate denies retention.run when actor is non-null, runner null-actor cron mode bypasses the gate, security event logger writes events with actor + scrubs sensitive context keys (password and token both come back as '[REDACTED]' while non-sensitive context survives), logger coerces invalid severity back to info, security event repo filters/counts/aggregates correctly across multiple seeded events, retention controller list+show envelope shape, retention controller validation of archive payload (missing archive_table_name throws InvalidArgumentException), retention controller gate denies retention.manage on creates, security controller index envelope with total field, security controller record requires manage permission, security controller record creates an event with the actor stamped on actor_user_id. Lint-clean across all 13 new PHP files (3 models, 5 services across Retention/Security namespaces, 2 controllers, 2 route modules, 1 cron script), autoload verified through reflection (every new class loads under the existing PSR-4 mapping with no composer dump-autoload required), test file syntax-clean and skip-gated. The migration is incremental on top of 147 (Phase 10.8) with no schema dependencies beyond the pre-existing users table. With this in place every cross-cutting backlog item except Reporting/BI dashboards, Mobile PWA parity, SSO+MFA hardening, and the third-party integrations stack is complete; SOC compliance and configurable retention are now first-class surfaces alongside the rest of Phase 10's operational tooling.)_

---

## Current Status

**Phases 0–9 are complete.** Phase 10 (smart additions & polish) is the next active phase. Every cross-cutting workstream is shipped (Phase 0/divisions, multi-vehicle/CRM, retention+SOC, SSO+MFA, Reporting/BI, third-party integrations, Mobile PWA parity).

Per-phase rollup:
- Phase 0 — Foundation ✅
- Phase 1 — CRM expansion ✅
- Phase 2 — Installed Assets ✅ (2.1–2.5)
- Phase 3 — Support Desk / Tickets ✅ (3.1–3.5)
- Phase 4 — Contracts & Service Agreements ✅
- Phase 5 — Preventative Maintenance ✅
- Phase 6 — Customer Portal ✅
- Phase 7 — Fleet Maintenance ✅
- Phase 8 — FIT Inspections compliance layer ✅ (8.1–8.5)
- Phase 9 — Capital Planner ✅ (9.1–9.4)
- Phase 10 — Smart additions & polish ⏳
